## ADR-0022: Split phase 05 into sub-phases 05.1, 05.2, and 05.3

- Date: 2026-05-01
- Status: accepted
- Context: Phase 05 ("HTTP/2 downstream + upstream (low-level framer, own conn mgr)") was projected as a single phase by `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §8 row 05. The parent-05 state-1 brainstorm (commit `cd1a70e`, parent SPEC at `docs/envoy-rust/phases/05-http2/SPEC.md`) resolved a cascade of scope decisions that, taken together, push the phase past the §6.1 split-gate (~25 tasks / ~1500 LoC): (a) the cross-phase Docker-gated `host.docker.internal`/`STATIC` regression originating in phase-02.2's ADR-0015 landing (`435c6fa`) and surfaced by phase-04.3's CI push cadence (carryforward C-1 in the 04.3 REVIEW landed at `eb030d1`) requires `ClusterType::StrictDns` schema growth + a coordinated 5-fixture YAML edit (~400 LoC); (b) downstream H2C HCM via the `h2 = "0.4"` codec (D-3.2 permitted-foundation) + `envoy-http2` foundation crate (sole-dep-owner of `h2`, mirroring `envoy-http1`/`httparse` and `envoy-tls`/`rustls`) + listener-side `Http2ProtocolOptions` + fixture `0009-http2-direct-response` + first-conformance-suite attach `tests/conformance/h2spec/` at the ≥95% pass gate (~1300 LoC); (c) upstream H2C origination via `envoy-http2::Client` + cluster-side `Http2ProtocolOptions` via `typed_extension_protocol_options` + router H2-arm extending the 04.3-landed `RouteAction::Route` arm + new helper crate `tests/helpers/http2-echo-server` + fixture `0010-http2-router-upstream` (~1300 LoC). Combined scope across all three layers totals ~3000 LoC for a single-sub-phase 05.α (everything-in-one), exceeding §6.1 gates by ~100% in LoC and ~30% in task count.
- Options considered: (i) **single phase** — rejected, exceeds §6.1 gates by ~100% in LoC and ~30% in task count; (ii) **two-way split** (05.α = preamble + downstream H2 + h2spec; 05.β = upstream H2 + parent close) — rejected, 05.α at ~1700 LoC sits at the upper edge of the §6.1 gate with ~10% headroom; phase-04's experience showed brainstorm-time LoC estimates can drift by ~20% during execution (phase-04.3 estimated ~1490 LoC and landed ~1900 LoC of net Rust delta per the 04.3 REVIEW §1), so the headroom is insufficient against likely drift; bundling C-1 with downstream H2 also muddles the audit trail for whichever future phase audits the cross-phase fixture-hardening close-out; (iii) **two-way split with deferred h2spec** (05.α = preamble + downstream H2 only; 05.β = h2spec + upstream H2 + parent close) — rejected, scatters the h2spec attach across the same sub-phase that ships upstream H2 + helper + fixture 0010, recreating the gate-pressure that motivated the split; also h2spec attach is naturally co-located with downstream-listener work (h2spec drives a server endpoint), not upstream work; (iv) **nested split of 05.α** (05 → 05.α → 05.α.1 / 05.α.2; 05 → 05.β) — rejected, three-level numbering is awkward (`05.α.1`); BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md §6.1 explicitly flags nested splits of an already-split sub-phase as suspicious and prescribes `superpowers:systematic-debugging` first; (v) **3-way flat split** by surface boundary — preamble → downstream → upstream — chosen.
- Decision: split phase 05 into three sub-phases by surface boundary, not by traffic direction:
  - **05.1 (`05.1-fixture-hardening`)** — `ClusterType::StrictDns` schema variant + `Cluster::new` extension for DNS resolution at cluster-build time (`tokio::net::lookup_host`) + coordinated 5-fixture YAML edit flipping `type: STATIC` → `type: STRICT_DNS` on `tests/fixtures/{0003,0004,0005,0006,0008}/{envoy.yaml,envoy-rust.yaml}` + closes phase-02.1 REVIEW I3 (positive `Static` regression guard; enabled by introducing the second `ClusterType` variant) + closes phase-04.3 REVIEW C-1 (cross-phase Docker-gated regression). **NO H2 work in 05.1.** **ADR-0023** (`ClusterType::StrictDns` accepted; `LOGICAL_DNS` deferred) lands at 05.1 Task 1 inline. ~400 LoC, ~5 tasks.
  - **05.2 (`05.2-http2-downstream`)** — new library crate `crates/envoy-http2/` (sole workspace dep on `h2 = "0.4"`; mirrors `envoy-http1`'s sole-owner-of-`httparse` posture established in 04.1) + `CodecType::HTTP2` accept path + listener-side `Http2ProtocolOptions` schema (4 fields; `max_concurrent_streams`, `initial_stream_window_size`, `initial_connection_window_size`, `max_frame_size`) + HCM-on-H2 dispatch (reuses 04.x's `HCMConfig` + route-walk + router invocation site end-to-end; only the codec layer at the connection edge changes) + fixture `0009-http2-direct-response` (H2C → `direct_response` route action) + `tests/conformance/h2spec/` runner crate at the **≥95% pass** gate with catalogued failures in `known-failures.txt` + harness `Driver::Http2` + `drive_http2`. **ADR-0024** (`http` crate typed-surface scoping) and **ADR-0025** (`h2spec` integration posture) are CONDITIONAL — the planner decides at 05.2 Task 1 whether either warrants landing. ~1300 LoC, ~14 tasks.
  - **05.3 (`05.3-http2-upstream`)** — `envoy-http2::Client` (per-connection plaintext H2 client; one TCP connection per upstream call; no pooling — pooling defers to upstream-robustness family) + cluster-side `Http2ProtocolOptions` via `typed_extension_protocol_options.HttpProtocolOptions` + new `Cluster.upstream_protocol: UpstreamProtocol { Http1, Http2 }` field (default `Http1` for backwards-compat) + router H2-arm extending the 04.3-landed `RouteAction::Route` arm at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs` (dispatches H1-or-H2 based on the cluster's upstream protocol; reuses `write_proxied_response` unchanged since the response wire-format on the downstream is HCM-on-downstream's concern) + new helper crate `tests/helpers/http2-echo-server/` (sibling of `tcp-echo-server`/`tls-echo-server`/`http1-echo-server`) + fixture `0010-http2-router-upstream` (H2C downstream → router → H2C upstream cluster) + harness `Http2EchoBackend` + parent-phase-05 close. The 05.3 state-6 phase-done commit ALSO flips parent ROADMAP row `05` from `in-progress` to `done` per the ROADMAP-schema invariant ("the parent flips to `done` only after all sub-phases are `done`"). Mirrors phase 04's `e626862`-shape close-out where the 04.3 commit also closed parent 04. ~1300 LoC, ~14 tasks.
- Rationale: the 3-way flat split mirrors phase-04's `1d9740d`-shape precedent (under ADR-0020) and is the second 3-way split in the project (phases 02 and 03 used 2-way splits). Each of the three sub-phases fits comfortably under the §6.1 gates with ~13% headroom against likely execution-time drift (per phase-04.3's ~+20% drift experience). Failure attribution localizes per sub-phase: 05.1 produces a clean REVIEW.md as an audit artifact for the cross-phase C-1 close-out; 05.2's REVIEW.md owns the h2spec ≥95% gate verdict + the codec-foundation crate; 05.3's REVIEW.md owns the upstream-direction surface + the parent-05 close. The split boundary is by surface (preamble → downstream → upstream), not strictly by codec layer; this groups the fixture-hardening work coherently with the cross-phase regression-fix scope rather than scattering it into a downstream-H2 sub-phase. The C-1 fixture-hardening preamble is a coherent, externally-caused dependency that the H2 work materially benefits from completing first (05.2's fixture 0009 dispatch through the differential harness depends on 05.1's restored Docker-gated baseline, and 05.3's fixture 0010 reuses the `STRICT_DNS` cluster-type for `host.docker.internal`).
- Consequences: parent SPEC at `docs/envoy-rust/phases/05-http2/SPEC.md` was committed at parent-05 state-1 (commit `cd1a70e`, the predecessor of this commit) projecting this split; sub-phase SPECs (`05.1-fixture-hardening/SPEC.md`, `05.2-http2-downstream/SPEC.md`, `05.3-http2-upstream/SPEC.md`) land alongside this ADR in this same parent-05 state-2 commit. ROADMAP gains 3 new rows (`05.1`, `05.2`, `05.3`) at this commit; row `05`'s `sub-phases` column was already populated as `05.1, 05.2, 05.3` at the state-1 commit. Sub-phases ship strictly in order (05.1 → 05.2 → 05.3) — they cannot be parallelized because (a) 05.2's fixture 0009 dispatch through the differential harness depends on 05.1's restored Docker-gated baseline (the harness's `cluster_mgr` build path uses the schema 05.1 grows), and (b) 05.3 extends both the schema (`Http2ProtocolOptions` cluster-side, `Cluster.upstream_protocol` field) and the runtime (router H2-arm in HCM dispatch) introduced in 05.2. Parent ROADMAP row `05` flips `done` at sub-phase 05.3's state-6 phase-done commit, mirroring phase 04's `e626862`-shape close-out (which mirrored phase 03's `ca81226`-shape close-out). Phase 05's projected ADR ledger after this commit: ADR-0022 (this ADR; landed at this commit), ADR-0023 (`StrictDns`; lands at 05.1 Task 1), conditional ADR-0024 (`http` crate scoping; lands at 05.2 Task 1 if the planner determines it warrants policy-grade documentation), conditional ADR-0025 (`h2spec` integration posture; lands at 05.2 Task 1 if the gate-mechanics warrant policy-grade documentation). If ADR-0024 and/or ADR-0025 do not land, those numbers stay available for phase-06+ ADRs.
- Provenance: this ADR was projected as the next-sequential available ADR number in parent-phase-05 SPEC §7 (`docs/envoy-rust/phases/05-http2/SPEC.md`, committed at SHA `cd1a70e`). Like phase-04's split (ADR-0020), phase-05's split lands cleanly at ADR-0022 with no renumbering needed (ADR-0021 was the latest ADR before this commit; no inter-ADR landings have occurred between phase-04.2 Task 1 at `984aedd` and this commit, since phase-04.3 landed no ADRs per its SPEC §7 disposition). Unlike phase-03's split (per ADR-0017's provenance footer) which renumbered three projected ADRs, phase-05's projected ADR-0022 / ADR-0023 numbers match the actual landed numbers; ADR-0024 / ADR-0025 are CONDITIONAL and may not land — if 05.2 Task 1 ratifies neither doctrine call, those numbers stay available for later phases.

## ADR-0023: `ClusterType::StrictDns` accepted; `LOGICAL_DNS` deferred

- Date: 2026-05-01
- Status: accepted
- Context: Phase 05.1 is the fixture-hardening preamble for parent phase 05 (HTTP/2 cleartext data plane). The cross-phase Docker-gated `host.docker.internal`/`type: STATIC` regression — originating at phase-02.2's ADR-0015 landing (commit `435c6fa`) and discovered at phase-04.3 task 14 (commit `eb6f972`) per the C-1 trace in parent-05 SPEC §1 — must be closed before any new H2 surfaces are layered on top of the 5 affected fixtures (0003/0004/0005/0006/0008). Upstream Envoy v1.33.0's `socket_address.address` parse semantics expect either a literal IP (under `type: STATIC`) or DNS resolution opt-in (under `type: STRICT_DNS` or `type: LOGICAL_DNS`); envoy-rust's parser currently accepts only `STATIC` (single-variant `ClusterType { Static }` enum at `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs:60-62`). Phase-02.1 REVIEW I3 (positive `ClusterType::Static` variant-name regression guard) has been deferred since phase-02.1 close because the single-variant enum had no second variant against which to discriminate `Static` structurally; adding a second variant unblocks I3 mechanically.
- Options considered: (i) **Add only `StrictDns`.** Resolves DNS at cluster-build time; results cached for the cluster's lifetime. Sufficient for the C-1 fix because `host.docker.internal` resolves to a single Docker-host-gateway address that doesn't change during the fixture run. **Chosen.** (ii) Add both `StrictDns` and `LogicalDns`. Mirrors Envoy's full proto more completely. Rejected: `LogicalDns`'s per-request re-resolution semantics require a non-trivial runtime extension (the cluster must drop the cached resolution after the resolved addresses are picked once, vs. round-robining over the cached set indefinitely under `StrictDns`); no 05.1 fixture exercises this distinction; D-3.6's "every phase is a green build" + the §6.1 split-gate reward minimal forward landings. (iii) Add `StrictDns` + a configurable `dns_refresh_rate` knob to enable periodic re-resolution. Rejected: same as (ii); no 05.1 fixture needs it; defers to a later phase per parent-05 SPEC §4. (iv) Defer the entire `STRICT_DNS` extension; fix C-1 by replacing `host.docker.internal` with a literal IP across the 5 fixtures. Rejected: would require either (a) testcontainers-side IP discovery at fixture-render time (the host-gateway IP varies across Docker setups), which is brittle, or (b) baking a static IP into the YAMLs, which is platform-specific. ADR-0015's `host.docker.internal` posture is the right cross-platform choice; the right fix is to make envoy-rust accept the DNS-name shape, not to abandon it. (v) Use a different DNS resolver (e.g., `trust-dns-resolver` / `hickory-resolver`) instead of `tokio::net::lookup_host`. Rejected: `tokio::net::lookup_host` is part of the existing `tokio` permitted foundation per D-3.2 (no new dep needed; no new ADR scope-extension required). A third-party resolver crate is not on D-3.2's permitted list and would require its own permitted-foundations-extension ADR, which 05.1 does not need.
- Decision: Extend `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs::ClusterType` from single-variant `Static` to `Static | StrictDns`. Validator accepts the `STRICT_DNS` serde tag (`#[serde(rename_all = "SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE")]` is already on the enum, so the tag mechanically maps to the new variant); runtime resolution lives in `crates/envoy-cluster/src/cluster.rs::from_bootstrap` (the cluster-manager constructor) via `tokio::net::lookup_host(format!("{}:{}", address, port)).await`. Resolution failures surface as a new `ClusterError::DnsResolutionFailed { cluster: String, address: String, source: std::io::Error }` variant on the existing `crates/envoy-cluster/src/cluster.rs::ClusterError` enum (planner-time refinement of SPEC §3 D1's projected `ConfigError::ClusterDnsResolutionFailed` placement — the existing `from_bootstrap` returns `ClusterError`, signpost 14 confirms envoy-cluster's typed-error chain stays unchanged, and adding the variant to ClusterError is mechanically simpler than introducing a cross-crate `From<ClusterError> for ConfigError` wrapper; see PLAN.md signpost A for the full reasoning). The `LOGICAL_DNS` variant is **NOT** added in 05.1; a future phase that needs per-request DNS re-resolution lands `LogicalDns` then. The existing `Static` variant's parse + runtime paths are unchanged (regression-guarded by the new positive `static_cluster_constructs_with_literal_ip` test in 05.1 D2, which closes phase-02.1 REVIEW I3).
- Rationale: `STRICT_DNS` is the simpler, more common case and is mechanically sufficient for the C-1 fix (`host.docker.internal` resolves locally via Docker's `host-gateway` mechanism per ADR-0015, and the resolved address doesn't change during the fixture run, so per-request re-resolution offers no value). `tokio::net::lookup_host` is the chosen resolver primitive because it's part of the existing `tokio` foundation under D-3.2 and requires no new permitted-foundations grant. Deferring `LOGICAL_DNS` follows D-3.6's minimalism principle ("every phase is a green build" — narrow scope = clean acceptance gate). Adding the second `ClusterType` variant unblocks the multi-phase phase-02.1 REVIEW I3 carryforward at zero additional cost (the I3 close-out test is one of D2's 3 unit tests).
- Consequences: `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs::ClusterType` gains the `StrictDns` variant (~5 LoC including doc comment). `crates/envoy-cluster/src/cluster.rs::ClusterError` gains the `DnsResolutionFailed { cluster, address, source }` variant (~7 LoC). `crates/envoy-cluster/src/cluster.rs::from_bootstrap` is promoted to `async fn` and gains a `STRICT_DNS` resolution branch via `tokio::net::lookup_host(..).await` (~50 LoC including the per-cluster resolution loop, the zero-result defensive branch, and the existing-test `.await` updates). `crates/envoy-cluster/Cargo.toml` gains a direct `tokio = { version = "1", features = ["net", "rt", "macros"] }` dep (was previously absent; `tokio` is already a top-level dep on other workspace crates so the workspace's transitive graph is unchanged). `crates/envoy-bin/src/main.rs:83` adds `.await` to the `from_bootstrap` call (one token). `tests/fixtures/{0003,0004,0005,0006,0008}/{envoy.yaml,envoy-rust.yaml}` flip `type: STATIC` → `type: STRICT_DNS` (~30 LoC YAML diff total across 10 files). `crates/envoy-config/fuzz/corpus/parse_bootstrap/strict_dns_cluster.yaml` is added (1 new seed). **Phase-02.1 REVIEW I3 closes** at this commit (the positive `Static` regression guard `static_cluster_constructs_with_literal_ip` is one of D2's 3 unit tests). **Phase-04.3 REVIEW C-1 closes** at the 05.1 state-4 phase-done verification commit (the 5 affected Docker-gated fixtures pass simultaneously). **Phase-04.1 REVIEW M-claim** (drive_http1 per-function unit test) is unblocked but stays deferred per the 04.3 disposition; carryforward chain continues. `cargo deny check` remains clean: no new top-level Cargo deps; the `tokio` `net` feature is already activated in the workspace's resolved feature set (verified at PLAN-write time via `grep '"net"' crates/*/Cargo.toml`). Cargo.lock sync at state-4 is expected to be a no-op or near-no-op. Future phases that need `LogicalDns` (per-request DNS re-resolution), `dns_refresh_rate` (periodic re-resolution under `StrictDns`), `dns_lookup_family` (A/AAAA selection control), `respect_dns_ttl` (TTL-driven re-resolution), or `dns_resolvers` (custom resolver pool) extend then; this ADR's narrow scope is deliberate.
- Provenance: this ADR was projected as the next-sequential available ADR number in parent-05 SPEC §7 (`docs/envoy-rust/phases/05-http2/SPEC.md`, committed at parent-05 state-1 SHA `cd1a70e`); ADR-0022 (parent-05 split decision) lands at parent-05 state-2 alongside the three sub-phase SPECs (mirrors phase-04 state-2 commit `1d9740d`); ADR-0023 lands at this commit (05.1 Task 1). The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is ADR-0022; ADR-0023 lands at the next-sequential number with no renumbering needed (no inter-ADR landings between parent-05 state-2 and this commit). Closes phase-02.1 REVIEW I3 (positive `Static` variant-name regression guard, deferred since phase-02.1 close, rolled forward unchanged through phases 02.2/03.1/03.2/04.1/04.2/04.3). Materially closes phase-04.3 REVIEW C-1 at the 05.1 state-4 phase-done verification commit (the C-1 carryforward chain originated at phase-02.2's ADR-0015 landing `435c6fa`, was discovered at phase-04.3 task 14 commit `eb6f972`, dispositioned at the phase-04.3 STATE.md handoff commit `e626862`, and ends at the 05.1 state-4 verification commit). Phase-04.1 REVIEW M-claim is unblocked but stays deferred. Implementation refinement: SPEC §3 D1 projected the new error variant on `envoy_config::ConfigError` as `ClusterDnsResolutionFailed`; the planner refined this at PLAN-write time to `envoy_cluster::ClusterError::DnsResolutionFailed` because (a) the existing `from_bootstrap` constructor lives in envoy-cluster and returns `ClusterError`, (b) SPEC §6 signpost 14 explicitly preserves envoy-cluster's typed-error chain, and (c) the alternative cross-crate wrapper-variant approach is mechanically larger; the diagnostic shape (`{cluster, address, source: std::io::Error}`) is identical, so the C-1 fix's user-facing error message is identical.

## ADR-0024: `Cluster.dns_lookup_family` field + `DnsLookupFamily` enum (parse-only)

- Date: 2026-05-02
- Status: accepted
- Context: Phase 05.1's STRICT_DNS schema landing exposed a cross-platform regression on macOS Docker: Envoy v1.33's `STRICT_DNS` cluster default `dns_lookup_family: AUTO` prefers AAAA records; macOS Docker resolves `host.docker.internal` to an IPv6 address; the fixture helper backends bind on IPv4 only (per D4 in 05.4 / Fix 1 of the backup branch) and the upstream Envoy's connect to the resolved IPv6 endpoint fails with `Connection refused` → 503 to the client. Fixing this requires forcing Envoy to resolve V4_ONLY via the cluster-level `dns_lookup_family` knob; making `envoy-config`'s parser accept the new field on the existing `Cluster` struct requires extending the schema. Phase-04.3 REVIEW C-1's IPv6/IPv4 selection regression is the cross-phase carryforward driving this decision; the original Envoy v1.33 `malformed IP address` startup error is GONE after 05.1 (per 05.1 REVIEW.md §3 I1) but the residual 503 mismatch on fixture 0008 (CI run `25258722850`) requires this fix.
- Options considered: (i) **Add `dns_lookup_family: Option<DnsLookupFamily>` parse-only field with a 3-variant enum (V4Only / V6Only / Auto).** Schema growth is ~15 LoC; runtime is unchanged (envoy-rust's `tokio::net::lookup_host` returns the system-stack default; the field is parsed-and-stored at zero cost). **Chosen.** (ii) Add the field as a typed runtime knob and filter `lookup_host` results by family. Rejected: scope inflation. envoy-rust's runtime is consuming a literal IP at the substituted `127.0.0.1:port` site (envoy-rust.yaml is unchanged in 05.4); the family filter has no observable runtime effect on envoy-rust. Adding it would land code with no test that exercises it. (iii) Add only the V4_Only variant; reject V6_Only and Auto at parse time. Rejected: brittle. Envoy's proto enum has 3 variants for v1.33 (per `ENVOY_TARGET.md` pin); accepting only one would force any future fixture using V6_Only or Auto to fail-parse with a doctrine-correct reason but no clear remediation. Better to accept the full v1.33 surface upfront. (iv) Defer the schema growth; rely on field-set divergence (envoy.yaml has the field; envoy-rust.yaml does not). Rejected: SPEC §6 signpost 4 documents that some test path may need to parse envoy.yaml through envoy-config (likely the fuzz corpus walk if a planner adds a seed). Defensive parse acceptance is the right posture.
- Decision: Extend `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs::Cluster` with `pub dns_lookup_family: Option<DnsLookupFamily>` field (defaults to `None` via `#[serde(default)]`). Add `pub enum DnsLookupFamily { V4Only, V6Only, Auto }` with `#[serde(rename_all = "SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE", deny_unknown_fields)]`. Re-export from `crates/envoy-config/src/lib.rs`. **The field is parsed-and-stored on envoy-rust's typed Cluster struct but NOT consumed at runtime** in 05.4; the existing 05.1-landed `tokio::net::lookup_host` resolution path is unchanged. The runtime non-consumption is a deliberate scope-cap matching the C-1 fix's actual need: only the upstream Envoy side observes the V4_ONLY knob (via the per-fixture envoy.yaml D2 edit at Task 2).
- Rationale: `dns_lookup_family` is required for upstream Envoy v1.33 on macOS Docker to bypass the AAAA/A selection regression. envoy-rust's typed parser must accept the field for symmetry with Envoy's proto and for potential future test paths that parse envoy.yaml through envoy-config. The runtime non-consumption preserves D-3.6 minimalism (no code with no test exercises it); whichever later phase first needs envoy-rust to filter resolved addresses by family lands the runtime extension then, with its own test.
- Consequences:
  - `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs::Cluster` gains the `dns_lookup_family: Option<DnsLookupFamily>` field (~5 LoC).
  - `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs` gains the `DnsLookupFamily` enum (~10 LoC).
  - `crates/envoy-config/src/lib.rs` re-exports `DnsLookupFamily` from the public API (~1 LoC).
  - 1 new `envoy-config` parse test exercising V4_ONLY (~25 LoC).
  - 2 hand-written `Cluster` initialiser updates in `crates/envoy-cluster/src/cluster.rs::tests` (`dns_lookup_family: None`; ~2 LoC).
  - **D2 (5-fixture envoy.yaml `dns_lookup_family: V4_ONLY` edit) becomes safe to land** — the parser accepts the new field across any test path.
  - **Phase-04.3 REVIEW C-1's IPv6/IPv4 selection regression closes** at D7 (substantively, alongside the other 5 fixes).
  - V6Only and Auto runtime semantics in envoy-rust are explicitly NOT implemented in 05.4; future phase that needs them lands the runtime extension.
  - Fuzz corpus growth deferred to PLAN-discretion (PLAN signpost H elects NOT to add a new seed in 05.4).
- Provenance: This ADR was conditionally projected as ADR-0024 in 05.1 STATE.md ("the C-1 follow-up sub-phase brainstorm has first priority on this number; if the follow-up does not land an ADR, ADR-0024 stays available for 05.2 Task 1"); the 05.4 brainstorm exercises the priority. The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is ADR-0023 (landed at 05.1 Task 1 `bfabcb6`); ADR-0024 lands at the next-sequential number with no renumbering needed.

---

## ADR-0026: `Listener.listener_filters` parse-and-ignore field in `envoy-config` (new pattern)

- Date: 2026-05-02
- Status: accepted
- Context: Phase 05.1 Task 4's CI run revealed that fixture 0006's TLS-SNI test was masked behind fixture 0008's earlier failure (alphabetic ordering); after the other fixes land, fixture 0006 surfaces as RED on macOS Docker because Envoy v1.33 does NOT auto-inject the TLS inspector listener filter for SNI-based filter chain selection (the auto-injection works on Linux but not on the Docker-Desktop/macOS combination — verified by the 05.1 aborted attempt at backup branch `backup/task4-scope-creep-2026-05-02` commit `9279895`). The fix on the upstream Envoy side is to declare the listener filter explicitly in `envoy.yaml`: `listener_filters: [{name: envoy.filters.listener.tls_inspector, ...}]`. envoy-rust performs SNI dispatch at the rustls layer (per phase 03.2's design) and does NOT execute listener filters; the field has no envoy-rust runtime semantics. However, with `envoy-config`'s parser using `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` on every struct including `Listener`, any test path that parses fixture 0006's envoy.yaml through envoy-config would fail-reject the new field — and even though no current test path does so (PLAN signpost from 05.4 SPEC §6 signpost 4), defensive acceptance is doctrinally cleaner than perpetual field-set divergence.
- Options considered: (i) **Add `Listener.listener_filters: Vec<serde_yaml::Value>` parse-and-ignore field with `#[serde(default)]`.** Stores listener-filter blocks as opaque `serde_yaml::Value`; envoy-rust never inspects or executes them. **Chosen.** (ii) Add `Listener.listener_filters: Vec<ListenerFilter>` typed-and-ignored field with a typed `ListenerFilter` enum exhausting the v1.33 set (`tls_inspector`, `original_dst`, `original_src`, `proxy_protocol`, `http_inspector`). Rejected: scope inflation. The typed enum would need ~5 variants × ~10 LoC each + per-variant typed_config payloads + 5+ parse tests; envoy-rust gains zero runtime semantics from typing them; only one variant is needed for fixture 0006's actual fix. (iii) Defer the schema growth; rely on field-set divergence (envoy.yaml has listener_filters; envoy-rust.yaml does not; no test path parses envoy.yaml through envoy-config). Rejected: brittle. SPEC §6 signpost 4 documents that the open question of "which test path parses envoy.yaml through envoy-config" may be answered YES by a future planner who adds an envoy.yaml-parsing test or fuzz seed. Defensive acceptance is the right default. (iv) Add the field as a strict `#[serde(skip)]` ignored-at-deserialization field. Rejected: this would skip the field at deserialization time entirely (the `Vec<serde_yaml::Value>` would always be empty), losing the ability for any future test or audit to introspect the parsed listener filters. Storing them as `Vec<serde_yaml::Value>` preserves the ability to inspect (e.g., a test could assert "fixture 0006 declares the tls_inspector filter" without typing the inspector itself).
- Decision: Extend `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs::Listener` with `pub listener_filters: Vec<serde_yaml::Value>` field (defaults to `vec![]` via `#[serde(default)]`). The field is parsed-and-stored as opaque YAML values; envoy-rust does NOT interpret or execute them at runtime. Add a parse test (`parses_listener_with_tls_inspector_listener_filter`) exercising the full bootstrap with a TLS-bearing listener carrying the tls_inspector block. Add `listener_filters: vec![]` to the one hand-written `Listener` initialiser in `crates/envoy-tls/src/tests.rs::synth_listener_two_tls_chains`. Add the explicit `tls_inspector` block to fixture 0006's `envoy.yaml` (only — `envoy-rust.yaml` is unchanged because envoy-rust's SNI dispatch lives at the rustls layer).
- Rationale: This is the **introduction of a new pattern in envoy-config**: parse-and-ignore for fields that envoy-rust cannot or will not consume at runtime but that upstream Envoy requires for fixture validity. Every prior YAML divergence used field-set divergence (the field exists in envoy.yaml and is absent from envoy-rust.yaml). The parse-and-ignore pattern is the right call for `listener_filters` specifically because: (a) the field carries arbitrary listener-filter typed_config payloads (multiple filter types possible; future Envoy versions may surface more); typing the variants exhaustively would be a non-trivial growth surface; (b) envoy-rust never executes listener filters by design (architectural choice from phase 03.2 — SNI lives in the rustls layer); (c) making the parse-and-ignore explicit at the schema level is more honest than maintaining field-set divergence forever, and prepares for any future test path that parses envoy.yaml through envoy-config.
- Consequences:
  - `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs::Listener` gains the `listener_filters: Vec<serde_yaml::Value>` field (~5 LoC).
  - 1 new `envoy-config` parse test exercising the tls_inspector block (~85 LoC including the YAML payload).
  - 1 hand-written `Listener` initialiser in `crates/envoy-tls/src/tests.rs` updated (`listener_filters: vec![]`; ~1 LoC).
  - `tests/fixtures/0006-tls-sni/envoy.yaml` gains the explicit listener_filters block (~9 LoC YAML).
  - **Fixture 0006's TLS-SNI handshake succeeds** on macOS Docker — substantively closes one of phase-04.3 REVIEW C-1's three latent regressions.
  - **The parse-and-ignore pattern is now a documented envoy-config posture.** Future fields that meet the criteria (Envoy-config-only with no envoy-rust runtime semantics; required for upstream-Envoy `envoy.yaml` parseability under any test path; reviewed under D-3.5 ambiguity-resolution discipline) may follow the same pattern. Whichever later phase first needs to ACTUALLY EXECUTE a listener filter lands a typed-variant extension on the field plus a runtime dispatch arm — not a new ADR (extending an existing pattern).
- Provenance: This ADR was conditionally projected as ADR-0024–0026 in 05.1 STATE.md. ADR-0024 (DnsLookupFamily) lands at 05.4 Task 1; this ADR lands at 05.4 Task 3; ADR-0025 (CL: 0 suppression) lands at 05.4 Task 5. The DECISIONS.md ledger is append-only and lists ADRs in landing-time order: ADR-0023 → ADR-0024 → ADR-0026 → ADR-0025.

---

## ADR-0025: Suppress `content-length: 0` on empty-body GET in `envoy-http1::client` (RFC 7230 §3.3.2 + Envoy v1.33 parity)

- Date: 2026-05-02
- Status: accepted
- Context: envoy-http1's client at HEAD `1d05cd0` injects a synthetic `content-length: <len>` header on every outbound request that doesn't already carry an explicit Content-Length. For empty-body requests (e.g., the HTTP/1.1 GET that fixture 0008's `Driver::Http1` issues), this emits `content-length: 0` on the wire. Envoy v1.33 honors RFC 7230 §3.3.2 ("A user agent SHOULD NOT send a Content-Length header field when the request message does not contain a payload body and the method semantics do not anticipate such a body") and OMITS Content-Length on empty-body requests. Fixture 0008's deterministic-echo body shape is a byte-for-byte alphabetic list of received headers + the body bytes; the spurious envoy-rust-side `content-length: 0` lands in the echoed body and breaks `response_body: byte_exact` against the upstream Envoy side that omits it.
- Options considered: (i) **Suppress synthetic `content-length: 0` on empty-body requests; pass through explicit Content-Length unchanged.** Behaviour change: only inject when body is non-empty AND no explicit CL is set. **Chosen.** (ii) Always emit `content-length: <len>` (status quo). Rejected: violates RFC 7230 §3.3.2; breaks fixture 0008 differential equivalence. (iii) Always emit `content-length: 0` for empty-body GET; update upstream Envoy fixture to inject `content-length: 0` on its side too via `request_headers_to_add`. Rejected: increases envoy.yaml-side asymmetry burden; is the wrong direction (fixture YAML bending around envoy-rust's misbehaviour rather than fixing envoy-rust); doesn't honor the RFC. (iv) Make the suppression a configurable HCM/Router knob. Rejected: scope inflation. RFC compliance is not a per-request opt-in; it's the correct default. If a future use case wants explicit CL: 0 (e.g., to comply with a specific upstream's quirks), it can pass an explicit Content-Length on the request, which the new code correctly passes through unchanged.
- Decision: Modify `crates/envoy-http1/src/client.rs::Client::send_request` request-write path: only inject the synthetic `content-length: <len>` header when (a) the request does not carry an explicit Content-Length AND (b) the request body is non-empty. The `body_is_nonempty` predicate uses the existing `Request::body_bytes() -> Option<&[u8]>` accessor at `crates/envoy-http1/src/codec.rs:62-64`. The 1 affected envoy-http1 unit test in `crates/envoy-http1/src/client.rs::tests` (`send_request_writes_serialized_request_bytes`) flips its assertion from `s.contains("content-length: 0\r\n")` to `!s.contains("content-length: 0\r\n")`. Fixture 0008's `expectations.yaml` removes `  content-length: 0\n` from the expected echo body.
- Rationale: RFC 7230 §3.3.2 is unambiguous: empty-body requests SHOULD NOT carry Content-Length. Envoy v1.33 honors this. Fixture 0008's differential property is "envoy ↔ envoy-rust byte-equal echo body" — both proxies must omit the header for the fixture to be green. The fix is small (~10 LoC) and correctly bounded to empty-body requests: requests with explicit Content-Length pass through unchanged (preserving any caller's deliberate Content-Length emission); requests with non-empty body continue to emit synthetic Content-Length (preserving the existing happy path).
- Consequences:
  - `crates/envoy-http1/src/client.rs` request-write path gains a `body_is_nonempty` check (~5 LoC).
  - 1 envoy-http1 unit test flips its CL: 0 assertion (~5 LoC).
  - `tests/fixtures/0008-http1-router-upstream/expectations.yaml` `expected_body` line drops `  content-length: 0\n` (~1 LoC YAML).
  - **Fixture 0008 `response_body: byte_exact` differential equivalence holds** — substantively closes one of phase-04.3 REVIEW C-1's three latent regressions (the other two being the IPv4/IPv6 selection issue addressed by ADR-0024 and the listener-filter issue addressed by ADR-0026).
  - envoy-rust now matches Envoy v1.33's request-side header emission set on empty-body requests.
  - Future requests with non-empty body continue to receive synthetic Content-Length — no regression.
- Provenance: This ADR was conditionally projected as ADR-0024 or ADR-0025 in 05.1 STATE.md (the C-1 follow-up's projected ADRs start at ADR-0024 onward). ADR-0024 is taken by the DnsLookupFamily schema (lands at 05.4 Task 1); this ADR lands at 05.4 Task 5 alongside the envoy-http1 client behaviour change. ADR-0026 (listener_filters parse-and-ignore) lands at 05.4 Task 3 numerically before this one but the landing-time order in DECISIONS.md is by task-execution order: ADR-0024 (Task 1) → ADR-0026 (Task 3) → ADR-0025 (Task 5). The ledger remains append-only with no renumbering.

## ADR-0027: `http` crate (`http::Request` / `http::Response` / `http::HeaderMap`) typed-surface scoping

- **Date:** 2026-05-03.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Phase 05.2 introduces `crates/envoy-http2/`, the workspace's sole-dep-owner of `h2 = "0.4"` (per parent-05 SPEC §3 cross-sub-phase architectural rule 1 + ADR-0022). The `h2` crate's API exposes `http::*` types directly: `h2::server::Connection::accept` returns `(http::Request<h2::RecvStream>, h2::server::SendResponse<bytes::Bytes>)`; `h2::client::SendRequest::send_request` accepts `http::Request<()>`; etc. The codec-edge translation modules in `envoy-http2` (`request.rs` Task 6, `response.rs` Task 7) import these symbols by name. The narrow scope question is whether `http` belongs as a direct dep on `crates/envoy-http2/Cargo.toml` (with this ADR documenting the narrow scoping, parallel to ADR-0021's narrow scoping for `regex`), or stays transitive-only through `h2`'s public API.
- **Options considered:**
  - **(i) Add `http = "1"` as a direct dep on `crates/envoy-http2/Cargo.toml`.** Direct imports `use http::{Request, Response, HeaderMap};` work cleanly; static-analysis tools (cargo-deny, `cargo audit`, transitive-version drift detection) see the dep explicitly. **Chosen.**
  - **(ii) Use `h2::http` re-exports to avoid a direct dep.** The `h2` crate may re-export the `http` types; investigation showed the re-export surface is partial (e.g., `http::HeaderName` not re-exported) — codec-edge translation requires reading individual fields (`request.method()`, `request.uri().path()`, `request.headers()`); incomplete re-exports block the implementation. Rejected.
  - **(iii) Use `http::*` transitively via `h2`'s public API only.** Treat `http::*` types as opaque types touched only at function boundaries. Rejected: the codec-edge translation requires field access; opaque-only access blocks the implementation.
- **Decision:** Add `http = "1"` as a direct dep on `crates/envoy-http2/Cargo.toml`. Narrowly scoped: only `crates/envoy-http2/` imports `http::*` symbols; no other workspace crate imports `http::*` directly (verifiable by `grep -rn 'use http::' crates/`). This is parallel to ADR-0021's narrow scoping for `regex` (where `regex` is permitted only on `crates/envoy-config/` for header / route matching at config-load time).
- **Rationale:** Direct deps are easier to reason about at static-analysis time. The `http` crate is dual-licensed MIT/Apache-2.0 (already covered by `deny.toml` license allow-list) and is the de-facto Rust HTTP types crate (used by `hyper`, `reqwest`, `axum`, etc.; first-party `rust-lang` org maintenance). Treating its first use as a foundation grant — bounded narrowly to the codec-edge translation surface in `envoy-http2` — is the cheapest, most honest formalization of the dep direction.
- **Consequences:** `crates/envoy-http2/Cargo.toml`'s `[dependencies]` section lists `http = "1"`. `Cargo.lock` formalizes `http` as a direct surface (it's already present transitively via `h2`'s deps, so the lock-file diff is structural-only — `http` moves from a transitive dep to a direct dep). Future scope-extension ADRs (HCM internal use of `http::*` types beyond codec-edge translation, filter-framework `http::*` types) name this ADR explicitly. `cargo-deny` license check at Task 1 + Task 14 confirms the existing MIT/Apache-2.0 allow-list covers `http`.
- **Provenance:** projected as conditional ADR-0024 in 05.2 SPEC §7 + parent-05 SPEC §7. Renumbered to ADR-0027 at landing time because phase 05.4 landed ADR-0024 / ADR-0025 / ADR-0026 between SPEC writeup (parent-05 state-2 commit `f1804a7`) and 05.2 execution. Mirrors the phase-03 ADR-renumbering precedent documented in `STATE.md` Notes section. Lands at 05.2 Task 1 alongside the `crates/envoy-http2/Cargo.toml` scaffold creation.

## ADR-0028: Resolution of the `envoy-http1` ↔ `envoy-http2` cycle introduced by SPEC §3 D4 router dispatch

- **Date:** 2026-05-04.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Phase 05.3 SPEC §3 D4 projects the router H2-arm dispatch landing at TWO sites: (a) `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs`'s `BuildOutcome::Proxy` arm (H1-listener side) and (b) `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs`'s `BuildOutcome::Proxy` arm (H2-listener side, replacing 05.2's 502 stub). Both sites need to call BOTH `envoy_http1::Client` (for H1 clusters) AND `envoy_http2::Client` (for H2 clusters). At HEAD, `crates/envoy-http2/Cargo.toml:21` already path-deps `envoy-http1` (per 05.2 Task 1, for `HCMConfig` + `build_response` + the `BuildOutcome` enum per SPEC §3 cross-sub-phase architectural rule 2). Adding `envoy-http2` as a path-dep of `envoy-http1` would create a circular dep that Cargo rejects. The cycle was unanticipated at parent-05 + 05.3 SPEC writeup time (commit `f1804a7`).
- **Options considered:**
  - **(A) Trait-object hoist via `envoy-bin`.** Define a trait `UpstreamDispatch` (in `envoy-http1` or a new `envoy-router-core` crate); have `envoy-bin` wire a `ProtocolAwareDispatch` impl that knows how to call both Clients; thread `Arc<dyn UpstreamDispatch>` through `HCMConfig` to the `BuildOutcome::Proxy` arms on both H1 and H2 listener sides. ~200-250 LoC restructure across `envoy-http1`, `envoy-http2`, `envoy-bin`, and tests.
  - **(B) Defer the H1-listener-side dispatch.** Ship only the H2-listener-side dispatch at Task 7 (which lives inside `envoy-http2` and can call both `envoy_http1::Client` and `envoy_http2::Client` without cycle since `envoy-http2` already deps on `envoy-http1`). Fixture 0010 (H2 listener + H2 cluster, per SPEC §1 D7) still passes via Task 7's dispatch. The H1-listener-with-H2-cluster path (an H1 downstream listener proxying to an H2 upstream cluster) defers to a later phase. ~50 LoC scope (ADR + PROGRESS only).
  - **(C) Hoist `Client` + `ClientStream` out of `envoy-http2` into a new `envoy-http-client` crate** depended on by both `envoy-http1` and `envoy-http2`. Cleanest break of the cycle but largest restructure (~400 LoC moved across 3+ crates).
- **Decision:** **(B) — defer the H1-listener-side dispatch.** Phase 05.3 ships only the H2-listener-side dispatch at Task 7. The H1-listener-side `BuildOutcome::Proxy` arm at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:209-303` retains the existing 04.3 H1-only dispatch unchanged. SPEC §3 D4 H1-side projection is partial; the H1-listener-with-H2-cluster path defers to a later phase that lands either (A) or (C).
- **Rationale:** Phase 05.3's only new fixture (0010) is H2-listener + H2-cluster per SPEC §1 D7 — the H1-listener-side dispatch is not exercised by any 05.3 fixture. The full SPEC §3 D4 acceptance signal is "fixture 0010 green at the Docker-gated CI level" per SPEC §1 acceptance signal (a); option (B) achieves this with ~50 LoC of work vs option (A)'s ~200-250. The architectural cleanup (option (A) or (C)) is a tractable later-phase task once a fixture forces the H1-listener-with-H2-cluster path.
- **Consequences:**
  - H1-listener-with-H2-cluster combinations are NOT supported in 05.3. An H1 listener can proxy only to H1 clusters (the existing 04.3 path). An H2 listener (Task 7) can proxy to either H1 or H2 clusters.
  - SPEC §3 D4 H1-side projection ("the dispatch ALSO lands at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs`") is partial — recorded in PROGRESS Task 6 + the 05.3 REVIEW.md state-5 file.
  - The cycle resolution scope is reset to a later phase. The REVIEW.md state-5 file flags this as a carryforward; the 05.3 state-6 commit's `Phase-05.3 rollovers` STATE.md subsection records the deferral.
  - No new top-level Cargo deps in Task 6.
  - The existing `envoy-http2 → envoy-http1` path-dep is preserved unchanged.
- **Provenance:** unanticipated at parent-05 SPEC + 05.3 SPEC writeup (commit `f1804a7`). Cycle surfaced at 05.3 Task 6 task time when the planner attempted to add `envoy-http2` to `crates/envoy-http1/Cargo.toml` per SPEC §3 D4. Per D-3.5 (decisions are written, not remembered), this ADR lands inline at Task 6's commit.

## ADR-0029: Split phase 06 into sub-phases 06.1, 06.2, and 06.3

- **Date:** 2026-05-09.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Phase 06 ("Access log (file sink, Envoy default format) + stats + Prometheus admin endpoint") was projected as a single phase by `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §8 row 06. The parent-06 state-1 brainstorm (parent SPEC at `docs/envoy-rust/phases/06-observability/SPEC.md`, committed at the predecessor of this commit) resolved a cascade of scope decisions that, taken together, push the phase past the §6.1 split-gate (~25 tasks / ~1500 LoC):
  - **(a)** New workspace member `crates/envoy-stats/` (sole-dep-owner of any stats deps; `Counter`/`Gauge`/`StatsRegistry`/`prometheus::write_exposition` primitives, hand-rolled atop `std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, AtomicI64}` + `std::sync::RwLock<HashMap<...>>`, no new permitted-foundations grant) + new workspace member `crates/envoy-admin/` (sole-dep-owner of admin-listener wiring; `AdminHandler` + `AdminEndpoint` enum, depends on `envoy-http1` for HCM dispatch + `envoy-stats` for the registry read) + phase-01 admin migration (the bare-bones `/ready` handler at `crates/envoy-bin/src/main.rs` migrates to a real HCM-backed listener serving `/ready` + `/stats` + `/stats/prometheus`) + envoy-config schema additions (`Admin.access_log_path` parse-and-ignore; `HttpConnectionManagerConfig.stat_prefix` parse-and-consume) + representative stats wiring (one counter per layer: `listener.<name>.downstream_cx_total`, `cluster.<name>.upstream_cx_total`, `http.<stat_prefix>.downstream_rq_total`) + new differential fixture `0011-admin-stats-prometheus` + harness `Driver::AdminScrape` + `BodyRule::PrometheusExposition` + first-time population of BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md `Stat-name mapping` section (initial entries) (~1300 LoC, ~12 tasks).
  - **(b)** New workspace member `crates/envoy-accesslog/` (sole-dep-owner of any access-log deps; `AccessLogRecord` struct, `FileSink`, hand-rolled Envoy default-format emitter with hand-rolled ISO-8601 timestamp emitter — 14 tokens per Envoy v1.33 default format) + envoy-config schema additions (`HttpConnectionManagerConfig.access_log: Vec<AccessLogConfig>` with file-sink-only validator gate + `ConfigError::UnsupportedAccessLogType` typed-error variant) + HCM access-log wiring at on-response-complete time (fire-and-forget; emission errors must not affect the response-write path) + new differential fixture `0012-access-log-file-sink` + harness `Driver::Http1WithAccessLog` + per-token `AccessLogLineRule` + first-time population of BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md `Access log field mapping` section (~1300 LoC, ~11 tasks).
  - **(c)** Comprehensive stats wiring extending 06.1's representative subset (per-response-class HCM counters `http.<stat_prefix>.downstream_rq_{2xx,3xx,4xx,5xx}`; connection-lifetime gauges `listener.<name>.downstream_cx_active` + `cluster.<name>.upstream_cx_active`; upstream-rq counters `cluster.<name>.upstream_rq_total` + `..._upstream_rq_5xx`; access-log-line counter `http.<stat_prefix>.access_logs_total`; listener accept-failure counter `listener.<name>.downstream_cx_accept_failed`) + 05.3 REVIEW I1 closure preamble at Task 1 (`ConfigError::Http2ClusterFromHttp1Listener` parse-time validator gate, mirroring phase-05.1 Task 1's posture toward phase-02.1 REVIEW I3) + extension of fixture 0011's `expectations.yaml` to assert the comprehensive stat set + extension of BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md `Stat-name mapping` section + parent-06 close-out (~1200 LoC, ~10 tasks).

  Combined scope across all three layers totals ~3800 LoC and ~33 tasks for a single-sub-phase 06.α (everything-in-one), exceeding §6.1 gates by ~150% in LoC and ~30% in task count.
- **Options considered:**
  - **(i) Single phase.** Rejected — exceeds §6.1 gates by ~150% in LoC and ~30% in task count. A plan that lands ~3800 LoC of mixed new-crate scaffolding (two new crates) + cross-crate wiring (HCM + listener + cluster + admin) + two new differential fixtures + first-time population of two BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md sections historically splits under mid-execution pressure (§6.1's in-flight trigger when any single task's sub-steps blow past ~10 items), which is strictly worse than splitting at state 2.
  - **(ii) Two-way split** (06.α = stats + admin + comprehensive wiring; 06.β = accesslog + parent close). Rejected — 06.α at ~2500+ LoC sits well past the §6.1 gate even before drift; bundling comprehensive stats wiring with the foundation work in one slice provides no review-surface clarity (the comprehensive-wiring slice naturally couples with the parent-close instead, mirroring phase-05.3's posture as the closing sub-phase).
  - **(iii) Two-way split** (06.α = stats + admin; 06.β = accesslog + comprehensive wiring + parent close). Rejected — 06.β at ~2500 LoC; bundling accesslog with the comprehensive wiring slice scatters the access-log review surface across the same sub-phase that closes the parent-06 ROADMAP row, recreating the gate-pressure that motivated the split.
  - **(iv) Two-way split** (06.α = stats; 06.β = accesslog + admin + comprehensive wiring + parent close). Rejected — admin's natural pair is stats (admin reads the registry to emit Prometheus exposition); landing them apart leaves 06.α as a "stats subsystem with no consumer" intermediate state with no fixture to demonstrate the foundation works end-to-end.
  - **(v) Four-way split** (06.α = stats; 06.β = admin; 06.γ = accesslog; 06.δ = comprehensive wiring + parent close). Rejected — artificially separates stats primitives from one demonstration consumer (admin/Prometheus); each slice would be ~700-1000 LoC, too small to motivate a full state-machine cycle of brainstorm + plan + execute + verify + review for each.
  - **(vi) Nested split of 06.α** (06 → 06.α → 06.α.1 / 06.α.2; 06 → 06.β). Rejected — three-level numbering is awkward (`06.α.1`); BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md §6.1 explicitly flags nested splits of an already-split sub-phase as suspicious and prescribes `superpowers:systematic-debugging` first.
  - **(vii) 3-way flat split by surface boundary** — preamble (stats foundation + admin migration + representative wiring) → independent slice (access log) → cleanup slice (comprehensive wiring + 05.3 I1 closure + parent close). Chosen.
- **Decision:** split phase 06 into three sub-phases by surface boundary, not by traffic direction (which doesn't apply to observability):
  - **06.1 (`06.1-stats-and-admin`)** — new workspace member `crates/envoy-stats/` (`Counter` over `AtomicU64`; `Gauge` over `AtomicI64`; `StatsRegistry` over `RwLock<HashMap<String, StatHandle>>`; `prometheus::write_exposition` emitter) + new workspace member `crates/envoy-admin/` (`AdminHandler` + `AdminEndpoint::{Ready, Stats, StatsPrometheus}`; HTTP/1.1 only in 06.1 — H2 admin defers; exact-match path routing only) + phase-01 admin migration at `crates/envoy-bin/src/main.rs` (the bare-bones `/ready` handler is replaced by an HCM-backed admin listener constructed from `envoy_admin::AdminHandler`; regression-guarded by an in-process backstop test at `crates/envoy-bin/tests/admin_ready.rs` plus the existing Docker-gated fixture `0002-static-admin-ready`) + envoy-config schema additions (`Admin.access_log_path: Option<String>` parse-and-ignore per the ADR-0026 pattern; `HttpConnectionManagerConfig.stat_prefix: Option<String>` parse-and-consume — threaded into HCM stats namespace) + representative stats wiring (`Listener.cx_total: Arc<Counter>`; `Cluster.cx_total: Arc<Counter>`; `HCMStats.downstream_rq_total: Arc<Counter>`) + new differential fixture `tests/fixtures/0011-admin-stats-prometheus/` + harness `Driver::AdminScrape { path, expected_status, expected_content_type, expected_body_rule }` + `BodyRule::PrometheusExposition { allowlist_envoy_only, allowlist_envoy_rust_only }` (asserts on the symmetric difference of metric names between the two proxies' scrapes) + first-time population of BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md `Stat-name mapping` section with initial entries for the representative subset. **NO access-log work in 06.1.** Conditional ADR-0030 (foundations grant for `time = "0.3"` or similar) and conditional ADR-0031 (Cargo.lock cadence) stay available; recommended posture is no foundations grants. ~1300 LoC, ~12 tasks.
  - **06.2 (`06.2-access-log`)** — new workspace member `crates/envoy-accesslog/` (`AccessLogRecord` struct with 15 fields covering the 14-token Envoy default format; `FileSink` impl using `tokio::fs::OpenOptions::append(true)` + `AsyncWriteExt::write_all` + a per-sink `tokio::sync::Mutex<File>` for append-serialized writes; `default_format::format(record) -> String` emitter; hand-rolled ISO-8601 emitter from `std::time::SystemTime` with a Gregorian calendar arithmetic helper + golden tests for known epoch seconds + leap-day boundary; the `Sink` trait is **deferred** per parent SPEC §3 D8.2 option (c) — `FileSink` ships concretely; trait + multi-sink dispatch land when N≥2 sinks exist) + envoy-config schema additions for `access_log:` block on `HttpConnectionManagerConfig` (file-sink-only validator gate via `ConfigError::UnsupportedAccessLogType { actual: String }` typed-error variant; ≥1 fuzz-corpus seed `hcm_access_log_file.yaml` extending the existing `parse_bootstrap` corpus) + HCM access-log wiring at on-response-complete time (the existing `write_response` call site at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs`; dispatch is fire-and-forget, errors logged via `tracing::warn!` — emission failures must not affect the response-write path; the H2 HCM inherits the wiring via the `HCMConfig` type-alias from phase 05.2) + new differential fixture `tests/fixtures/0012-access-log-file-sink/` + harness `Driver::Http1WithAccessLog { ..., expected_access_log_lines: Vec<AccessLogLineRule> }` + `AccessLogLineRule::{Exact, Iso8601Format, DurationMs, Wildcard, EnvoyOnly, ...}` per-token rules + first-time population of BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md `Access log field mapping` section per parent SPEC §2.2 (one row per default-format token, marked `value-exact` or `name-required, value-may-differ` per the parent-SPEC's projection rules). Conditional ADR-0030 (foundations grant for `async_trait = "0.1"`) stays available; recommended posture is option (c) of parent SPEC §3 D8.2 — defer the `Sink` trait until N≥2 sinks. ~1300 LoC, ~11 tasks.
  - **06.3 (`06.3-stats-wiring-and-close`)** — 05.3 REVIEW I1 closure preamble at Task 1 (`ConfigError::Http2ClusterFromHttp1Listener { listener: String, cluster: String }` parse-time validator gate at `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs::validate`; the per-listener cluster-reachability scan rejects an H1-listener or AUTO-listener configured with an H2-cluster; mirrors phase-05.1 Task 1's posture toward phase-02.1 REVIEW I3 — a previously-identified gap closed cheaply at the start of a later phase) + comprehensive stats wiring across HCM/router/listener/cluster (per-response-class HCM counters `http.<stat_prefix>.downstream_rq_{2xx,3xx,4xx,5xx}`; connection-lifetime gauges `listener.<name>.downstream_cx_active` + `cluster.<name>.upstream_cx_active`; upstream-rq counters `cluster.<name>.upstream_rq_total` + `..._upstream_rq_5xx`; access-log-line counter `http.<stat_prefix>.access_logs_total`; listener accept-failure counter `listener.<name>.downstream_cx_accept_failed`) + extension of fixture 0011's `expectations.yaml` to assert the comprehensive stat set + extension of BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md `Stat-name mapping` section with one entry per new stat (each with `value-exact` or `name-required, value-may-differ` disposition + rationale) + parent-06 state-6 close-out. The 06.3 state-6 phase-done commit ALSO flips parent ROADMAP row `06` from `in-progress` to `done` per the ROADMAP-schema invariant ("the parent flips to `done` only after all sub-phases are `done`"); mirrors phase 04's `e626862`-shape close-out and phase 05's `82c26b8`-shape close-out. ~1200 LoC, ~10 tasks.
- **Rationale:** the 3-way flat split mirrors phase-04's `1d9740d`-shape precedent (under ADR-0020) and phase-05's `f1804a7`-shape precedent (under ADR-0022); this is the third 3-way split in the project. Each of the three sub-phases fits comfortably under the §6.1 gates with healthy headroom (~13-20%) against likely execution-time drift (per phase-04.3's ~+20% drift experience and phase-05.3's similar drift). Failure attribution localizes per sub-phase: 06.1's REVIEW.md owns the stats-foundation + admin-listener-migration verdict (and the regression-equivalence of fixture 0002's `/ready` semantics post-migration); 06.2's REVIEW.md owns the access-log + first-fixture-token-equivalence verdict + the BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md `Access log field mapping` first-population audit; 06.3's REVIEW.md owns the comprehensive-stats-wiring verdict + the 05.3 I1 closure + the parent-06 close. The split boundary is by subsystem coherence (stats+admin → accesslog → comprehensive wiring), not by traffic direction (which doesn't apply to observability). The stats+admin pair is coherent (admin is the natural first consumer of the stats registry — it reads stats to emit Prometheus exposition; landing them together avoids an awkward "stats subsystem with no consumer" intermediate state); the access-log subsystem is independent (it consumes request/response state and emits lines, with only tangential stats-counter wiring deferred to 06.3); the comprehensive-wiring slice extends 06.1's foundations across the codebase systematically and naturally pairs with the parent close-out and the 05.3 I1 closure preamble. Sub-phase ordering invariant: 06.1 → 06.2 → 06.3 cannot be parallelized because (a) 06.2's HCM access-log wiring is co-located with the on-response-complete site that 06.3's per-response-class counters also touch (06.3 extends the same site), (b) 06.3's access-log-line counter (`http.<stat_prefix>.access_logs_total`) references both the registry registered in 06.1 and the access-log emission added in 06.2, (c) 06.3 closes the parent-06 ROADMAP row.
- **Consequences:** parent SPEC at `docs/envoy-rust/phases/06-observability/SPEC.md` was committed at parent-06 state-1 (the predecessor of this commit) projecting this split per parent-SPEC §5 + §7 + §8; sub-phase SPECs (`06.1-stats-and-admin/SPEC.md`, `06.2-access-log/SPEC.md`, `06.3-stats-wiring-and-close/SPEC.md`) land alongside this ADR in this same parent-06 state-2 commit. ROADMAP gains 3 new rows (`06.1`, `06.2`, `06.3`) at this commit; row `06`'s `sub-phases` column updates from `—` to `06.1, 06.2, 06.3`; row `06`'s `status` remains `in-progress` (it flipped to `in-progress` at the parent-06 state-1 close-out commit when STATE.md first pointed at phase 06). Sub-phases ship strictly in order (06.1 → 06.2 → 06.3) — they cannot be parallelized per the rationale above. Parent ROADMAP row `06` flips `done` at sub-phase 06.3's state-6 phase-done commit, mirroring phase 04's `e626862`-shape close-out and phase 05's `82c26b8`-shape close-out. STATE.md advances at this commit to point at `06.1` lifecycle state 1 (next-skill `superpowers:brainstorming` scoped to 06.1's surface). Phase 06's projected ADR ledger after this commit: ADR-0029 (this ADR; landed at this commit). **Conditional ADRs stay available:** ADR-0030 (foundations grant for `time = "0.3"` or `async_trait = "0.1"`; not pre-projected; recommended posture is no foundations grants — D-3.2 lists *Stats subsystem* / *Access log formatters and sinks* / *Admin API* on the *Must be written from scratch* list, so counter/gauge primitives, Prometheus exposition format emitter, and Envoy default-format access-log emitter are all hand-rolled atop existing `tokio` + `bytes` + `tracing` + `thiserror` + `serde_yaml` foundations); ADR-0031 (Cargo.lock cadence ratification, conditional on ADR-0030 actually landing — phase-04.1 REVIEW M5/M9 carries forward unchanged unless ADR-0030 forces a cadence pick). Each sub-phase's brainstorm may surface additional ADR-shaped decisions (a stats-name validation rule, a Prometheus exposition format edge case, an access-log token semantic); numbers stay available, and each sub-phase's SPEC §7 projects its own ADRs at its brainstorm time.
- **Provenance:** this ADR was projected as the next-sequential available ADR number in parent-06 SPEC §7 (`docs/envoy-rust/phases/06-observability/SPEC.md`, committed at parent-06 state-1 — the predecessor of this commit). The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0028** (landed at phase 05.3 Task 6 `366af8b` — H1↔H2 cycle resolution); ADR-0029 lands at the next-sequential number with no renumbering needed (no inter-ADR landings between phase-05.3 close at `82c26b8` and this commit). Like phase-04's split (ADR-0020) and phase-05's split (ADR-0022), phase-06's projected ADR-0029 number matches the actual landed number; ADR-0030 and ADR-0031 are CONDITIONAL and may not land — if no sub-phase ratifies a foundations grant or a Cargo.lock cadence ADR, those numbers stay available for later phases per the established ledger discipline (parent-04's ADR-0020 + ADR-0021 landed without renumbering; parent-05's ADR-0022 + ADR-0023 landed without renumbering; ADR-0024 / ADR-0025 / ADR-0026 / ADR-0027 / ADR-0028 reflect the actual landing sequence per their provenance footers).

## ADR-0030: Split phase 07 into sub-phases 07.1 and 07.2

- **Date:** 2026-05-12.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Phase 07 ("Filter chain framework: iteration protocol, per-route config, extension registry") was projected as a single phase by `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §8 row 07. The parent-07 state-1 brainstorm (parent SPEC at `docs/envoy-rust/phases/07-filter-chain-framework/SPEC.md`, committed at the predecessor of this commit `7337f2c`) resolved a cascade of scope decisions that, taken together, push the phase past the §6.1 split-gate (~25 tasks / ~1500 LoC):
  - **(a)** New workspace member `crates/envoy-filter/` (sole-dep-owner of filter-chain dispatch logic; `HttpFilterInstance` enum, `FilterPipeline` struct, `Decision::{Continue, StopAndSend(Response)}` enum, `build_from_config` constructor, `decode_headers` + `encode_headers` iteration methods; module decomposition `lib.rs` + `pipeline.rs` + `instance.rs` + `router.rs` + `error.rs`; new typed-error `FilterError`; synchronous non-async iteration per parent-SPEC §6 Rule 5; depends on `envoy-config` + `envoy-http1::codec` value-types only — `envoy-http1`'s HCM types are NOT consumed, preserving the `envoy-config → envoy-filter → envoy-http1, envoy-http2 → envoy-bin` dep stack with no cycles per parent-SPEC §6 Rule 10) + HCM filter-chain wiring at both H1 (`crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs::serve_connection`) and H2 (`crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs::handle_one_stream` + `finalize_h2_stream`) sites + writer-arm refactor at H1 (factoring wire-write out of the 5 writer arms at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:378-516` so `encode_headers` runs AFTER the writer arm has populated the response but BEFORE the wire write per parent-SPEC §6 Rule 8 — the load-bearing structural change) + symmetric `finalize_h2_stream` refactor at H2 + terminal-router validator at `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs::validate_hcm` (relaxes the existing cardinality gate at lines 1335-1347 from `len != 1` to `len >= 1 AND Router is the last entry AND no duplicate Router`; new `ConfigError` variants `EmptyHttpFilters` / `RouterNotTerminal { listener, position }` / `DuplicateRouterFilter { listener }`; existing `MultipleHttpFilters` variant retained but no longer constructed). **No new fixture** — regression-equivalence under the existing Router-only chain is the differential proof; all 12 existing fixtures (`0001-tcp-echo` through `0012-access-log-file-sink`) must stay green simultaneously at the Docker-gated CI level as 07.1's state-4 phase-done gate (~900 LoC, ~9 tasks).
  - **(b)** First concrete pluggable filter — `envoy.filters.http.header_mutation` (Envoy v1.33 has this as a real filter at `envoy.extensions.filters.http.header_mutation.v3.HeaderMutation`) — extending `HttpFilterInstance` with a `HeaderMutation(HeaderMutationFilter)` variant + envoy-config schema additions (`HttpFilterTypedConfig::HeaderMutation` variant; `HeaderMutationConfig` / `Mutations` / `HeaderMutationEntry` / `HeaderValueOption` / `HeaderValue` / `AppendAction` structs; all with `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]`) + `AppendAction` MVP support for `APPEND_IF_EXISTS_OR_ADD` + `OVERWRITE_IF_EXISTS_OR_ADD` (other variants `ADD_IF_ABSENT` / `OVERWRITE_IF_EXISTS` validator-reject via `ConfigError::UnsupportedHeaderMutationAppendAction`) + validator extension at `validate_http_filters` (per-entry: non-empty key + RFC 7230 token-set + `append_action` in supported subset) + fuzz corpus extension (`hcm_header_mutation_filter.yaml` seed at `crates/envoy-config/fuzz/corpus/parse_bootstrap/`) + new differential fixture `tests/fixtures/0013-http-filter-header-mutation/` (HeaderMutation in front of Router on a Router-proxied route to an Http1EchoBackend; appends `x-filter-stamp: phase-07` on request_mutations + `x-filter-response-stamp: phase-07` on response_mutations; bilaterally asserted — the Http1EchoBackend echoes received request headers into the response body, proving both decode-side stamp landed at backend AND encode-side stamp landed at client) + in-process backstop at `crates/envoy-bin/tests/http_filter_header_mutation.rs` + parent-07 state-6 close-out (closing sub-phase flips parent ROADMAP row `07` from `in-progress` to `done` per the ROADMAP-schema invariant) (~800 LoC, ~10 tasks).

  Combined scope across the two layers totals ~1700 LoC and ~19 tasks for a single-sub-phase 07.α (everything-in-one), exceeding the §6.1 LoC gate by ~13% and the task-count gate by ~24% — both gates are below the ~+20% execution-time drift observed at phase-04.3 and phase-05.3, so a single-phase shipment risks a §6.2 anti-pattern mid-execution re-split when the H1 writer-arm refactor surfaces complexity (any single task's sub-steps blow past ~10 items).
- **Options considered:**
  - **(i) Single phase.** Rejected — exceeds §6.1 LoC gate by ~13% and task gate by ~24% before drift; after applying the parent-04.3 / parent-05.3 ~+20% drift adjustment, combined scope projects to ~2040 LoC, well over the cap. The H1 writer-arm refactor (factoring wire-write out of each of the 5 writer arms at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:378-516` so `encode_headers` can run BEFORE the wire write) is the load-bearing complexity contributor: mechanically simple per arm but highly cross-cutting. Single-phase shipment historically splits under mid-execution pressure (§6.1's in-flight trigger when any single task's sub-steps blow past ~10 items), which is strictly worse than splitting at state 2.
  - **(ii) Three-way split** (07.α = `envoy-filter` foundation crate only; 07.β = HCM filter-chain wiring + writer-arm refactor + validator; 07.γ = HeaderMutation filter + fixture 0013 + parent close). Rejected — the framework crate is incomplete without its consumer-side invocation (07.α would ship a library crate with no behavior-equivalence proof; the regression-equivalence guarantee comes from consumer wiring exercising all 12 existing fixtures); 07.β's HCM-integration is mechanically intertwined with the framework's iteration types (decode_headers / encode_headers consume FilterPipeline mutability; the writer-arm refactor co-emerges with the encode-side invocation site); splitting at this boundary leaves 07.α as a "framework primitives library with no consumer" intermediate state with no fixture to demonstrate the foundation works. Each slice would also be ~500-600 LoC, too small to motivate a full state-machine cycle.
  - **(iii) Two-way split by code area** (07.α = envoy-config schema additions for HeaderMutation + HttpFilterTypedConfig variant; 07.β = envoy-filter runtime + HCM wiring + HeaderMutation runtime + fixture). Rejected — mixes the framework foundation (envoy-filter crate creation + HCM wiring + writer-arm refactor) with the consumer-side filter (HeaderMutation runtime + fixture) in one sub-phase 07.β, leaving 07.α as a thin schema-only sub-phase that does not stand alone (no runtime can exercise the parsed config). Also scatters the HeaderMutation surface across two sub-phases for arbitrary scope-fit reasons.
  - **(iv) Two-way split with Router-as-first-filter at 07.1 and HeaderMutation at 07.2** (decision; chosen). Each half stands alone with one direction of dependency. 07.1 delivers the framework foundation + HCM integration as one coherent slice (the framework crate and its consumer sites are architecturally inseparable); the differential surface is regression-equivalence (all 12 existing fixtures stay green simultaneously). 07.2 delivers the first concrete pluggable filter as the demonstration slice; the differential surface is fixture 0013 lighting up bilaterally (request-side stamp at backend via Http1EchoBackend body echo; response-side stamp at client) plus the parent-07 close-out.
  - **(v) Nested split of 07.1** (07 → 07.1 → 07.1.1 / 07.1.2; 07 → 07.2). Rejected — three-level numbering is awkward (`07.1.1`); BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md §6.1 explicitly flags nested splits of an already-split sub-phase as suspicious and prescribes `superpowers:systematic-debugging` first. The 2-way split at (iv) keeps each sub-phase under both §6.1 gates with healthy drift headroom; no nesting is needed.
- **Decision:** split phase 07 into two sub-phases by deliverable cohesion (foundation + first consumer/parent-close), mirroring phase-02's ADR-0013 2-way precedent and phase-03's ADR-0017 2-way precedent:
  - **07.1 (`07.1-filter-framework-foundation`)** — new workspace member `crates/envoy-filter/` (sole-dep-owner of filter-chain dispatch logic; `HttpFilterInstance` enum Router-only at 07.1; `FilterPipeline` struct holding `Vec<HttpFilterInstance>`; `Decision::{Continue, StopAndSend(Response)}` enum from day one for forward-compat — no 07.x filter emits StopAndSend in MVP; `FilterError` typed-error; `build_from_config` constructor; `decode_headers` + `encode_headers` non-async iteration methods; depends on `envoy-config` + `envoy-http1::codec` only; `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` per D-3.8) + HCM filter-chain wiring at H1 (`crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs::serve_connection` decode-side invocation immediately after `parse_request` returns Ok and before `build_response`; encode-side invocation at the unified factored site after the writer-arm match populates the response value but before the wire write — requires the load-bearing 5-writer-arm refactor at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:378-516`) + HCM filter-chain wiring at H2 (symmetric `finalize_h2_stream` refactor at `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs`; decode-side invocation in `handle_one_stream` after `http_to_envoy_request` translation; encode-side invocation in `finalize_h2_stream` before `send_envoy_response`) + terminal-router validator at `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs::validate_hcm` (relaxes the existing `MultipleHttpFilters` cardinality gate at lines 1335-1347 from `len != 1` to `len >= 1 AND Router-last AND no-duplicate-Router`; new `ConfigError` variants `EmptyHttpFilters { listener: String }`, `RouterNotTerminal { listener: String, position: usize }`, `DuplicateRouterFilter { listener: String }`). **No new fixture in 07.1** — the framework is regression-equivalent under the existing Router-only chain; the state-4 phase-done gate verifies all 12 existing fixtures `0001-0012` stay green simultaneously at the Docker-gated CI level, proving the framework wiring introduces no wire-behavior regression. Recommended posture: no new foundations grants (synchronous non-async iteration avoids `async_trait`; manipulates only `Vec<(String, String)>` + `Bytes` primitives). ~900 LoC, ~9 tasks. Depends on `06` (done as of `b918f33`).
  - **07.2 (`07.2-header-mutation-filter`)** — extends `HttpFilterInstance` with a `HeaderMutation(HeaderMutationFilter)` variant + `HeaderMutationFilter` runtime struct holding `request_mutations: Vec<RuntimeHeaderMutation>` + `response_mutations: Vec<RuntimeHeaderMutation>` (with `RuntimeHeaderMutation { key: String /* lowercased once at build time */, value: String, action: RuntimeAppendAction }`) + `FilterPipeline::build_from_config` HeaderMutation arm + `HeaderMutationFilter::decode_headers` (request_mutations applied to request headers) + `HeaderMutationFilter::encode_headers` (response_mutations applied to response headers) + envoy-config schema additions at `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs:442-447` (`HttpFilterTypedConfig::HeaderMutation(HeaderMutationConfig)` variant; new structs `HeaderMutationConfig { mutations: Mutations }`, `Mutations { request_mutations: Vec<HeaderMutationEntry>, response_mutations: Vec<HeaderMutationEntry> }`, `HeaderMutationEntry { append: HeaderValueOption }`, `HeaderValueOption { header: HeaderValue, append_action: AppendAction }`, `HeaderValue { key: String, value: String }`, `AppendAction` enum with MVP variants `APPEND_IF_EXISTS_OR_ADD` + `OVERWRITE_IF_EXISTS_OR_ADD`; all with `#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]`) + validator extension at `validate_http_filters` (per-entry: `header.key` non-empty + RFC 7230 token-set; `append_action` in supported subset; new `ConfigError::UnsupportedHeaderMutationAppendAction` variant) + fuzz corpus extension (`hcm_header_mutation_filter.yaml` seed at `crates/envoy-config/fuzz/corpus/parse_bootstrap/`; `.gitignore` allow-list entry; `fuzz_corpus_seeds_parse_or_reject_cleanly` test extension) + new differential fixture `tests/fixtures/0013-http-filter-header-mutation/` (5 files: `envoy.yaml` with HCM `http_filters: [HeaderMutation, Router]` chain appending `x-filter-stamp: phase-07` on request_mutations + `x-filter-response-stamp: phase-07` on response_mutations, single route to an Http1EchoBackend cluster; `envoy-rust.yaml` identical modulo the per-side STRICT_DNS / dns_lookup_family pattern from fixture 0008; `inputs/payload.bin` 0-byte placeholder; `expectations.yaml` asserting bilateral equivalence on response status + body + headers; `README.md` explaining the surface) + Docker-gated `tests/differential/tests/http_filter_header_mutation.rs` + in-process backstop at `crates/envoy-bin/tests/http_filter_header_mutation.rs` + parent-07 state-6 close-out. The 07.2 state-6 phase-done commit ALSO flips parent ROADMAP row `07` from `in-progress` to `done` per the ROADMAP-schema invariant ("the parent flips to `done` only after all sub-phases are `done`"); mirrors phase-02's `f04e21a`-shape close-out, phase-03's `ca81226`-shape close-out, phase-04's `e626862`-shape close-out, phase-05's `82c26b8`-shape close-out, and phase-06's `b918f33`-shape close-out. ~800 LoC, ~10 tasks. Depends on `07.1`.
- **Rationale:** the 2-way flat split mirrors phase-02's `1c38ca9`-shape precedent (under ADR-0013) and phase-03's `8b75aaf`-shape precedent (under ADR-0017); this is the third 2-way split in the project (phases 02 / 03 / 07 each split 2-way; phases 04 / 05 / 06 each split 3-way). Each of the two sub-phases fits comfortably under the §6.1 gates with healthy headroom (~40% LoC headroom on 07.1, ~47% on 07.2) against likely execution-time drift (per phase-04.3's ~+20% drift experience and phase-05.3 / phase-06.x similar drift). Failure attribution localizes per sub-phase: 07.1's REVIEW.md owns the framework-foundation + HCM-integration + writer-arm-refactor verdict (and the regression-equivalence guarantee that all 12 existing fixtures stay green simultaneously); 07.2's REVIEW.md owns the HeaderMutation filter + fixture 0013 bilateral-assertion verdict + the parent-07 close. The split boundary is by deliverable cohesion (framework foundation + HCM integration → first concrete pluggable filter + fixture + parent close), not by code area: the framework crate and its consumer wiring are architecturally inseparable (07.1 ships both as one coherent slice with regression-equivalence as the differential proof); the first concrete filter and its bilateral fixture are the demonstration slice (07.2 proves the framework's iteration semantics are equivalent to Envoy's on both decode and encode sides). Sub-phase ordering invariant: 07.1 → 07.2 cannot be parallelized because (a) 07.2's HeaderMutation depends on the framework's `HttpFilterInstance` enum + `FilterPipeline` types landed in 07.1; (b) 07.2's HCM-engagement of the encode-side stamp requires the encode-side iteration site (the unified factored site at H1, the `finalize_h2_stream` refactor at H2) landed in 07.1; (c) 07.2 closes the parent-07 ROADMAP row.
- **Consequences:** parent SPEC at `docs/envoy-rust/phases/07-filter-chain-framework/SPEC.md` was committed at parent-07 state-1 (the predecessor of this commit, `7337f2c`) projecting this split per parent-SPEC §5 + §7 + §8 + §10; sub-phase SPECs (`07.1-filter-framework-foundation/SPEC.md`, `07.2-header-mutation-filter/SPEC.md`) land alongside this ADR in this same parent-07 state-2 commit. ROADMAP gains 2 new rows (`07.1`, `07.2`) at this commit; row `07`'s `sub-phases` column updates from `—` to `07.1, 07.2`; row `07`'s `status` remains `in-progress` (it flipped to `in-progress` at the parent-07 state-1 close-out commit `7337f2c` when STATE.md first pointed at phase 07 with lifecycle state 2). Sub-phases ship strictly in order (07.1 → 07.2) — they cannot be parallelized per the rationale above. Parent ROADMAP row `07` flips `done` at sub-phase 07.2's state-6 phase-done commit, mirroring phase-02's `f04e21a`-shape close-out, phase-03's `ca81226`-shape close-out, phase-04's `e626862`-shape close-out, phase-05's `82c26b8`-shape close-out, and phase-06's `b918f33`-shape close-out. STATE.md advances at this commit to point at `07.1` lifecycle state 1 (next-skill `superpowers:brainstorming` scoped to 07.1's surface per `SKILL_ROUTING.md` line 13 + `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §5 state 1). Phase 07's projected ADR ledger after this commit: ADR-0030 (this ADR; landed at this commit). **Conditional ADRs stay available:** ADR-0031 (foundations grant for `async_trait = "0.1"` or similar; not pre-projected; recommended posture is no foundations grants — D-3.2 lists *Filter chain engine (network + HTTP filter iteration protocol)* and *Every individual filter (network and HTTP)* on its *Must be written from scratch* list, so the iteration protocol + the HeaderMutation filter are hand-rolled atop existing `tokio` + `bytes` + `serde` + `tracing` + `thiserror` foundations; the framework's synchronous non-async iteration shape per parent-SPEC §6 Rule 5 avoids the `async_trait` foundations grant entirely on the MVP iteration states `decode_headers` + `encode_headers`); ADR-0032 (Cargo.lock cadence ratification, conditional on ADR-0031 actually landing — phase-04.1 REVIEW M5/M9 carries forward unchanged unless ADR-0031 forces a cadence pick). Each sub-phase's brainstorm may surface additional ADR-shaped decisions (e.g., the per-route `typed_per_filter_config` scaffolding decision at 07.1; an iteration-order edge case; the per-stream pipeline-clone shape at H2); numbers stay available, and each sub-phase's SPEC §7 projects its own ADRs at its brainstorm time.
- **Provenance:** this ADR was projected as the next-sequential available ADR number in parent-07 SPEC §7 (`docs/envoy-rust/phases/07-filter-chain-framework/SPEC.md`, committed at parent-07 state-1 — the predecessor of this commit `7337f2c`). The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0029** (landed at parent-06 state-1+state-2 combined-recovery commit `1f7661a` — parent-06 split decision); ADR-0030 lands at the next-sequential number with no renumbering needed (no inter-ADR landings between parent-06 close at `b918f33` and this commit; the 06.1 / 06.2 / 06.3 execution arc honored the no-foundations-grant posture per parent-06 SPEC §7 end-to-end, so neither conditional ADR-0030 nor conditional ADR-0031 from parent-06's projection actually landed — those numbers were released for later phase use and parent-07's split takes ADR-0030 as the next-sequential available number). Like phase-02's split (ADR-0013), phase-03's split (ADR-0017), phase-04's split (ADR-0020), phase-05's split (ADR-0022), and phase-06's split (ADR-0029), phase-07's projected ADR-0030 number matches the actual landed number; conditional ADR-0031 (foundations grant) and conditional ADR-0032 (Cargo.lock cadence) may not land — if no sub-phase ratifies a foundations grant, those numbers stay available for later phases per the established ledger discipline.
