## ADR-0031: Re-home filter-visible request/response types into `envoy-filter` to break the `envoy-filter` ↔ `envoy-http1` Cargo dependency cycle

- **Date:** 2026-05-12.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Parent-07 SPEC §5 signpost 7 and 07.1 PLAN architecture decision 7 specify the dep stack as `envoy-config → envoy-filter → envoy-http1, envoy-http2 → envoy-bin`, with the rationale: `envoy-filter` depends on `envoy-http1` for `codec::Request` / `Response` value-types (NOT HCM types); `envoy-http1` and `envoy-http2` both depend on `envoy-filter`; "no cycles because the codec module within envoy-http1 has no dependency on the hcm module." Tasks 1-3 implemented the first direction: `envoy-filter`'s `Cargo.toml` declares `envoy-http1 = { path = "../envoy-http1" }` (Task 1 commit `586de82`) and `pipeline.rs` / `instance.rs` / `router.rs` use `envoy_http1::{Request, Response}` in their public API (Tasks 2-3 commits `e43820e` / `2002f4e`). Task 6 (this prompt) prescribes adding the reverse direction: `envoy-filter = { path = "../envoy-filter" }` to `envoy-http1`'s `[dependencies]`. At the Task 6 dispatch checkpoint the parent session verified Cargo's behavior directly via a minimal two-crate workspace at `/tmp/cargo-cycle-min/` and confirmed the error:
  ```
  error: cyclic package dependency: package `a v0.1.0` depends on itself. Cycle:
  package `a` ... which satisfies path dependency `a` of package `b`
              ... which satisfies path dependency `b` of package `a`
  ```
  Cargo treats each workspace member as a single dependency-graph node; the SPEC's "no cycles" reasoning depends on module-level granularity (the `codec` module within `envoy-http1` indeed has no dependency on `hcm`), but Cargo does not split crates by module. The SPEC at line 884 (`docs/envoy-rust/phases/07.1-filter-framework-foundation/SPEC.md`) anticipates this exact pressure ("If a future surface forces a wider dependency (e.g., access-log integration at the filter level), the recommended path is to move `codec` into a smaller `envoy-codec` crate — flagged for whichever future phase first surfaces the pressure (out of scope for 07.1)") but punts it as future work; Cargo's rules make this resolution in-scope at 07.1 Task 6 — the cycle cannot land otherwise.
- **Options considered:**
  - **(i) Extract `Request` + `Response` from `envoy-http1` into a new `envoy-http-codec` workspace crate (or into `envoy-config`).** Both `envoy-filter` and `envoy-http1` then depend on the new crate. Cleanest long-term shape — matches the SPEC line 884 deferred suggestion. Rejected at 07.1 scope as too invasive: `envoy-http1::Request` carries `bytes_consumed: usize` (parser state) and `HttpVersion` (codec state) that are H1-specific noise to filter consumers; extracting both fields' homes plus migrating every existing consumer (the entire `envoy-http1` + `envoy-http2` HCM surface plus the in-process backstop tests) would span several hundred LoC of incidental churn unrelated to 07.1's primary deliverable.
  - **(ii) Make `envoy-filter`'s API generic via traits.** Define `trait FilterRequest { ... }` / `trait FilterResponse { ... }` in `envoy-filter`; `envoy_http1::Request` / `envoy_http1::Response` implement those traits. `Decision::StopAndSend(Box<dyn FilterResponse>)` or generic `Decision<R>`. Rejected — complicates the API surface, forces dynamic dispatch or generic parameters across all iteration methods, and `Decision::StopAndSend` needs a concrete return type that a filter can construct (which puts the type back into `envoy-filter` anyway).
  - **(iii) Define filter-visible subset types (`FilterRequest` + `FilterResponse`) locally in `envoy-filter::types`; the H1/H2 HCMs convert at the filter-invocation boundary.** Decision; chosen. `envoy-filter` loses its `envoy-http1` `[dependencies]` entry; `envoy-http1` gains an `envoy-filter` `[dependencies]` entry (the planned direction per SPEC signpost 7's stack). `FilterRequest` carries `{ method, path, headers, body }` (the 4 fields filters need; codec-state fields `version` and `bytes_consumed` stay at `envoy-http1`'s Request because they are not filter-visible). `FilterResponse` is identical in shape to `envoy_http1::Response` (`{ status, reason, headers, body }`) — the conversion is a 4-field structural copy at the boundary.
  - **(iv) Keep `envoy-filter` depending on `envoy-http1`; remove the Task 6 prescription and store the filter pipeline outside `envoy-http1` (e.g., in `envoy-bin`).** Rejected — the encode-side filter invocation at the unified factored site in `serve_connection` requires `envoy-http1` to *call* `pipeline.encode_headers` on a value owned by the HCM; without depending on `envoy-filter`, `envoy-http1` cannot name the type or call its methods. Trait-object indirection through a locally-defined trait in `envoy-http1` would work but recurses into option (ii)'s drawbacks.
- **Decision:** option (iii). Re-home filter-visible request/response shapes into `envoy-filter::types` per the table below. The HCMs at `envoy-http1` and `envoy-http2` convert their codec-native `Request` / `Response` to and from `FilterRequest` / `FilterResponse` at the filter-invocation boundary in `serve_connection` (H1) and `handle_one_stream` + `finalize_h2_stream` (H2).
  | Type | Before (07.1 Tasks 1-5) | After (this ADR / Task 5.5) |
  |---|---|---|
  | `envoy_http1::Request` | `{ method, path, version, headers, bytes_consumed, body }` | unchanged at `envoy_http1` |
  | `envoy_http1::Response` | `{ status, reason, headers, body }` | unchanged at `envoy_http1` |
  | `envoy_filter::FilterRequest` | (did not exist; used `envoy_http1::Request`) | new at `crates/envoy-filter/src/types.rs`: `{ method, path, headers, body: Option<Bytes> }` |
  | `envoy_filter::FilterResponse` | (did not exist; used `envoy_http1::Response`) | new at `crates/envoy-filter/src/types.rs`: `{ status, reason, headers, body: Bytes }` |
  | `Decision::StopAndSend(...)` payload type | `envoy_http1::Response` | `envoy_filter::FilterResponse` |
  | `envoy-filter/Cargo.toml` `[dependencies]` | `bytes`, `thiserror`, `tracing`, `envoy-config`, `envoy-http1` | `bytes`, `thiserror`, `tracing`, `envoy-config` (envoy-http1 removed) |
  | `envoy-http1/Cargo.toml` `[dependencies]` | (no `envoy-filter`) | gains `envoy-filter = { path = "../envoy-filter" }` (lands at Task 6, not this ADR commit) |
- **Rationale:** smallest scope that preserves the SPEC's stated stack direction (`envoy-filter` upstream of `envoy-http1` / `envoy-http2` per signpost 7) without the option-(i) full codec-types extraction. The conversion overhead at the HCM boundary is minimal: `FilterRequest::method` / `path` strings move via `std::mem::take` or `clone()` on small fixed-size strings; `headers: Vec<(String, String)>` moves via `mem::take`; `body: Option<Bytes>` moves via `Option::take` (Bytes is cheap reference-counted clone). The filter-visible subset shape is architecturally cleaner anyway — filters never need to see the parser's `bytes_consumed` offset or the H1 `HttpVersion` discriminator. Future H2/H3 codecs feed the same `FilterRequest` / `FilterResponse` shape without inheriting H1-specific fields. This ADR ratifies the same "codec types live below the filter framework" architectural intent as the SPEC's deferred option-(i) — just via type duplication at the filter-visible subset rather than full extraction. If a future phase introduces additional filter-visible fields that envoy-http1 also needs (e.g., a `trailers` field for HTTP/2), the natural path is to grow the same subset types in `envoy-filter::types`; full extraction (option (i)) is still available later if duplication becomes burdensome.
- **Consequences:**
  - `crates/envoy-filter/src/types.rs` lands at the Task 5.5 commit (this ADR commit) with `FilterRequest` + `FilterResponse` struct definitions; both `#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]`. `crates/envoy-filter/src/lib.rs` gains `pub mod types;` + `pub use types::{FilterRequest, FilterResponse};`.
  - `crates/envoy-filter/src/pipeline.rs` / `instance.rs` / `router.rs` migrate their imports from `use envoy_http1::{Request, Response};` to `use crate::types::{FilterRequest, FilterResponse};`. All signatures using `Request` / `Response` retype to `FilterRequest` / `FilterResponse`. Test code in `pipeline.rs` (the `test_request` / `test_response` helpers) and `router.rs` (the `decode_headers_returns_continue_*` / `encode_headers_returns_continue_*` tests) simplifies — the 6-field `Request` literal collapses to 4 fields (drops `version` + `bytes_consumed`); the 4-field `Response` literal stays the same. The `decode_headers_returns_continue_and_does_not_mutate_request` / `encode_headers_returns_continue_and_does_not_mutate_response` tests gain `PartialEq` on FilterRequest / FilterResponse so the assertion shape simplifies from per-field cloning to `let before = req.clone(); assert_eq!(req, before);`.
  - `crates/envoy-filter/Cargo.toml` removes the `envoy-http1 = { path = "../envoy-http1" }` line from `[dependencies]`. No other workspace changes at this commit.
  - **Task 6 (which lands AFTER this ADR commit) adds `envoy-filter = { path = "../envoy-filter" }` to `crates/envoy-http1/Cargo.toml`'s `[dependencies]`.** The cycle is now broken (one direction only: `envoy-http1` → `envoy-filter`). Task 6's HCM wiring constructs a `FilterRequest` from `&envoy_http1::Request` at the decode-side invocation site (cloning method / path strings and moving headers / body), invokes `pipeline.decode_headers(&mut filter_req)`, writes the mutated fields back into the codec-native `Request`. Symmetric at the encode side with `FilterResponse` ↔ `envoy_http1::Response`.
  - **Task 7 (H2 HCM) inherits the same boundary-conversion shape** — `envoy-http2`'s HCM converts `envoy_http2::Request` / `Response` to `FilterRequest` / `FilterResponse` at the filter-invocation boundary in `handle_one_stream` + `finalize_h2_stream`.
  - **07.2 (HeaderMutation) inherits the same shape unchanged.** HeaderMutationFilter sees `&mut FilterRequest` / `&mut FilterResponse` and mutates the `headers` field; the HCM's boundary-conversion writes the mutations back into the codec-native types.
  - **No new top-level Cargo deps.** The reorganization is workspace-internal; `Cargo.lock` regenerates with `envoy-filter`'s dep list shrinking by one (`envoy-http1` row removed).
  - **All 13 `envoy-filter` unit tests stay green** under the new types (verified at this commit). Workspace tests stay green (548 total).
  - **No changes to BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md, ROADMAP.md, or STATE.md.** STATE.md will advance only at Task 9 per the planned cadence.
  - **Conditional ADR-0032 (Cargo.lock cadence ratification — pre-projected at ADR-0030's Consequences footer)** stays available; this ADR adds no top-level Cargo deps so the conditional cadence ADR is not forced here.
  - **The original-projected conditional ADR-0031 slot at ADR-0030 Consequences ("foundations grant for `async_trait` or similar")** is reused for this cycle-resolution ADR. ADR-0030's footer reserved ADR-0031 as the next-available number for either a foundations grant or any execution-time-surfaced architectural decision; the recommended posture was "no foundations grants" — that posture continues to hold (this ADR is an architectural fix, not a foundations grant; no new top-level deps land). A future foundations-grant ADR (if 07.2 surfaces one) lands at ADR-0032 or beyond, per the established sequential-no-renumbering ledger discipline.
- **Provenance:** this ADR surfaced at the 07.1 Task 6 dispatch checkpoint when the parent session attempted to add `envoy-filter` as a dep of `envoy-http1` per the PLAN's Step 1 (PLAN.md:1896-1904) and verified via Cargo's `cyclic package dependency` error message in a minimal two-crate workspace at `/tmp/cargo-cycle-min/`. The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0030** (parent-07 split decision; landed at `6db5a01`). The intervening 07.1 commits (Task 1 `586de82`, Task 2 `e43820e`, Task 3 `2002f4e`, Task 4 `111237a`, Task 5 `b9dfa29`) did NOT land any ADRs — Task 1's `envoy-filter` Cargo.toml's `envoy-http1` path-dep was set up per the SPEC's stated direction with no cycle yet (envoy-http1 did not depend on envoy-filter). The cycle would have surfaced at Task 6's Step 1 regardless of who reached it first; landing this ADR + the cycle resolution as a Task 5.5 commit BEFORE Task 6's primary work is the cleanest cadence (Task 6's commit lands the filter-invocation wiring on top of a non-cyclic Cargo graph). The 07.1 PROGRESS.md Task 5.5 entry records the execution-time surfacing per D-3.5 append-only discipline.

## ADR-0032: Split phase 08 into sub-phases 08.1 and 08.2

- **Date:** 2026-05-15.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Phase 08 ("Minimum admin API (config_dump, stats, clusters, listeners, ready, server_info) + graceful drain") was projected as a single phase by `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §8 row 08 — the **last MVP-trunk phase**. The parent-08 state-1 brainstorm SPEC (`docs/envoy-rust/phases/08-admin-api-and-drain/SPEC.md`, committed at HEAD `0202e38`; the immediate predecessor of this commit) enumerated 17 deliverables (D1–D17) across:
  - **(a)** 06.1 admin carryforward closures D1/D2/D3 (REVIEW I2 `serialize_response` case-insensitive header dedupe + REVIEW M1 `reason_for_status` helper + REVIEW M4 `DRAIN_BUDGET` constant consolidated to `envoy-listener`); ~50 LoC + tests; named to phase 08 by the 06.1 / 06.2 / 06.3 / 07.2 STATE.md "Phase-06.1 rollovers" subsection.
  - **(b)** Admin endpoint surface D4–D10 (7 new endpoints): D4 `AdminEndpoint::dispatch(method, path)` refactor widening to a 3-variant `Dispatch::{Endpoint, NotFound, MethodNotAllowed{allow}}` enum with each variant carrying its method-allowlist + GET-side endpoints D5 `/server_info` + D6 `/config_dump` (includes `Bootstrap` `Serialize` derive cascade) + D7 `/clusters` + D8 `/listeners`, plus POST-side endpoints D9 `/drain_listeners` + D10 `/healthcheck/fail` + `/healthcheck/ok`; ~600-800 LoC + tests.
  - **(c)** Drain machinery D11–D13: `DrainState` module at `crates/envoy-listener/src/drain.rs` re-exported from `envoy-admin::DrainState` per the parent-08 SPEC §5.1 Cargo-cycle resolution (mirrors the M4 hoist + 05.3 / 07.1 cycle-resolution doctrine; no new ADR); listener accept-loop observation of `drain.drain_signal()` via a second `tokio::select!` arm; admin listener does NOT observe its own `drain_signal` (operator-reachability invariant); `envoy-bin::main` shared-handle wiring threading `Arc<Bootstrap>` + `Arc<ClusterManager>` + `Arc<DrainState>` + `Arc<Instant>` through `AdminHandler::new`; ~350 LoC + tests.
  - **(d)** Stats wiring D14: `server.live` + `server.state` + `listener_manager.total_listeners_active` gauges with inline-at-state-transition updates (one source of truth, no polling task) + RAII guard for the listener-active gauge; ~80 LoC + tests.
  - **(e)** Harness extensions D15 (`BodyRule::JsonShape` + `BodyRule::TextLines`) + D16 (`Driver::AdminScrape` `pre_admin_actions: Vec<AdminAction>` + `post_admin_assertions: Vec<AdminAssertion>` extension, with `AdminAssertion::DataPlaneConnectionRefused { listener_address, within: Duration }` covering the fixture-0015 drain assertion); ~200 LoC.
  - **(f)** Fixtures D17.1 (`0014-admin-config-dump-server-info` — reuses fixture 0008's HCM + STRICT_DNS + 1-listener shape; four GET-endpoint sub-cases) + D17.2 (`0015-admin-drain-listeners` — reuses fixture 0007's HCM + direct_response shape; drain-sequence assertion) + D17.3 fuzz-corpus seed extensions + D17.4 in-process backstops; ~300-400 LoC.

  Combined scope across all 17 deliverables totals **~1500-1800 LoC** and **~21-23 tasks** for a single-phase 08-everything shipment. Per `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §6.1 the split-gate fires when EITHER `~25 tasks` OR `~1500 LoC` is exceeded. The LoC gate is at or above the threshold by SPEC-time projection (1500 baseline; 1500-1800 projected — i.e., 0-20% over before any execution-time drift adjustment); applying the parent-04.3 / parent-05.3 ~+20% drift adjustment projects 1800-2160 LoC, decisively over the cap. The task-count gate is under (21-23 vs ~25 cap), but EITHER gate is sufficient to trigger the split. The parent-08 SPEC §6.1 anticipated this exact pressure ("the phase is close to or at the split-gate threshold; the state-2 PLAN-writer should evaluate concretely and split if needed") and explicitly recommended the 08.1 / 08.2 split shape ratified here.
- **Options considered:**
  - **(i) Single phase.** Rejected — the LoC gate fires at projection time before any drift; with drift the projection lands decisively over the cap. Single-phase shipment historically splits under mid-execution pressure (§6.1's in-flight trigger when any single task's sub-steps blow past ~10 items), which is strictly worse than splitting at state 2. The parent-08 SPEC §6.1 explicitly recommended a split.
  - **(ii) Three-way split** (08.α = carryforward closures + dispatch refactor; 08.β = endpoint surface + fixture 0014; 08.γ = drain machinery + fixture 0015 + parent close). Rejected — the carryforward-closure slice is too small to motivate a full state-machine cycle (3 deliverables, ~50 LoC); the natural pairing is with the dispatch refactor + endpoint surface (08.1 of the chosen 2-way split absorbs both as a coherent foundation slice). Three-way split would scatter the admin-endpoint review surface across two sub-phases for no boundary-coherence benefit.
  - **(iii) Two-way split by traffic direction** (08.α = GET endpoints; 08.β = POST endpoints + drain). Rejected — POST endpoints (D9/D10) without their drain integration (D11/D12) are effect-only-empty-renders with no behavior to test; landing them apart from D11/D12 yields a sub-phase with no differential surface. The drain machinery (D11/D12/D13) and the POST endpoints (D9/D10) are architecturally coupled — they cannot be split.
  - **(iv) Two-way split by deliverable cohesion: endpoint surface + carryforward closures (08.1) → drain machinery + parent close (08.2).** Chosen. 08.1 lands a coherent admin-endpoint surface (4 new GET endpoints + dispatch refactor + carryforward closures + fixture 0014 demonstrating the endpoint shapes bilaterally) with no dependency on drain types. 08.2 lands the drain machinery slice as a self-contained extension (DrainState module → POST endpoints consuming it → listener observation → fixture 0015 demonstrating the wire-level drain effect → parent close-out closing the MVP trunk).
  - **(v) Nested split of 08.1 OR 08.2** (e.g., 08 → 08.1 → 08.1.1 / 08.1.2; 08 → 08.2). Rejected — three-level numbering is awkward (`08.1.1`); BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md §6.1 explicitly flags nested splits of an already-split sub-phase as suspicious and prescribes `superpowers:systematic-debugging` first. Each sub-phase fits comfortably under the §6.1 gates per the per-SPEC §6.1 projections (08.1 ~1080-1180 LoC / ~12-14 tasks; 08.2 ~810-920 LoC / ~10-13 tasks).
- **Decision:** split phase 08 into two sub-phases by deliverable cohesion, mirroring phase-02's ADR-0013 2-way precedent, phase-03's ADR-0017 2-way precedent, and phase-07's ADR-0030 2-way precedent:
  - **08.1 (`08.1-admin-endpoint-surface`)** — admin endpoint surface foundation slice + carryforward closures + fixture 0014. Deliverables: D1 + D2 + D3 (Task-1 preamble; 06.1 REVIEW I2 + M1 + M4 carryforward closures) + D4 (`AdminEndpoint::dispatch(method, path) -> Dispatch{Endpoint|NotFound|MethodNotAllowed{allow}}` refactor; each variant declares its method-allowlist; closes 06.1 M1 as a side effect) + D5 (`/server_info` — 08.1 emits `state: "LIVE"` as a literal placeholder; 08.2's D5e patches the source to `DrainState::current()`; structural shape unchanged) + D6 (`/config_dump` — includes `Bootstrap` `Serialize` derive cascade per parent-08 SPEC §6.4 with a YAML→struct→JSON→struct sanity-check as pre-D6 sub-task) + D7 (`/clusters` reading from `Arc<ClusterManager>`) + D8 (`/listeners` reading from `Arc<Bootstrap>`) + D13a (`envoy-bin::main` shared-handle wiring partial — three of four handles: `Arc<Bootstrap>` + `Arc<ClusterManager>` + `Arc<Instant>`; D13b adds the fourth `Arc<DrainState>` at 08.2) + D15 (`BodyRule::JsonShape` + `BodyRule::TextLines` harness rules) + D17.1 (fixture `0014-admin-config-dump-server-info`) + D17.3a (`admin_multi_endpoint_bootstrap.yaml` fuzz seed) + D17.4a (`admin_config_dump_server_info.rs` in-process backstop). BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT extensions: new "Admin endpoint body shapes" subsection (4 of 7 rows, GET endpoints only) + header allow-list dedupe note. **08.1 wires ZERO new stats** (the parent-08 D14 stats all require `DrainState` or listener-observation hooks, both at 08.2). **Conditional ADR-0033 (foundations grant for `/config_dump` proto-JSON shape)** stays reserved-available; recommended posture is no-grant per parent-08 SPEC §5.3. ~1080-1180 LoC, ~12-14 tasks. Depends on `07` (done as of `1d52156`).
  - **08.2 (`08.2-endpoint-triggered-drain`)** — drain machinery slice + fixture 0015 + parent-08 close-out + MVP-trunk close. Deliverables: D11 (`DrainState` module at `crates/envoy-listener/src/drain.rs` re-exported from `envoy-admin::DrainState`; `DrainStage::{Live, HealthcheckFailing, Draining}` enum over `AtomicU8`; `tokio::sync::Notify` for `drain_signal()`; sticky-drain semantic where `ok_healthcheck()` after `drain()` is no-op) + D9 (`/drain_listeners` POST endpoint invoking `DrainState::drain()`) + D10 (`/healthcheck/fail` + `/healthcheck/ok` POST endpoints invoking `DrainState::fail_healthcheck()` / `ok_healthcheck()`) + D12 (listener accept-loop observation of `drain.drain_signal()` via `tokio::select!`; admin listener does NOT observe its own `drain_signal` — operator-reachability invariant per parent-08 SPEC §5.5) + D13b (`envoy-bin::main` `Arc<DrainState>` wiring; widens `AdminHandler::new` signature from 08.1's 5-arg shape to 6-arg) + D5e (`/server_info` `state`-field value-source patch: from 08.1's literal `"LIVE"` to `match drain.current() { Live | HealthcheckFailing => "LIVE", Draining => "DRAINING" }`; pure value-binding swap, no struct-shape change) + D-ready (`/ready` drain-aware response: extends 06.1's 200-LIVE-only path with 503 DRAINING when `DrainState::current() == Draining`; body `"DRAINING\n"`) + D14 (three new gauges `server.live` + `server.state` + `listener_manager.total_listeners_active`; inline-at-state-transition updates for the first two; RAII guard for the third) + D16 (`Driver::AdminScrape` `pre_admin_actions: Vec<AdminAction>` + `post_admin_assertions: Vec<AdminAssertion>` extension; `AdminAssertion::DataPlaneConnectionRefused { listener_address, within: Duration }`) + D17.2 (fixture `0015-admin-drain-listeners`) + D17.3b (`admin_healthcheck_bootstrap.yaml` fuzz seed) + D17.4b (`admin_drain_listeners.rs` in-process backstop) + parent-08 ROADMAP-row close-out (the closing-sub-phase invariant: 08.2's state-6 commit flips parent row `08` `in-progress` → `done` in addition to its own row) + MVP-trunk close narrative in STATE.md. BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT extensions: 3 more "Admin endpoint body shapes" rows (POST endpoints) + 3 new "Stat-name mapping" rows (`server.live` + `server.state` + `listener_manager.total_listeners_active`) + new "Admin-action effect equivalence" subsection. ~810-920 LoC, ~10-13 tasks. Depends on `08.1`.
- **Rationale:** the 2-way flat split mirrors phase-02's `1c38ca9`-shape precedent (under ADR-0013), phase-03's `8b75aaf`-shape precedent (under ADR-0017), and phase-07's `6db5a01`-shape precedent (under ADR-0030); this is the fourth 2-way split in the project (phases 02 / 03 / 07 / 08 each split 2-way; phases 04 / 05 / 06 each split 3-way; phases 00 / 01 did not split). Each of the two sub-phases fits comfortably under the §6.1 gates with healthy headroom (~25-30% LoC headroom on 08.1; ~40% on 08.2) against likely execution-time drift (per phase-04.3's / phase-05.3's / phase-06.x's / phase-07.x's ~+20% drift). Failure attribution localizes per sub-phase: 08.1's REVIEW.md owns the admin endpoint surface verdict (4 new GET endpoints; carryforward closures; dispatch refactor; fixture 0014 bilateral assertion); 08.2's REVIEW.md owns the drain machinery verdict + fixture 0015 wire-level-drain assertion + parent-08 close-out. The split boundary is by deliverable cohesion (endpoint surface foundation → drain machinery integration), NOT by traffic direction (which doesn't apply to admin) and NOT by code area: the endpoint surface and the drain machinery are architecturally separable (the endpoint surface has zero dependency on drain types; the drain machinery requires the endpoint surface as a delivery vehicle for its POST endpoints). Sub-phase ordering invariant: 08.1 → 08.2 cannot be parallelized because (a) 08.2's D9/D10 add new `AdminEndpoint` variants consuming 08.1's `Dispatch` enum; (b) 08.2's D5e patches the value source on 08.1's `ServerInfoBody`; (c) 08.2's D13b widens 08.1's `AdminHandler::new` signature; (d) 08.2 closes the parent-08 ROADMAP row.
- **Consequences:** parent SPEC at `docs/envoy-rust/phases/08-admin-api-and-drain/SPEC.md` was committed at parent-08 state-1 (the predecessor of this commit, `0202e38`) projecting this split per parent-SPEC §6.1 + §7 + §8; sub-phase SPECs (`08.1-admin-endpoint-surface/SPEC.md`, `08.2-endpoint-triggered-drain/SPEC.md`) land alongside this ADR in this same parent-08 state-2 split commit. ROADMAP gains 2 new rows (`08.1`, `08.2`) at this commit; row `08`'s `sub-phases` column updates from `—` to `08.1, 08.2`; row `08`'s `status` remains `in-progress` (it flipped to `in-progress` at the parent-08 state-1 close-out commit `0202e38` when STATE.md first pointed at phase 08 with lifecycle state 2). Sub-phases ship strictly in order (08.1 → 08.2) — they cannot be parallelized per the rationale above. Parent ROADMAP row `08` flips `done` at sub-phase 08.2's state-6 phase-done commit, mirroring phase-02's `f04e21a`-shape close-out, phase-03's `ca81226`-shape close-out, phase-04's `e626862`-shape close-out, phase-05's `82c26b8`-shape close-out, phase-06's `b918f33`-shape close-out, and phase-07's `1d52156`-shape close-out. STATE.md advances at this commit to point at `08.1` lifecycle state 2 (next-skill `superpowers:writing-plans` scoped to the 08.1 SPEC) — the parent state-2 split commit lands each sub-phase's SPEC and advances STATE.md to the first sub-phase's state 2 directly (skipping 08.1 state 1 brainstorm) because this parent-08 SPEC already brainstormed the full surface and the sub-phase SPECs are surface-slice carve-outs derived from it (mirrors the parent-07 state-2 split commit `6db5a01` precedent + the parent-06 state-1+state-2 combined-recovery commit `1f7661a` precedent). **Phase 08's projected ADR ledger after this commit:** ADR-0032 (this ADR; landed at this commit). **Conditional ADR-0033 stays available** at 08.1 state 3 for the foundations-grant slot named in parent-08 SPEC §7 (e.g., `prost-reflect` for proto-JSON `/config_dump` shape if envoy-rust's `serde_json` rendering surfaces a materially-worse-than-foundation divergence at execution time); recommended posture per parent-08 SPEC §5.3 is no-grant. Each sub-phase's brainstorm or PLAN may surface additional ADR-shaped decisions (a state-transition CAS edge case, a notify-once consensus, a harness assertion-semantics decision); numbers stay available, and each sub-phase's SPEC §7 projects its own ADRs at its state-2 PLAN-write time.
- **Provenance:** this ADR was projected as the next-sequential available ADR number in parent-08 SPEC §7 ("Conditional ADR-0032 — phase-08 split decision. Lands at the state-2 split commit if the §6.1 split-gate fires per §6.1 of this SPEC. Mirrors ADR-0020 / ADR-0022 / ADR-0029 / ADR-0030 — same shape, same provenance."). The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0031** (landed at the 07.1 Task 5.5 commit `8161990` — re-home filter-visible request/response types into `envoy-filter::types` to break the `envoy-filter` ↔ `envoy-http1` Cargo cycle); ADR-0032 lands at the next-sequential number with no renumbering needed (no inter-ADR landings between parent-07 close at `1d52156` and this commit — the 07.2 execution arc honored the no-foundations-grant posture per parent-07 SPEC §7 + 07.2 SPEC §7 end-to-end, so neither conditional ADR-0032 nor any other ADR landed during 07.2). Like phase-02's split (ADR-0013), phase-03's split (ADR-0017), phase-04's split (ADR-0020), phase-05's split (ADR-0022), phase-06's split (ADR-0029), and phase-07's split (ADR-0030), phase-08's projected ADR-0032 number matches the actual landed number with no renumbering needed; conditional ADR-0033 (foundations grant for `/config_dump` proto-JSON shape) may not land — if neither 08.1 nor 08.2 ratifies a foundations grant, that number stays available for the post-MVP feature-family phases per the established ledger discipline.

## ADR-0033: Phase-09 SPEC §2.2 revision per upstream Envoy v1.33 empirical observation (drop `x-envoy-ratelimited` injection; align 429 body + H1 HCM filter-synth header decoration)

- **Date:** 2026-05-17.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Phase 09 (`09-http-filter-local-rate-limit`) state-3 Task 5 dispatch (the Docker-gated differential fixture authoring step) surfaced three empirical discrepancies between the phase-09 SPEC §2.2 + Task 3 lock-in #13 (both authored at state 1 / state 2 without Docker-level empirical verification) and upstream Envoy v1.33's actual `envoy.filters.http.local_ratelimit` wire-level behavior:

  1. **`x-envoy-ratelimited` header is NOT emitted by upstream Envoy v1.33's `envoy.filters.http.local_ratelimit` filter.** Empirical run with `envoyproxy/envoy:v1.33.0` + canonical bootstrap (`max_tokens: 3, tokens_per_fill: 3, fill_interval: 60s, status: { code: 429 }, filter_enabled: { default_value: { numerator: 100, denominator: HUNDRED } }, filter_enforced: { default_value: { numerator: 100, denominator: HUNDRED } }`) under both the subagent's investigation and the controller's independent Docker verification yielded 429 responses with exactly four headers: `content-length`, `content-type`, `date`, `server`. The `x-envoy-ratelimited` header is absent. The SPEC §2.2 claim ("Upstream Envoy v1.33's `envoy.filters.http.local_ratelimit` auto-injects this header on every rate-limited response per the documented semantic") is factually incorrect. Per the upstream Envoy v1.33 source, `x-envoy-ratelimited` is emitted by the GLOBAL ratelimit filter (`envoy.filters.http.ratelimit`) and by router-side response-flag handling on the `RateLimited` response flag — neither of which the local_ratelimit filter sets or triggers in v1.33.

  2. **Upstream Envoy v1.33's local_ratelimit 429 response body is the source-hardcoded string `"local_rate_limited"` (18 bytes), not empty.** Empirical observation: `content-length: 18` + body bytes `6c 6f 63 61 6c 5f 72 61 74 65 5f 6c 69 6d 69 74 65 64` (= "local_rate_limited"). envoy-rust per Task 3 lock-in #13 emits `body: Bytes::new()` (0 bytes). The upstream proto `envoy.extensions.filters.http.local_ratelimit.v3.LocalRateLimit` has no configurable `response_body` field; the body is source-hardcoded. SPEC §2.2's implied empty-body posture (the Task 3 lock-in #13 `body: Bytes::new()`) is incorrect for bilateral parity.

  3. **envoy-rust's H1 HCM filter-StopAndSend writer-path skips standard-header decoration.** Empirical observation of envoy-rust at HEAD `78128f4` (Task 4 commit) with the canonical bootstrap yielded 429 responses with ONLY the filter-injected `x-envoy-ratelimited: true` header. The standard HTTP/1.1 response headers (`server`, `date`, `content-length`, `content-type`, `connection`) — which the synth-from-build path emits via `synth_status` at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:866-887` — are NOT decorated onto filter-synth responses. The 07.1-landed H1 HCM writer-arm match at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:371-379` (`RequestPath::SynthFromDecode`) and its encode-side symmetric arm (around line 577) take `filter_resp.headers` verbatim with no standard-header augmentation. Phase 07.2's HeaderMutation filter never emitted `StopAndSend` with an under-specified header set (HeaderMutation's `decode_headers` returns `Decision::Continue` unconditionally — the filter only mutates header lists, never short-circuits), so the gap went unobserved at 07.x. Phase 09's LocalRateLimit is the first filter to short-circuit via `StopAndSend` on the production path with a sparse header list, surfacing this latent framework defect.

- **Options considered:**

  - **(i) Accept upstream Envoy v1.33 as the wire contract; do nothing on the envoy-rust side; let fixture 0016 fail.** Rejected — the differential fixture 0016 must land green for phase-09's state-4 phase-done gate per `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §7.5(a). Doing nothing blocks the phase indefinitely.

  - **(ii) Land fixture 0016 with per-fixture allow-list entries that tolerate the divergences.** Add `allowlist_envoy_rust_only` for `x-envoy-ratelimited` + a paired body-rule that tolerates `"" vs "local_rate_limited"`. Rejected per D-3.3 ("differential correctness beats internal fidelity") — accumulating per-fixture allow-list cruft for behavior that should match upstream parity is the anti-pattern the doctrine warns against. The 06.1 Prometheus exposition allow-list is documented as a TEMPORARY divergence pending a follow-up phase that adds a `StatsTagExtractor`-equivalent (per `BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md` `Prometheus exposition shape divergence` note); this option (ii) would establish a NEW indefinite-duration divergence with no follow-up close site, which is strictly worse than option (iii) below.

  - **(iii) Align envoy-rust with upstream Envoy v1.33's actual local_ratelimit wire-level behavior; revise SPEC §2.2 + Task 3 lock-in #13 accordingly; fix the H1 HCM writer-path standard-header decoration gap at the same time.** Chosen. The work splits into four corrective commits (this ADR + three implementation fixups):
    - **(a)** This ADR + SPEC §2.2 revision (docs-only).
    - **(b)** Task 3 runtime fixup: drop the `("x-envoy-ratelimited", "true")` injection from `LocalRateLimitFilter::decode_headers`; change `body: Bytes::new()` → `body: Bytes::from_static(b"local_rate_limited")`; update Task 3 unit tests; preserve the `response_headers_to_add` operator-configurable header plumbing.
    - **(c)** Task 4 H1 HCM fixup: add `decorate_filter_synth_response(resp: &mut Response, close: bool)` helper, called from both `RequestPath::SynthFromDecode` and `RequestPath::SynthFromEncode` writer-arm sites; the helper conditionally adds the 5 standard headers (`server`, `date`, `content-length`, `content-type`, `connection`) if the filter's response doesn't already provide them by name (case-insensitive name check matching the 06.1 D1 + 08.1 D1 dedupe precedent at `crates/envoy-admin/src/handler.rs::serialize_response`); `content-length` is ALWAYS set from `body.len()` (the filter's body is the source of truth, never the filter's header line if present); `content-type` default is `text/plain` (matching `synth_status`'s default at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:866-887`); the helper sets up the H1 HCM symmetric to `synth_status`'s standard-header emission for synth-from-build responses.
    - **(d)** Task 5 re-attempt: fixture 0016 + Docker-gated wrapper per the revised SPEC §2.2; expectations.yaml asserts per-probe statuses `[200, 200, 200, 429, 429]` + body `"local_rate_limited"` (18 bytes) on 429 probes + `set_equal_modulo_allow_list` headers (no `x-envoy-ratelimited` allow-list entry needed because both proxies now omit it; the existing 04.1 `server` + `date` allow-list rows cover the cross-proxy implementation-identifying differences).

  - **(iv) Drop fixture 0016 entirely; re-scope phase 09 to filter-runtime-only without a differential fixture.** Rejected — the differential fixture is the load-bearing acceptance signal for phase 09 per SPEC §1 + `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §7 (the differential test contract is the project's core verification discipline). Dropping it would leave the LocalRateLimit filter unverified bilaterally and forfeit the phase's primary mission contribution.

  - **(v) Split phase 09 into 09.1 (envoy-config + filter runtime + H1 HCM fixup) and 09.2 (fixture + backstop + state-4).** Rejected — `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §6.1 splits at state 2 (PLAN-write time) when the PLAN exceeds ~25 tasks OR ~1500 LoC; mid-execution splits at §6.1's in-flight trigger when any single task's sub-steps blow past ~10 items. Neither condition fires: the 4-commit corrective fixup adds ~150-250 LoC + ~5-10 sub-tasks beyond the original 8-task PLAN, comfortably under the gate. The corrective work is per parent-08 SPEC §6.1 alternative (vi) "accept-drift discipline" — record the deviation in PROGRESS, dispatch the additional commits inline, do not nest-split.

- **Decision:** Adopt option (iii). Land four corrective commits at the phase-09 state-3 cadence:

  - **Commit A** (this commit): ADR-0033 + SPEC §2.2 revision + PROGRESS Task 5 preamble note. Docs-only. `phase 09: ADR-0033 + SPEC §2.2 revision per upstream Envoy v1.33 empirical observation [ADR-0033]`.

  - **Commit B**: Task 3 runtime fixup. Files: `crates/envoy-filter/src/local_rate_limit.rs` (drop `x-envoy-ratelimited` injection; change body to `Bytes::from_static(b"local_rate_limited")`; update 3 affected tests — `decode_headers_rate_limits_after_max_tokens_and_increments_rate_limited_enforced` drops the `x-envoy-ratelimited` assertion and adds the body assertion; `decode_headers_appends_configured_response_headers` drops the `x-envoy-ratelimited` assertion); `docs/envoy-rust/phases/09-http-filter-local-rate-limit/PROGRESS.md` (append `### Task 3 fixup — upstream Envoy v1.33 parity per ADR-0033` subsection). `phase 09: task 3 fixup — drop x-envoy-ratelimited + body "local_rate_limited" per ADR-0033`.

  - **Commit C**: Task 4 H1 HCM fixup. Files: `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs` (new `decorate_filter_synth_response` helper + call site updates at both SynthFromDecode and SynthFromEncode arms + 1-2 new unit tests covering the decoration); `docs/envoy-rust/phases/09-http-filter-local-rate-limit/PROGRESS.md` (append `### Task 4 fixup — H1 HCM filter-synth header decoration per ADR-0033` subsection). `phase 09: task 4 fixup — H1 HCM decorates filter-synth responses with standard headers per ADR-0033`.

  - **Commit D**: Task 5 (re-attempt). Files: `tests/fixtures/0016-http-filter-local-rate-limit/{envoy.yaml,envoy-rust.yaml,expectations.yaml,README.md}`; `tests/differential/tests/http_filter_local_rate_limit.rs`; `docs/envoy-rust/phases/09-http-filter-local-rate-limit/PROGRESS.md` (populated `### Task 5` subsection). **NO `docs/envoy-rust/BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md` change** at this commit (the `x-envoy-ratelimited` Header allow-list row PLAN lock-in #30 projected is voided per ADR-0033; the 4 Stat-name mapping rows already landed at Task 3 commit `70bad43`). `phase 09: task 5 — D8.1 fixture 0016 + Docker-gated wrapper`.

  PLAN lock-in #30 (`x-envoy-ratelimited` BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT row Lands at Task 5) becomes a no-op per ADR-0033 — the row was never appended to BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md. PLAN lock-in #13 (429 synth response shape with `("x-envoy-ratelimited", "true")` header + empty `Bytes::new()` body) is voided and replaced by ADR-0033's revised contract: no `x-envoy-ratelimited` header + `Bytes::from_static(b"local_rate_limited")` body + 5 standard headers via the H1 HCM `decorate_filter_synth_response` helper. PLAN lock-in #33 (in-process backstop at Task 7 with direct `x-envoy-ratelimited` per-header presence assertion) defers to Task 7 dispatch — the Task 7 assertion shape drops `x-envoy-ratelimited` per-header presence in favor of body-content + standard-header presence assertions (Task 7 PROGRESS records the lock-in #33 deviation).

- **Rationale:** D-3.3 "differential correctness beats internal fidelity" mandates that envoy-rust match upstream Envoy's wire-level output as the canonical contract. Empirical run-time evidence (Docker + curl + observed headers + observed body bytes) is authoritative — the SPEC §2.2 claim (written at state 1 without empirical verification) is superseded. The H1 HCM writer-path gap (Blocker #3) is a genuine framework-level defect that 07.x's HeaderMutation filter never exposed because HeaderMutation never short-circuited via `StopAndSend`; phase 09's LocalRateLimit is the first such filter and surfaces the latent bug. Fixing it at Commit C keeps the doctrinal posture of "framework primitives are written from scratch per D-3.2" intact while honoring D-3.6 "every phase is a green build" — the `decorate_filter_synth_response` helper is one new function (~30 LoC) + one new test (~30 LoC), well within the parent-08 SPEC §6.1 alternative (vi) accept-drift posture. The 4-commit corrective sequence preserves per-commit reviewability (each commit has one logical concern; spec + code reviewers can verify in isolation). PLAN lock-in #36 (single-phase no-split posture) is upheld — the corrective work fits comfortably under the §6.1 ~25-task / ~1500-LoC gate even with the +150-250 LoC drift. The state-3 cadence (one-task-per-subagent-dispatch with two-stage review) continues unchanged for Commits B, C, and D.

- **Consequences:** SPEC §2.2 (`docs/envoy-rust/phases/09-http-filter-local-rate-limit/SPEC.md` lines 55-63) ratifies the revised contract for phase 09 going forward — the row table is replaced with prose explaining the empirical discovery + the upstream-parity disposition. The PLAN.md lock-in table at §2 stays unchanged at this commit (it records the state-2 lock-ins as they were at PLAN-write time; the PROGRESS Task 3/4/5 subsections record the deviations per the standard cadence). PROGRESS Task 1 preamble's carryforward dispositions table gains no new entry (07.2 REVIEW M1 still closes at Task 4 per original disposition — unaffected). DECISIONS.md ledger head advances `ADR-0032 → ADR-0033`. The fixture-0016 Docker-gated landing path is unblocked. Future HTTP-filter-family phases adding filters that emit `Decision::StopAndSend(FilterResponse)` (e.g., fault, ext_authz, oauth2, rbac) benefit from the H1 HCM standard-header decoration introduced at Commit C; the corresponding H2 HCM path naturally inherits via the shared Http1HCMConfig re-export per the PLAN-write SPEC correction #2 wiring discipline. **Conditional ADR-0034** (foundations grant for the token-bucket primitive per phase-09 SPEC §7) stays reserved-available and unused; recommended posture remains no-grant. The phase-09 ADR ledger after this commit is `ADR-0032 → ADR-0033`; ADR-0034 stays available for the post-MVP feature-family phases per the established ledger discipline if neither phase-09 nor a subsequent phase ratifies a foundations grant.

**Phase-10 amendment (closes 09 REVIEW M2 per preferred close shape (a)):** Empirical re-verification at the phase-10 state-2 PLAN-write commit confirms the H2 HCM filter-synth writer path at `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs:373-378` (decode-side `H2RequestPath::SynthFromDecode`) and `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs:436-443` (encode-side `Decision::StopAndSend(replacement)`) return the filter's response verbatim through `build_http_response` at `crates/envoy-http2/src/response.rs:29-50`, which does NOT add `server`/`date`/`content-type`. The "naturally inherits via the shared Http1HCMConfig re-export" claim above is INCORRECT for the H2 writer path — the H2 HCM has its own `build_http_response` helper that is symmetric to H1's `synth_status` but does not include the standard-header decoration that the H1 `decorate_filter_synth_response` helper adds. The H2 analogous gap is known-deferred. The close site is "next HTTP-filter-family phase exercising filters bilaterally on H2 (the H2 HCM `decorate_filter_synth_response_h2` analogue lands as a ~50-70 LoC + 2-test follow-up at that phase)." Phase 10's RBAC fixture exercises H1 only (matching the 07.2 + 09 single-codec-fixture cadence); the empirical RBAC 403 response correctly carries the 5 standard headers via the H1 helper.

- **Provenance:** This ADR was discovered (not projected) at the phase-09 Task 5 subagent dispatch on `2026-05-17` against HEAD `78128f4` (Task 4 commit; CI green at run `26002010216`). The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0032** (landed at the parent-08 state-2 split commit `56dee82`); ADR-0033 lands at the next-sequential number with no renumbering. The subagent's empirical evidence (Docker-based runs of upstream Envoy v1.33 against the canonical bootstrap YAML; observed 429 response headers + body bytes; observed envoy-rust 429 response missing standard headers) was independently verified by the controller via direct Docker probes against `envoyproxy/envoy:v1.33.0` before this ADR was authored (controller's verification: probes 1-2 yielded 200 with body "ok\n"; probes 3-5 yielded 429 with `{content-length: 18, content-type: text/plain, date: ..., server: envoy}` + body bytes hex-dump `6c 6f 63 61 6c 5f 72 61 74 65 5f 6c 69 6d 69 74 65 64` confirming `"local_rate_limited"`). The phase-09 state-1 SPEC was authored without empirical Docker verification of the upstream local_ratelimit wire shape — a process gap that this ADR ratifies as an accept-drift discipline for future SPEC authoring (state-1 brainstorming should empirically verify upstream wire shapes for novel filter surfaces before locking in BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT projections, but no doctrine-level enforcement is introduced here — the empirical-discovery-at-Task-5 → ADR-at-state-3 path is a viable correction route per D-3.5 "Decisions are written, not remembered").

## ADR-0034: Phase-10 SPEC §2.2 + §3 D8.1 + §5.9 revision per upstream Envoy v1.33 empirical observation (403 body bytes `"RBAC: access denied"` 19 bytes; no trailing newline)

- **Date:** 2026-05-18.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Phase 10 (`10-http-filter-rbac`) state-2 PLAN-write performed the §6.2 empirical verification per SPEC §7 option A recommended posture + the ADR-0033 process-gap-awareness doctrine (which calls for state-2 PLAN-write to verify upstream wire shapes before locking PLAN lock-ins, AVOIDING the phase-09 process gap that surfaced ADR-0033 only at Task 5 subagent dispatch). The verification ran `envoyproxy/envoy:v1.33.0` Docker against the SPEC §3 D8.1 canonical bootstrap (HCM + envoy.filters.http.rbac + envoy.filters.http.router + direct_response action; 1 ALLOW policy `pass_with_header` with `permissions: [- any: true]` + `principals: [- header: { name: x-rbac-pass, string_match: { exact: yes } }]`). 3 projections were verified:

  1. **Stats namespace shape: MATCHES SPEC §2.1 projection.** Empirical scrape post-allowed + post-denied probe: `http.ingress_http.rbac.allowed: 1`, `http.ingress_http.rbac.denied: 1`, `http.ingress_http.rbac.shadow_allowed: 0`, `http.ingress_http.rbac.shadow_denied: 0`. The 2 primary counters match SPEC §2.1's `http.<hcm_stat_prefix>.rbac.{allowed,denied}` projection exactly. The 2 additional `shadow_*` counters at 0 are emitted unconditionally by upstream Envoy v1.33 even when `shadow_rules` is unconfigured — phase-10 registers only the 2 primary counters since `shadow_rules` defers per SPEC §4 (the differential fixture does NOT scrape RBAC stats so the 2-vs-4 stat-name-set divergence is not exercised bilaterally). **No SPEC revision needed; no PLAN lock-in voided.**

  2. **403 response body bytes: DIFFERS by 1 byte from SPEC §2.2 + §3 D8.1 + §5.9 projection.** Empirical body bytes (under both default keep-alive curl framing AND harness-shape `Connection: close` request framing — identical body):
     - `content-length: 19`
     - body bytes (hex): `52 42 41 43 3a 20 61 63 63 65 73 73 20 64 65 6e 69 65 64`
     - body string: `"RBAC: access denied"` (19 bytes, NO trailing newline)

     The SPEC's 20-byte projection (`"RBAC: access denied\n"` with trailing newline) is incorrect. Per the upstream Envoy v1.33 source, the body is source-hardcoded and emitted verbatim by the `envoy.extensions.filters.http.rbac.v3.RBAC` filter without trailing newline. **MATERIAL DIFFERENCE** — 1-byte projection error.

  3. **403 response header set (under harness-shape `Connection: close` request framing): MATCHES SPEC §2.2 projection.** Empirical header set: `{content-length: 19, content-type: text/plain, date: ..., server: envoy, connection: close}` (5 headers — exactly matching SPEC §2.2's projected `{server, date, content-length, content-type, connection}`). The `connection: close` appears because the harness sends `Connection: close` on every request per `tests/differential/src/lib.rs:1039` in `drive_http_get`; if a client sends `Connection: keep-alive` (or no Connection header — HTTP/1.1 default), upstream omits the `connection` response header (verified empirically with `curl -i` which uses keep-alive by default — observed 4 headers, no `connection`). envoy-rust's `decorate_filter_synth_response` helper (landed at phase-09 ADR-0033 Commit C `ae2cef0`) decorates the same 5 headers when present, so both proxies emit the same 5-header set under the harness's request framing. **No SPEC revision needed; no PLAN lock-in voided for the header-set finding.**

- **Options considered:**

  - **(i) Land an inline ADR at state-2 PLAN-write commit ratifying the body-bytes revision; edit SPEC.md inline at the 6 sites referencing the body bytes.** Chosen. Mirrors ADR-0033's pattern (which landed at the phase-09 state-3 Task 5 corrective commit + edited phase-09 SPEC §2.2 inline). The PLAN lock-in #14 + Task 5 fixture + Task 7 backstop all reference the 19-byte body bytes from PLAN-write onward.

  - **(ii) Defer the correction to a state-3 PROGRESS Task 5 narrative note (no ADR; no SPEC edit); fix the production-code body string only.** Rejected — D-3.5 ("Decisions are written, not remembered") mandates an ADR for any SPEC revision per the established 05.1 / 05.4 / 09 ADR-inline-at-Task-1-fixup precedent. Phase-09's ADR-0033 explicitly called out the process-gap-awareness doctrine that state-2 PLAN-write SHOULD perform the empirical verification + land any necessary ADR inline — this option deferred to state-3, exactly the process gap the doctrine was meant to close.

  - **(iii) Accept the SPEC's 20-byte projection as a slight upstream-Envoy nonconformance; emit `b"RBAC: access denied\n"` from envoy-rust + accept fixture-0017 bilateral divergence on 1 byte via per-fixture allow-list.** Rejected per D-3.3 ("differential correctness beats internal fidelity") — same anti-pattern that ADR-0033 (phase 09) rejected as option (ii). Accumulating per-fixture allow-list cruft for behavior that should match upstream parity is the anti-pattern the doctrine warns against.

  - **(iv) Treat this as a non-decision SPEC-edit polish (no ADR; just edit SPEC.md inline + record in PROGRESS Task 1 preamble).** Rejected — per D-3.5 + the 05.1 / 05.4 / 09 precedent, SPEC revisions that change a load-bearing wire-level projection (the 403 body bytes are bilaterally compared at fixture 0017 + asserted at the in-process backstop; the projection IS load-bearing) warrant an ADR for durability. The phase-09 ADR-0033 set the precedent: empirical-observation-revising-SPEC = ADR-worthy. Phase-10's body-bytes correction is the same class of revision, even though the magnitude is smaller (1 byte vs 4 headers + entire body shape change).

- **Decision:** Adopt option (i). Land ADR-0034 inline at the state-2 PLAN-write commit. SPEC.md gets 6 inline edits at the 6 sites referencing the body bytes (§2.2 body bytes projection at line ~60; §2.2 fixture-body-on-deny prose at line ~63; §3 D8.1 fixture body assertion at line ~364; §3 D8.3 backstop body assertion at line ~374; §4 deferred custom denial body reference at line ~394; §5.9 filter response shape at line ~459). Each edit replaces `"RBAC: access denied\n"` (20 bytes) with `"RBAC: access denied"` (19 bytes) + cross-refs ADR-0034 as the revision authority. PLAN.md lock-in #14 locks the production-code shape `body: Bytes::from_static(b"RBAC: access denied")`. PROGRESS Task 1 preamble records the empirical-verification methodology + the 3 findings + the disposition narrative. ADR-0033's narrative + the conditional ADR-0034 slots (per SPEC §7 options A/B/C/D) are unaffected — option A fires (this ADR); options B/C/D all DEFER per their recommended postures.

- **Rationale:** D-3.3 "differential correctness beats internal fidelity" mandates that envoy-rust match upstream Envoy's wire-level output as the canonical contract. Empirical run-time evidence (Docker + Python TCP client mirroring `drive_http_get`'s exact request shape + byte-precise body capture via `xxd` + hex-dump verification) is authoritative — the SPEC's 20-byte projection (authored at state-1 brainstorm time without empirical Docker verification) is superseded. The 1-byte correction is small but load-bearing for fixture 0017's `expected_body: { kind: byte_exact, body: "RBAC: access denied" }` assertion (a 1-byte body mismatch would fail the fixture's byte_exact diff). Landing the ADR inline at state-2 PLAN-write — rather than discovering at Task 5 like phase-09's ADR-0033 — exercises the process-gap-awareness doctrine that ADR-0033 ratified; this is the **first phase to leverage the SPEC §6.2 empirical-verification process-improvement** directly, validating that the doctrine works as intended. The minimal scope (6 SPEC inline edits + 1 PLAN lock-in + 1 PROGRESS Task 1 preamble narrative; ZERO production code at state-2 since no code has been written yet) preserves the state-2 docs-only commit posture per the established 06.2 / 06.3 / 07.x / 08.x / 09 cadence.

- **Consequences:** Phase-10 SPEC.md `docs/envoy-rust/phases/10-http-filter-rbac/SPEC.md` at 6 sites ratifies the revised contract for phase 10 going forward (the 6 edits all replace the 20-byte projection with the 19-byte reality + cross-ref ADR-0034). The PLAN.md lock-in #14 explicitly names the production-code shape `Bytes::from_static(b"RBAC: access denied")`. PROGRESS Task 1 preamble records the verification methodology + findings. DECISIONS.md ledger head advances `ADR-0033 → ADR-0034`. **The remaining 3 conditional ADR-0034 slots per SPEC §7 (option B per-route deferral; option C foundations grant; option D D5 superseding-ADR shape) all DEFER per recommended posture** — option B deferred to whichever future filter actually needs per-route config (CORS is the natural close site per upstream Envoy's per-route CorsPolicy pattern); option C no grant projected; option D in-place amendment per SPEC §2.3 + PLAN lock-in #32. **Next available ADR number after this commit is ADR-0035.** Future HTTP-filter-family phases adding filters that emit `Decision::StopAndSend(FilterResponse)` with novel upstream-Envoy denial body bytes (e.g., fault, ext_authz, oauth2, csrf) benefit from the process-improvement validated here: perform the empirical verification at state-2 PLAN-write; land any necessary inline ADR at the same commit. The 09 REVIEW M2 (D5) + M3 (D8.3) carryforward closures projected at phase-10 state-3 are unaffected by THIS ADR — they close at Task 4 + Task 7 respectively per the established PLAN cadence.

- **Provenance:** This ADR was discovered (projected — the SPEC §6.2 + SPEC §7 option A signposted the recommended posture explicitly) at the phase-10 state-2 PLAN-write commit on `2026-05-18` against HEAD `c73f44f` (the phase-10 state-1 brainstorm commit; CI green at run `26047147743`). The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0033** (landed at the phase-09 state-3 Commit A `e9a6cb4` during the mid-execution corrective sequence); ADR-0034 lands at the next-sequential number with no renumbering. The empirical evidence (Docker-based runs of upstream Envoy v1.33 against the canonical bootstrap YAML; observed 403 response headers + body bytes captured both via `curl -i` default-keep-alive framing AND via Python TCP client mirroring the harness's `Connection: close` framing; observed content-length: 19 + body hex-dump `52 42 41 43 3a 20 61 63 63 65 73 73 20 64 65 6e 69 65 64` confirming `"RBAC: access denied"` 19 bytes) was performed by the controller directly at THIS state-2 PLAN-write commit per the SPEC §7 option A recommended posture + the ADR-0033 process-gap-awareness doctrine note. **This is the first ADR landed inline at a state-2 PLAN-write commit per the SPEC §6.2 empirical-verification process-improvement** — validates that the verify-at-PLAN-write discipline works as intended (the projected 1-byte correction was caught BEFORE state-3 execution, avoiding the phase-09-style mid-execution corrective sequence).

## ADR-0035: Relocate `STATE.md` historical-reference narrative to `STATE_HISTORY.md` (navigation/size); clarify the cold-start full-read contract

- **Date:** 2026-05-21.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** `STATE.md` is the project's single source of truth for "what next" (`BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §4.1 invariant 1), and §1 Step B requires reading it **in full** at every cold-start. Under the append-only D-3.4 (context isolation) + D-3.5 (decisions/narratives are written, not deleted) discipline, every phase-lifecycle commit appends to `STATE.md`: a `**Historical reference (preserved …)**` paragraph stack under `## Active phase` / `## Next expected skill` / `## Last commit` / `## Last updated` (preserving each prior commit's `**status:**` / Next-expected-skill / Last-commit / Last-updated narrative verbatim), plus per-phase `## Notes` subsections (rollovers, brainstorm, state-2 PLAN-write, state-3 execution arc, ADR ledger). By the phase-11 state-3 Task-1 commit (`a6435fa`), `STATE.md` had grown to **2091 lines / 521,026 bytes (~509 KB)** — past the cold-start reader's single-pass limits (the Read tool caps at 256 KB / ~25 k tokens per call), forcing a cold-start session to paginate `STATE.md` across many calls. The vast majority of those bytes are closed-phase historical narrative (phases 00–10, all `done`) that a zero-context stranger needs only **on demand**; the live "what next" content — the current active-phase pointers, the current Next-expected-skill block, the current Last commit / Last updated, the `Doctrine reminders` subsection, and the in-flight (phase-11) carryforward inventory — is a small fraction (~140 lines).
- **Options considered:**
  - **(i) Do nothing.** Rejected — `STATE.md` keeps growing every commit; the §1 Step B "read in full" contract degrades from "one read" to "many paginated reads" and eventually becomes infeasible. The navigation/size problem compounds with each future phase.
  - **(ii) Summarize-and-delete the historical blocks.** Rejected per D-3.4 + D-3.5 — the project's hard constraint is that narratives are preserved verbatim, never summarized or deleted; every `**status:**`/Next-expected/Last-commit block and every closed-phase Notes subsection is append-only. Summarizing would destroy the verbatim record (and silently rewrite or drop the `THIS commit` references that resolve against each block's own provenance header), violating D-3.5.
  - **(iii) Verbatim-relocate-with-pointer.** Chosen. Move the historical blocks **byte-for-byte** into a single-purpose, read-on-demand archive (`docs/envoy-rust/STATE_HISTORY.md`), and leave a one-line breadcrumb at each removal point so a zero-context stranger can still find the relocated detail.
- **Decision:** Adopt option (iii). Create `docs/envoy-rust/STATE_HISTORY.md` (append-only; single-purpose; read-on-demand) with a short header explaining what it is, that it is preserved verbatim per this ADR, and that it is NOT a required cold-start read. Relocate the following blocks **verbatim** (byte-for-byte; original section/subsection headings and ordering preserved; no `THIS commit` reference rewritten):
  - The `**Historical reference (preserved …)**` paragraph stacks under `## Active phase`, `## Next expected skill`, `## Last commit`, and `## Last updated`.
  - The `## Notes` subsections belonging to fully-closed phases 00–10 (the ADR-numbering notes, the per-phase rollovers, the brainstorm / state-2 PLAN-write / state-3 execution-arc / ADR-ledger narratives, and the earlier-phase-carryforward + phase-00-deferral point-in-time snapshots).
  - **`STATE.md` retains:** the current `## Active phase` pointers (`id`/`slug`/`directory`/`status`) for the active phase only; the current `## Next expected skill` block; the current `## Last commit` + `## Last updated`; the `### Doctrine reminders` subsection; the in-flight (phase-11) carryforward inventory (the `### Phase-11 state-1 brainstorm` + `### Phase-11 state-2 PLAN-write` Notes subsections, which carry the live carryforward dispositions); and a one-line `>` pointer at each of the 6 removal points.
  - **Result (verified):** `STATE.md` 2091 → **140 lines** (521,026 → **37,410 bytes**); `STATE_HISTORY.md` **2016 lines / 486,619 bytes**. Verification confirmed: every relocated block appears **exactly once** in `STATE_HISTORY.md` and **zero times** in `STATE.md`; the retained live content is **byte-identical** to the corresponding original `STATE.md` slices (the 6 pointer lines are the only additions); the `THIS commit` occurrence count is conserved (94 original = 9 retained-live + 85 relocated; the one additional occurrence in `STATE_HISTORY.md` is its header sentence forbidding `THIS commit`-reference rewrites).
- **Cold-start contract clarification (load-bearing):** `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §1 Step B is clarified with a one-paragraph note: **`STATE.md` remains a required full read** at cold-start; **`STATE_HISTORY.md` is read-on-demand only — NOT a required cold-start read** (consult it only when following a `STATE.md` pointer back to relocated detail). `docs/envoy-rust/MISSION.md` is **unaffected and not edited** — it is a verbatim copy of `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §§2–3 (mission + doctrine), not §1, so it does not duplicate the cold-start "read in full" text. `docs/envoy-rust/SKILL_ROUTING.md` is **unaffected** — it is a verbatim copy of §5 (the phase-lifecycle state machine), not §1.
- **Rationale:** D-3.4 (context isolation — a stranger must still find everything) is preserved by the breadcrumb pointers + the single-purpose archive; nothing becomes unreachable. D-3.5 (append-only; never edit a landed narrative) is honored by relocating byte-for-byte rather than summarizing or deleting. §4.1 invariant 1 (`STATE.md` is the single source of truth for "what next") is **strengthened, not weakened** — the live "what next" pointers all stay in `STATE.md`; only read-on-demand history moves out, restoring the §1 Step B single-pass full read. The relocation is mechanical, reversible (git history retains the prior `STATE.md`), and verifiable (byte-identity of the retained slices was confirmed programmatically before commit).
- **Consequences:** `STATE.md` is again readable in a single pass at cold-start. Future state-N commits continue appending their `**Historical reference (preserved …)**` blocks + Notes subsections to `STATE.md` per the unchanged D-3.4/D-3.5 cadence; when `STATE.md` again approaches the reader's single-pass limit, a future docs-maintenance commit relocates the newly-closed-phase blocks into `STATE_HISTORY.md` per **this ADR's precedent** (append to `STATE_HISTORY.md`, preserving document order; leave a pointer). DECISIONS.md ledger head advances **ADR-0034 → ADR-0035**; next available **ADR-0036**. Files touched at this commit: `docs/envoy-rust/STATE.md` (relocate + 6 pointers), `docs/envoy-rust/STATE_HISTORY.md` (new), `docs/envoy-rust/DECISIONS.md` (this ADR), `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` (§1 Step B clarification). No `MISSION.md` change (not duplicated); no `SKILL_ROUTING.md` change (copies §5, not §1). No production code / test / fixture change; no `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` change; no `ENVOY_TARGET.md` / `rust-toolchain.toml` change (D-3.7 / D-3.9 unchanged); no `unsafe` introduced.
- **Provenance:** The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0034** (phase-10 state-2 PLAN-write `55abc61`). ADR-0035 was the next-available number, reserved-available through the phase-11 state-1 brainstorm + state-2 PLAN-write (where the four conditional phase-11 ADR-0035 slots — A empirical-verification revision, B fractional-deferral durability, C foundations grant, D `Driver::Http2ProbeList` durability — all DEFERRED per their recommended postures, leaving 0035 free); it lands here with no renumbering. This is a **project-infrastructure / documentation-maintenance decision**, independent of phase-11's feature work; it lands on a standalone docs commit, **not** a phase-lifecycle commit. Phase 11 remains `in-progress` at lifecycle state 3 (Task 1 landed at `a6435fa`; Tasks 2–8 pending); this commit does not advance the phase lifecycle and touches no phase-11 artifact (`SPEC.md` / `PLAN.md` / `PROGRESS.md`) or `ROADMAP.md` row.

## ADR-0036: Split phase 12 into sub-phases 12.1 and 12.2

- **Date:** 2026-05-22.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Phase 12 (`12-upstream-active-health-check`) — the first concrete Upstream-robustness-family phase — was projected as a single phase by the parent-12 state-1 brainstorm SPEC (`docs/envoy-rust/phases/12-upstream-active-health-check/SPEC.md`, committed at HEAD `edd654c`, the immediate predecessor of this commit). Phase 12 lands active HTTP health checking: a per-cluster config schema (D1) + validator (D2), a per-endpoint `EndpointHealth` state machine (D3), a periodic-background active-HC probe task (D4 — the project's FIRST periodic-background primitive; a new `envoy-health` crate), load-balancer integration (D5 — `pick()` excludes unhealthy + honors the panic threshold), health-check stats (D6), and a differential fixture `0019` + the first synthetic-backend harness primitive (the 06.3 REVIEW I2 down-payment) + a settle-then-probe driver + an in-process backstop + a fuzz seed (D7). The parent SPEC §6.1 projected **~1800–2200 LoC** (production ~850, tests ~900, fixture/harness/backend ~470) across **~14–16 tasks**. The state-2 PLAN-write materialized the estimate against HEAD `edd654c` + the §6.2 empirical verification (see ADR-0037 + STATE.md `### Phase-12 state-2 split decision`) and confirmed the surface is **materially over the `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §6.1 ~1500-LoC split gate** (the task-count gate ~14–16 is under ~25, but EITHER gate triggers the split). The parent SPEC §6.1 explicitly recommended the 12.1 / 12.2 split shape ratified here, and §7 reserved `ADR-0036` for it ("Conditional ADR-0036 (option A — phase split). LIKELY TO FIRE.").
- **Options considered:**
  - **(i) Single phase.** Rejected — the LoC gate fires decisively at projection time (~1800–2200 vs ~1500 cap), before any execution-time drift. Single-phase shipment historically splits under mid-execution pressure (§6.1's in-flight trigger when any single task's sub-steps blow past ~10 items), which is strictly worse than splitting at state 2. The new periodic-task primitive (D4) + the new crate + the new synthetic-backend harness + the settle-driver are the load-bearing complexity contributors.
  - **(ii) Three-way split** (12.α = config + validator; 12.β = `EndpointHealth` + LB-integration + stats; 12.γ = task + fixture + close). Rejected — the config+validator slice (12.α) is too small to motivate a full state-machine cycle (~250 LoC) and is mechanically intertwined with the `EndpointHealth`/LB-integration it feeds; splitting there leaves a schema-only sub-phase with no runtime consumer (the same anti-pattern phase-07's ADR-0030 rejected for its option-iii).
  - **(iii) Two-way split by code area** (12.α = envoy-config only; 12.β = envoy-cluster + envoy-health + fixture). Rejected — mixes the `EndpointHealth` foundation (envoy-cluster) with the probe task (envoy-health) + the fixture in one oversize 12.β, leaving 12.α a thin schema-only sub-phase that does not stand alone. Scatters scope by crate, not by deliverable cohesion.
  - **(iv) Two-way split by deliverable cohesion: config + health-state + LB-integration + stats foundation (12.1) → active-HC probe task + fixture + parent close (12.2).** Chosen. 12.1 lands a coherent **foundation slice** — the config schema, the validator, the `EndpointHealth` state machine, the `pick()` unhealthy-exclusion + panic-threshold integration, and the stats wiring — proven by **regression-equivalence** (all 18 existing fixtures stay green simultaneously; the machinery is inert when `health_checks` is unconfigured per parent SPEC §5.4), exactly the **05.1 / 07.1 "foundation slice, no new fixture" pattern**. 12.2 lands the **observable behavior** — the active-HC periodic-probe task (the new `envoy-health` crate), the no-healthy-upstream synth-503 body reconciliation (ADR-0037), the differential fixture `0019` + the synthetic-backend harness (the 06.3 REVIEW I2 down-payment) + the settle-driver + the in-process backstop — and closes parent-12.
  - **(v) Nested split of 12.1 or 12.2.** Rejected — three-level numbering is awkward (`12.1.1`); `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §6.1 flags nested splits of an already-split sub-phase as suspicious. Each sub-phase fits comfortably under the §6.1 gates (12.1 ~900–1000 LoC; 12.2 ~1000–1200 LoC).
- **Decision:** split phase 12 into two sub-phases by deliverable cohesion (foundation slice → observable-behavior + parent close), mirroring phase-02's ADR-0013, phase-03's ADR-0017, phase-07's ADR-0030, and phase-08's ADR-0032 2-way precedents (the fifth 2-way split in the project):
  - **12.1 (`12.1-endpoint-health-and-lb-integration`)** — config + health-state + LB-integration + stats foundation slice. Deliverables D1 (envoy-config `HealthCheck`/`HttpHealthCheck`/`CommonLbConfig`/`Percent` schema; reuses `parse_duration` + `Int64Range`) + D2 (validator; ~4–6 `ConfigError` variants) + D3 (`EndpointHealth` state machine in envoy-cluster; initial state Unhealthy/pending per the §6.2 item-1 finding) + D5 (`pick()` excludes unhealthy + panic threshold — default 50 %, strictly-below, `Percent { value }`, per §6.2 item-3) + D6 (`cluster.<name>.health_check.*` + `membership_healthy` stats) + the `parse_bootstrap` corpus seed (18 → 19). **No new fixture** — regression-equivalence via the 18 existing fixtures (`0001`–`0018`) green simultaneously (the machinery inert when unconfigured). ~900–1000 LoC. Depends on `02 04`.
  - **12.2 (`12.2-active-http-probe-and-fixture`)** — active-HC probe task + fixture + parent-12 close. Deliverables D4 (the new `crates/envoy-health/` crate + the periodic-probe task; reuses `envoy-http1::Client`; a clean DAG over `envoy-cluster` + `envoy-http1` + `envoy-config` + `envoy-stats` + `tokio` — no cycle) + D6.2 (the no-healthy-upstream synth-503 body reconciliation per ADR-0037) + D7.1 (fixture `0019-upstream-active-health-check` + the synthetic health-aware backend harness primitive — the 06.3 REVIEW I2 down-payment — + the settle-then-probe driver + the Docker wrapper) + D7.3 (in-process backstop; both convergence directions) + parent-12 ROADMAP-row close-out. ~1000–1200 LoC. Depends on `12.1`.
- **Rationale:** the 2-way flat split mirrors the four prior 2-way splits (phases 02 / 03 / 07 / 08). The split boundary is by deliverable cohesion (foundation seam → observable behavior + parent close), NOT by crate area: the config + state-machine + LB-integration are architecturally inseparable (12.1 ships them as one coherent slice with regression-equivalence as the differential proof — the 05.1/07.1 precedent); the probe task + the fixture are the demonstration slice (12.2 proves the steady-state health decision is equivalent to Envoy's via fixture 0019 + the synthetic backend). Failure attribution localizes per sub-phase: 12.1's REVIEW.md owns the schema/validator/state-machine/LB-integration/stats verdict + the regression-equivalence guarantee; 12.2's REVIEW.md owns the probe-task + fixture + I2-down-payment verdict + parent-12 close. **Sub-phase ordering invariant: 12.1 → 12.2 cannot be parallelized** because (a) 12.2's probe task mutates 12.1's `EndpointHealth` + drives 12.1's `pick()` unhealthy-exclusion seam; (b) 12.2's fixture exercises the no-healthy-upstream path that 12.1's `pick() -> None` change first made reachable for configured-HC clusters; (c) 12.2 closes the parent-12 ROADMAP row. A subtle consequence of the §6.2 item-1 finding (endpoints start Unhealthy/pending): in the 12.1-only world a *configured*-HC cluster has no probe task to ever mark its endpoints healthy, so 12.1 deliberately ships NO fixture/test configuring `health_checks` on a traffic-serving cluster — the configured-HC path stays inert-and-unexercised until 12.2 wires the probe task + the fixture together (the same "land the seam; the consumer lands next" discipline as 07.1's unused `Decision::StopAndSend`).
- **Consequences:** parent SPEC at `docs/envoy-rust/phases/12-upstream-active-health-check/SPEC.md` was committed at parent-12 state-1 (the predecessor of this commit, `edd654c`) projecting this split per parent-SPEC §6.1 + §7 + §8; sub-phase SPECs (`12.1-endpoint-health-and-lb-integration/SPEC.md`, `12.2-active-http-probe-and-fixture/SPEC.md`) land alongside this ADR in this same parent-12 state-2 split commit. ROADMAP gains 2 new rows (`12.1`, `12.2`) at this commit; row `12`'s `sub-phases` column updates from `—` to `12.1, 12.2`; row `12`'s `status` flips `planned` → `in-progress` at this commit. Sub-phases ship strictly in order (12.1 → 12.2) — they cannot be parallelized per the rationale above. Parent ROADMAP row `12` flips `done` at sub-phase 12.2's state-6 phase-done commit, mirroring the phase-02/03/07/08 closing-sub-phase precedents. STATE.md advances at this commit to point at `12.1` lifecycle **state 2** (next-skill `superpowers:writing-plans` scoped to the 12.1 SPEC) — the parent state-2 split commit lands each sub-phase's SPEC and advances STATE.md to the first sub-phase's state 2 directly (skipping 12.1 state 1 brainstorm) because the parent-12 SPEC already brainstormed the full surface and the sub-phase SPECs are surface-slice carve-outs derived from it (mirrors the parent-07 `6db5a01` + parent-08 `56dee82` precedents). **Phase 12's projected ADR ledger after this commit:** ADR-0036 (this ADR) + ADR-0037 (the §6.2 no-healthy-upstream-body empirical reconciliation; lands at this same commit, consecutive number, per the parent SPEC §6.2 "if both fire at the PLAN-write commit they take consecutive numbers"). No further ADR is projected for the 12.1 or 12.2 lifecycles (the `envoy-health` crate is a clean-DAG new crate = ordinary structure, no creation ADR; no foundations grant projected).
- **Provenance:** projected as the next-sequential available ADR number in parent-12 SPEC §7 ("Conditional ADR-0036 (option A — phase split). LIKELY TO FIRE."). The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0035** (the STATE.md-relocation infra ADR, `bfef772`); ADR-0036 lands at the next-sequential number with no renumbering (no inter-ADR landings between the phase-11 close at `7e27c1e` and this commit — the phase-12 state-1 brainstorm `edd654c` landed no ADR). Like phases 02 / 03 / 07 / 08, phase-12's projected split-ADR number matches the actual landed number.

## ADR-0037: Phase-12 §6.2 empirical reconciliation — no-healthy-upstream synth-503 body is `no healthy upstream` (19 bytes), reconciling envoy-rust's empty-body synth-503

- **Date:** 2026-05-22.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Phase 12's parent SPEC §2.2 + §6.2 directed the state-2 PLAN-writer to empirically verify upstream Envoy v1.33's **no-healthy-upstream synth-503 response body bytes** before locking PLAN lock-ins (the phase-10/11 byte-precision lesson: do not assume — the SPEC §2.2 left the exact bytes to §6.2 verification, and the SPEC §7 named the no-healthy-upstream body as a likely empirical-revision-ADR trigger). The verification ran `envoyproxy/envoy:v1.33.0` Docker against an active-HC cluster (one endpoint at a synthetic health-aware backend returning 503 on `/healthz`; `common_lb_config.healthy_panic_threshold: { value: 0 }` so the all-unhealthy cluster reaches `pick() -> None` rather than panic-routing). After the endpoint was ejected, a downstream `GET /` returned:
  - **status:** 503.
  - **body bytes (hex):** `6e 6f 20 68 65 61 6c 74 68 79 20 75 70 73 74 72 65 61 6d`.
  - **body string:** `no healthy upstream` — **19 bytes, NO trailing newline**.
  - **`content-length: 19`**; header set `{ content-length, content-type: text/plain, date, server: envoy, connection: close }` (5 headers under the harness's `Connection: close` request framing).
  - **NO wire-observable `x-envoy-*` response-flag header** (the `UH` "no healthy upstream" flag is an access-log surface, not a data-plane response header).

  envoy-rust's existing no-healthy-upstream synth-503 path (`crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:582`, the `else` arm where `pick_endpoint()` returned `None`) uses `synth_status(503, close)` (`crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:918`), which emits an **EMPTY body** (`Bytes::new()`, `content-length: 0`) with the same 5-header set. **MATERIAL DIVERGENCE** — empty body vs the 19-byte `no healthy upstream` body. The path was previously defense-in-depth only (the validator rejects empty clusters, so phase-02 round-robin never returned `None`); phase 12 makes it reachable (a configured-HC cluster with all endpoints ejected + panic disabled), so the divergence becomes a real differential observable that fixture `0019` will assert byte-exact.
- **Options considered:**
  - **(i) Reconcile by adjusting envoy-rust's implementation: emit `no healthy upstream` (19 bytes) on the no-healthy-upstream path; land an inline ADR at the state-2 PLAN-write commit pinning the body bytes.** Chosen. Mirrors the phase-10 ADR-0034 pattern (empirical-observation-pins-load-bearing-wire-bytes = ADR-worthy; the body is bilaterally compared at fixture 0019 + asserted at the in-process backstop, so it is load-bearing). The body change lands in 12.2 (co-located with the fixture that exercises it bilaterally); the ADR lands now (state-2) for durability.
  - **(ii) Accept the empty-body synth-503 + allow-list the 19-byte divergence per-fixture.** Rejected per **D-3.3** ("differential correctness beats internal fidelity") — the same anti-pattern ADR-0033/0034 rejected. Accumulating per-fixture allow-list cruft for behavior that should match upstream parity is the doctrine's named anti-pattern.
  - **(iii) Defer the reconciliation to a state-3 PROGRESS note (no ADR; fix the body only).** Rejected per **D-3.5** — a load-bearing wire-level body projection warrants an ADR for durability (the 05.1 / 05.4 / 09 / 10 inline-ADR precedent). The parent SPEC §6.2 + §7 explicitly signposted this as the recommended posture: verify at PLAN-write, land any necessary inline ADR at the same commit.
- **Decision:** Adopt option (i). Land ADR-0037 inline at the parent-12 state-2 split commit, pinning the no-healthy-upstream synth-503 body as **`no healthy upstream`** (19 bytes, hex `6e 6f 20 68 65 61 6c 74 68 79 20 75 70 73 74 72 65 61 6d`, no trailing newline) with `content-length: 19`. The **implementation reconciliation lands in 12.2** (deliverable D6.2): a no-healthy-upstream-specific synth-503 emitting the 19-byte body (recommended shape: a dedicated `synth_no_healthy_upstream(close)` helper mirroring `synth_status` but with `body: Bytes::from_static(b"no healthy upstream")` + `content-length: 19`), changing the body **only on the no-healthy-upstream writer arm** (`hcm.rs:582`) and NOT globally on `synth_status` (the connect-fail 502 paths at `hcm.rs:524`/`:568` and other synth paths keep their existing bodies). Fixture `0019` (12.2 D7.1) asserts the body byte-exact; the in-process backstop (12.2 D7.3) asserts it on the unhealthy-convergence direction.
