The BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md `Response body` "no-healthy-upstream" disposition (19-byte `no healthy upstream`, byte-exact) lands at the 12.2 D6.2 task per the 06.x → 11 contract-extension cadence (at the task where the surface is first empirically exercised, NOT at this PLAN-write commit).
- **Rationale:** **D-3.3** mandates envoy-rust match upstream Envoy's wire-level output as the canonical contract; the empirical Docker run-time evidence (hex-dump byte-precise capture against `envoyproxy/envoy:v1.33.0`) is authoritative. The 19-byte body is load-bearing for fixture 0019's `byte_exact` assertion (an empty-vs-19-byte mismatch fails the fixture). Landing the ADR inline at state-2 PLAN-write — rather than discovering at fixture-build time — exercises the phase-10-ratified verify-at-PLAN-write discipline. The minimal scope (this ADR + the 12.2 D6.2 implementation lock-in + the STATE.md findings narrative; ZERO production code at state-2 since the split commit is docs-only) preserves the state-2 docs-only commit posture. The other 5 §6.2 items (initial health state = Unhealthy/pending; panic default 50 % strictly-below; the `health_check.*` + `membership_healthy` stat names; the `GET /healthz` + cluster-name-authority probe shape + default-200 + half-open `Int64Range`; the duration shape) all MATCHED the parent SPEC's projections and require no ADR (the duration finding — Envoy accepts `0.5s` but not `500ms`, envoy-rust accepts `500ms` but not `0.5s`, shared form = integer seconds — lands as a PLAN-time fixture-shape correction, not a wire-contract ADR).
- **Consequences:** DECISIONS.md ledger head advances **ADR-0036 → ADR-0037** (both land at this same parent-12 state-2 split commit, consecutive numbers, per the parent SPEC §6.2 "if both fire at the PLAN-write commit they take consecutive numbers across the lock-in narrative"). The 12.2 SPEC §2 + §3 D6.2 record the 19-byte body as a locked fact; the 12.2 commit carries `[ADR-0037]` (it implements the reconciliation). No further empirical-revision ADR is projected for phase 12 (the remaining 5 items matched). **Next available ADR number after this commit is ADR-0038.** Future upstream-robustness phases reconciling other synth-response bodies (e.g. circuit-breaker overflow, outlier-ejection responses) benefit from the verify-at-PLAN-write discipline re-validated here.
- **Provenance:** projected as `ADR-0037` in parent-12 SPEC §7 ("Conditional ADR-0037 (option B — §6.2 empirical-verification revision). PLAUSIBLE … esp. the initial-health-state semantics or the no-healthy-upstream body bytes"). The initial-health-state semantics MATCHED the projection (no revision); the no-healthy-upstream body bytes DIVERGED from envoy-rust's current implementation (empty vs 19-byte) → this ADR fires. The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0035**; ADR-0036 (the split) takes the first number, ADR-0037 (this empirical reconciliation) the next, both at this commit with no renumbering. The empirical evidence (Docker run of `envoyproxy/envoy:v1.33.0` against an active-HC cluster + a synthetic health-aware backend on a shared Docker bridge network; data-plane `GET /` after endpoint ejection captured via `curl -i` + a Python byte-precise body hex-dump; observed `content-length: 19` + body hex `6e 6f 20 68 65 61 6c 74 68 79 20 75 70 73 74 72 65 61 6d` = `"no healthy upstream"` 19 bytes) was captured by the controller directly at THIS state-2 PLAN-write commit per the parent SPEC §6.2 + §7 recommended posture.

## ADR-0038: Split phase 13 into sub-phases 13.1 and 13.2

- **Date:** 2026-05-24.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Phase 13 (`13-connection-pooling`) — the second concrete Upstream-robustness-family phase (after parent-12 closed at `3ec7fb9`) — was projected as a single phase by the parent-13 state-1 brainstorm SPEC (`docs/envoy-rust/phases/13-connection-pooling/SPEC.md`, committed at HEAD `221b0fd`, the immediate predecessor of this commit). Phase 13 lands per-protocol upstream connection pooling: a per-cluster `circuit_breakers.max_connections` envoy-config schema (D1) + validator (D2), an H1 connection-pool primitive (D3 — the project's FIRST connection-pool primitive; a new `pool.rs` module inside `envoy-http1`), the H1 router-arm pool integration (D4), the H2 connection-pool primitive (D5 — multiplexed-streams pool inside `envoy-http2`), the H2 router-arm pool integration (D6), pool stats wiring (D7) + the `cluster.<name>.upstream_cx_total` BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT row tightening to `value-exact` (D7.1 — the named full-closure site for 06.3 REVIEW I2 (b)), the configurable-status synthetic backend (D8 — extends the 12.2 `health-aware-http1-backend` per-path-status capability; the named full-closure site for 06.3 REVIEW I2 (a)), a differential fixture + Docker wrapper + in-process backstop (D9), a settle-and-scrape differential driver extension (D10 — the single-downstream-keep-alive-conn driver `Driver::Http1KeepAlive` required for the §6.2 item-(iv) discriminating-observable shape), and a `parse_bootstrap` fuzz seed (D11). The parent SPEC §6.1 projected **~1800–2400 LoC** (production ~970, tests ~1020, fixture/harness/backend ~470) across **~16–20 tasks**. The state-2 PLAN-write materialized the estimate against HEAD `221b0fd` + the §6.2 HEAVY 9-item empirical verification (see the §6.2 findings table in STATE.md `### Phase-13 state-2 split decision`) and confirmed the surface is **materially over the `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §6.1 ~1500-LoC split gate** (the task-count gate ~16–20 is approaching ~25; EITHER gate triggers the split). The parent SPEC §6.1 explicitly recommended the 13.1 / 13.2 split shape ratified here, and §7 reserved `ADR-0038` for it ("Conditional ADR-0038 (option A — phase split). LIKELY TO FIRE.").
- **Options considered:**
  - **(i) Single phase.** Rejected — the LoC gate fires decisively at projection time (~1800–2400 vs ~1500 cap), before any execution-time drift. Single-phase shipment historically splits under mid-execution pressure (§6.1's in-flight trigger when any single task's sub-steps blow past ~10 items), which is strictly worse than splitting at state 2. Two new connection-pool primitives (H1 + H2) + the new harness driver variant + the new fixture(s) + the BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT row tightening + the configurable-backend extension are the load-bearing complexity contributors.
  - **(ii) Three-way split** (13.α = envoy-config schema + validator + fuzz seed; 13.β = H1 pool + H1 router wiring + fixture; 13.γ = H2 pool + H2 router wiring + tightening + parent close). Rejected — the envoy-config schema slice (13.α) is too small to motivate a full state-machine cycle (~150 LoC) and is mechanically intertwined with the H1 pool that consumes the `max_connections` field; splitting there leaves a schema-only sub-phase with no runtime consumer (the same anti-pattern phase-07's ADR-0030 + phase-12's ADR-0036 option-(ii) both rejected).
  - **(iii) Two-way split by foundation-slice cohesion** (13.α = H1 pool + H2 pool foundations + envoy-config schema + NO new fixture; 13.β = H1+H2 router-arm wiring + new fixture(s) + per-class coverage + parent close). Rejected — mixes both protocols' pool primitives into 13.α without a single fixture exercising the new behavior. The §6.2 item-(ii) finding (default-enabled pool with hardcoded `max_connections: 1024`) means the pool runs on every existing fixture immediately when the router-arm dispatches through it — landing pool primitives without router-arm integration creates a half-wired seam (the primitive sits unused until 13.β wires the router-arm). The 05.1 / 07.1 / 12.1 foundation-slice precedent works because the new machinery is INERT when its config field is absent; phase-13's default-enabled pool breaks the inert-when-unconfigured property. The recommended H1-only / H2-only seam keeps each sub-phase's wiring atomic (config → primitive → router-arm → fixture per protocol).
  - **(iv) Two-way split by protocol cohesion: H1 pool + envoy-config schema + H1 router wiring + per-class counter fixture (13.1) → H2 pool + H2 router wiring + `upstream_cx_total` BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT tightening + parent close (13.2).** Chosen. 13.1 lands a coherent **H1-only slice** — the envoy-config schema, the validator, the H1 pool primitive, the H1 router-arm integration, the configurable-backend extension, the `Driver::Http1KeepAlive` harness variant, fixture 0020 (per-class counter bilateral coverage = 06.3 REVIEW I2 (a) full-closure surface), the in-process backstop, the fuzz seed. ~1000–1200 LoC. 13.2 lands the **H2-only slice + the contract-surface deliverable** — the H2 pool primitive, the H2 router-arm integration, the `cluster.<name>.upstream_cx_total` BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT row tightening to `value-exact` (the 06.3 REVIEW I2 (b) full-closure site; fires NOW that both protocols pool uniformly), the H2-pool stat row `upstream_cx_http2_total`, fixture 0021 (sibling — H2-pool bilateral observation; reuses the existing `http2-echo-server` helper), the in-process H2 backstop, parent-13 close (closing-sub-phase invariant). ~900–1100 LoC.
  - **(v) Nested split of 13.1 or 13.2.** Rejected — three-level numbering is awkward (`13.1.1`); `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §6.1 flags nested splits of an already-split sub-phase as suspicious. Each sub-phase fits comfortably under the §6.1 gates.
- **Decision:** split phase 13 into two sub-phases by protocol cohesion (H1 + per-class fixture → H2 + contract tightening + parent close), mirroring phase-02's ADR-0013, phase-03's ADR-0017, phase-07's ADR-0030, phase-08's ADR-0032, and phase-12's ADR-0036 2-way precedents (the SIXTH 2-way split in the project):
  - **13.1 (`13.1-h1-pool-and-fixture`)** — H1 pool + envoy-config circuit_breakers schema + H1 router-arm pool integration + configurable-backend extension + Driver::Http1KeepAlive + fixture 0020 + per-class counter wire bilateral coverage (06.3 REVIEW I2 (a) closure) + in-process H1 backstop + fuzz seed. Deliverables D1 (`CircuitBreakers`/`Thresholds`/`RoutingPriority` schema) + D2 (validator; ~3 `ConfigError` variants) + D3 (`H1Pool` primitive at `crates/envoy-http1/src/pool.rs`; `PoolGuard` RAII reusing `ConnGaugeGuard`; idle sweeper = the project's second periodic-background primitive) + D4 (H1 router-arm pool integration; `upstream_cx_total` increment-site migrates from `hcm.rs:514` into the pool's `acquire()` connect-on-miss branch) + D7-H1 (pool stats wiring: `cluster.<name>.upstream_cx_destroy` + `upstream_cx_http1_total` BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT rows; **NO `upstream_cx_total` tightening at 13.1**) + D8 (configurable-status synthetic backend: `--per-path PATH=STATUS,...` flag added to the 12.2 `health-aware-http1-backend`) + D9.1-H1 (fixture 0020-upstream-connection-pooling-and-per-class-counters; per-class counter bilateral coverage = 06.3 REVIEW I2 (a) full-closure site) + D9.3-H1 (in-process H1 backstop) + D10 (Driver::Http1KeepAlive harness extension) + D11 (parse_bootstrap corpus seed `cluster_circuit_breakers.yaml`; corpus 20 → 21). ~1000–1200 LoC. Depends on `04 05 06 12`.
  - **13.2 (`13.2-h2-pool-and-cx-total-tightening`)** — H2 pool + H2 router-arm pool integration + the `cluster.<name>.upstream_cx_total` BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT row tightening (06.3 REVIEW I2 (b) closure) + new H2 pool stat rows + fixture 0021 (or 0020 extension) + in-process H2 backstop + parent-13 close. Deliverables D5 (`H2Pool` primitive at `crates/envoy-http2/src/pool.rs`; per-connection active-streams tracking; multi-connection-per-endpoint at peer's SETTINGS_MAX_CONCURRENT_STREAMS cap; idle sweeper = the project's third periodic-background primitive) + D6 (H2 router-arm pool integration; `upstream_cx_total` increment-site migrates from `hcm.rs:280`+`:291` into the pool) + D7.1 (BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT.md `cluster.<name>.upstream_cx_total` row rewritten from `name-required, value-may-differ` to `value-exact` for H1+H2 clusters; TCP-proxy carved out — TCP pool defers; the **named full-closure site for 06.3 REVIEW I2 (b)**) + D7.2 (`cluster.<name>.upstream_cx_http2_total` BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT row) + D9.1-H2 (fixture 0021-upstream-h2-connection-pooling sibling; reuses the existing `http2-echo-server` helper as backend) + D9.3-H2 (in-process H2 backstop) + parent-13 close (flips ROADMAP rows `13.2` AND parent `13` `in-progress → done` SIMULTANEOUSLY per the closing-sub-phase invariant). ~900–1100 LoC. Depends on `13.1`.
- **Rationale:** the 2-way protocol-cohesion split mirrors the five prior 2-way splits (phases 02 / 03 / 07 / 08 / 12). The split boundary is by **deliverable cohesion via the per-protocol axis** (H1 surface + per-class fixture → H2 surface + contract tightening + parent close), NOT by code area (a 13.α envoy-config-only slice was rejected as too thin) and NOT by foundation-slice cohesion (the §6.2 default-enabled-pool finding breaks the inert-when-unconfigured property the 05.1/07.1/12.1 foundation-slice pattern relies on). Failure attribution localizes per sub-phase: 13.1's REVIEW.md owns the H1 pool + envoy-config schema + per-class counter coverage verdict + the 06.3 REVIEW I2 (a) full-closure attribution; 13.2's REVIEW.md owns the H2 pool + the `upstream_cx_total` value-exact tightening + the 06.3 REVIEW I2 (b) full-closure attribution + parent-13 close. **Sub-phase ordering invariant: 13.1 → 13.2 cannot be parallelized** because (a) 13.2's H2 pool architecturally mirrors 13.1's H1 pool (the cycle-resolution pattern, the `PoolGuard`/`ConnGaugeGuard` RAII discipline, the stats wiring conventions all land at 13.1 and carry to 13.2); (b) 13.2's BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT row tightening at D7.1 can only fire AFTER both protocols pool uniformly (otherwise H2 cluster fixtures see per-call accounting while H1 sees pool reuse — the row cannot tighten across all H1+H2 clusters); (c) 13.2 closes parent-13. The fixture-vs-extension choice (D9.1-H2: sibling fixture 0021 vs. extending 0020) is locked as **sibling fixture 0021** per the parent SPEC §6.1 recommendation — cleaner separation of H1-pool and H2-pool surfaces + reuses the existing `http2-echo-server` helper without per-path-status extension.
- **Consequences:** parent SPEC at `docs/envoy-rust/phases/13-connection-pooling/SPEC.md` was committed at parent-13 state-1 (`221b0fd`, the predecessor of this commit) projecting this split per parent-SPEC §6.1 + §7 + §8; sub-phase SPECs (`13.1-h1-pool-and-fixture/SPEC.md`, `13.2-h2-pool-and-cx-total-tightening/SPEC.md`) land alongside this ADR in this same parent-13 state-2 split commit. ROADMAP gains 2 new rows (`13.1`, `13.2`) at this commit; row `13`'s `sub-phases` column updates from `—` to `13.1, 13.2`; row `13`'s `status` flips `planned` → `in-progress` at this commit. Sub-phases ship strictly in order (13.1 → 13.2) — they cannot be parallelized per the rationale above. Parent ROADMAP row `13` flips `done` at sub-phase 13.2's state-6 phase-done commit, mirroring the phase-02/03/07/08/12 closing-sub-phase precedents. STATE.md advances at this commit to point at `13.1` lifecycle **state 2** (next-skill `superpowers:writing-plans` scoped to the 13.1 SPEC) — the parent state-2 split commit lands each sub-phase's SPEC and advances STATE.md to the first sub-phase's state 2 directly (skipping 13.1 state 1 brainstorm) because the parent-13 SPEC already brainstormed the full surface and the sub-phase SPECs are surface-slice carve-outs derived from it (mirrors the parent-07 `6db5a01` + parent-08 `56dee82` + parent-12 `4f9ba04` precedents). **Phase 13's projected ADR ledger after this commit:** ADR-0038 (this ADR) only. **The §6.2 empirical-revision ADR (parent SPEC §7 option B; reserved as `ADR-0039`) does NOT fire** — all 9 items MATCHED the parent SPEC's projections (no material divergence; the item-(iv) discriminating-observable nuance is a PLAN-time fixture-shape lock-in to use `Driver::Http1KeepAlive`, NOT a wire-contract change warranting an ADR). The item-(iii) finding (idle_timeout knob lives under `typed_extension_protocol_options.http.v3.HttpProtocolOptions.common_http_protocol_options.idle_timeout`) is a §4 deferral (phase-13 uses a hardcoded 60 s default), not a contract change. No further ADR is projected for the 13.1 or 13.2 lifecycles (the H1 pool + H2 pool are new modules INSIDE existing crates = ordinary structure, no creation ADR; the cycle-resolution via bin-wired injection is ordinary structure, no ADR; no foundations grant projected — pools are hand-rolled atop existing pulled crates per D-3.2).
- **Provenance:** projected as the next-sequential available ADR number in parent-13 SPEC §7 ("Conditional ADR-0038 (option A — phase split). LIKELY TO FIRE."). The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0037** (the parent-12 §6.2 no-healthy-upstream-body empirical reconciliation, `4f9ba04`); ADR-0038 lands at the next-sequential number with no renumbering (no inter-ADR landings between the phase-12.2 close at `3ec7fb9` and this commit — the phase-13 state-1 brainstorm `221b0fd` landed no ADR). Like phases 02 / 03 / 07 / 08 / 12, phase-13's projected split-ADR number matches the actual landed number.

## ADR-0039: Phase-13.2 Task 5 fixture topology pivot — H2-downstream-listener + H2-upstream-cluster + new `Driver::Http2KeepAlive` harness driver

- **Date:** 2026-05-25
- **Status:** accepted
- **Context:** Phase 13.2's PLAN Task 5 (D9.1-H2 deliverable) specifies fixture `0021-upstream-h2-connection-pooling` as a sibling of 13.1's fixture 0020 with an architectural change: the cluster carries `typed_extension_protocol_options.envoy.extensions.upstreams.http.v3.HttpProtocolOptions.explicit_http_config.http2_protocol_options: {}` (H2 upstream). The PLAN's prescribed downstream stays HTTP/1 (so the 13.1-landed `Driver::Http1KeepAlive` reuses VERBATIM). At Task 5 state-3 execution, the implementer subagent discovered that this exact topology — **HTTP/1 listener routing to H2 upstream cluster** — is REJECTED at envoy-rust config-load time by the 06.3 D14.3 parse-time validator gate at `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs:1997-2016`, which raises `ConfigError::Http2ClusterFromHttp1Listener` per ADR-0028's deferral. The 06.3 D14.3 gate was added precisely to surface this deferred path so operators don't get silent HTTP/1-on-the-wire to an HTTP/2-only backend. The implementer landed all 6 Task 5 artifacts per the PLAN (3 fixture YAMLs + 1 Docker wrapper + 1 harness `_h2_echo_backend` arm + PROGRESS subsection), the 5 stable-toolchain gates passed clean, but the Docker-gated wrapper test FAILED at envoy-rust bootstrap parse with the named ConfigError. The PLAN-writer (the 13.2 state-2 commit `8c7d8a2`) MISSED this architectural constraint: PLAN lock-in #7 + §6.2 item-vi inheritance from parent-13 did not surface it because the 06.3 D14.3 gate is at envoy-config, not envoy-http1/envoy-http2; the H2 fixture topology decision was based on 13.1 fixture 0020's pattern (H1-downstream + H1-upstream) plus a single-change-the-cluster-to-H2 substitution that did not anticipate the gate. The implementer reverted all Task 5 artifacts (state restored to HEAD `4ab2c61` — Tasks 1+2+3+4 plus Task 1 + Task 2 fold-ins landed; working tree clean).
- **Options considered:**
  - **(a) Close ADR-0028 — extract `envoy-http2::Client` into a new `envoy-http-client` crate; wire H2 pool dispatch into `envoy_http1::HCMConfig`; drop the 06.3 D14.3 gate.** Architecturally satisfying (closes a phase-05.3 carryforward). Out of scope for 13.2 — requires a new crate + cycle-resolution + foundations grant + an ADR. ~300-400 LoC + new architectural surface. Fragments 13.2's atomic closing-sub-phase scope.
  - **(b) Pivot fixture 0021 topology to downstream HTTP/2 listener + H2 upstream cluster.** The H2-downstream + H2-upstream topology routes through `envoy_http2::HCM` (Task 2's pool integration). The H2 pool's `acquire()` is exercised for each downstream H2 stream, claiming N stream slots on a shared upstream H2 conn. Requires a new harness driver — the existing `Driver::Http1KeepAlive` cannot drive H2 (it writes raw HTTP/1.1 wire bytes). Add `Driver::Http2KeepAlive` + `drive_http2_keep_alive` helper mirroring the 13.1 H1 sibling's shape: open one downstream H2 conn, issue N sequential streams via cloned `SendRequest`, bilateral admin-stat scrape. ~150-200 LoC (driver enum variant + helper + dispatch + test path). Preserves the discriminating-observable bilateral validation needed for credible 06.3 REVIEW I2 (b) closure (Task 4 — already landed at `4ab2c61`).
  - **(c) Defer fixture 0021 entirely; complete Task 5 as docs-only.** The in-process H2 backstop (Task 6) becomes the sole D9 evidence at 13.2; bilateral fixture validation of the value-exact disposition (the 06.3 REVIEW I2 (b) closure surface) is missing at the phase-13 close. Materially weakens the I2 (b) closure attribution at Task 8 (parent-13 close): "the row tightens to value-exact" but no bilateral fixture asserts the tightened value-exact disposition on H2 clusters.
- **Decision: option (b) — H2-downstream-listener + H2-upstream-cluster + new `Driver::Http2KeepAlive` harness driver.**
- **Rationale:** Option (b) is the minimum scope expansion that preserves the discriminating-observable bilateral validation. The H2-downstream + H2-upstream topology DOES exercise the Task 2-integrated H2 pool: each downstream H2 stream lands at `envoy_http2::HCM::handle_one_stream` and dispatches through `config.h2_pool_mgr.get(...).acquire(...)` (the pool path landed at Task 2). The upstream pool reuses its single conn across N streams (the discriminating observable). The new harness driver `Driver::Http2KeepAlive` mirrors `Driver::Http1KeepAlive`'s shape verbatim modulo the codec layer — single-shot driver complexity is already covered by `drive_http2` (lib.rs:1364). A new keep-alive driver adds ~150-200 LoC. Option (a) is correct eventually (the ADR-0028 deferral has been carrying since 05.3) but fragments 13.2's atomic closing-sub-phase scope — fixed in a future phase. Option (c) loses bilateral evidence and weakens the I2 (b) closure attribution materially; rejected. The fixture's discriminating observable shifts slightly: instead of "N H1 keep-alive requests over single downstream H1 conn → 1 upstream conn" (PLAN's shape), it becomes "N H2 multiplexed streams over single downstream H2 conn → 1 upstream H2 conn (N streams shared)" — the bilateral assertion `cluster.backend_cluster.upstream_cx_total: 1` + `cluster.backend_cluster.upstream_cx_http2_total: 1` still discriminates pooled-from-per-call (per-call would yield N, pooled yields 1).
- **Consequences:**
  - **The 13.2 PLAN Task 5 must be reshaped** at the next session's state-3 resumption. The reshaping replaces: (i) fixture 0021's downstream HCM `codec_type: HTTP1` → `HTTP2`; (ii) the expectations.yaml driver kind `http1_keep_alive` → a new `http2_keep_alive` variant; (iii) the harness's `Driver` enum + dispatch gains a new `Http2KeepAlive` variant + the `drive_http2_keep_alive` helper. The fixture's cluster + `circuit_breakers` + the `_h2_echo_backend` discriminator arm + the Docker wrapper test stay unchanged.
  - **The carryforward inventory entering 13.2's Task 5 resumption:** the ADR-0028 deferral (H1-listener × H2-cluster dispatch) remains open, carried forward to a follow-up phase. This ADR explicitly records that the 13.2 lifecycle does NOT close ADR-0028.
  - **PROGRESS Task 5 (when it lands at the next session) attributes:** (a) the PLAN-time miss discovery (the 06.3 D14.3 gate vs. the PLAN's H1-listener × H2-cluster topology); (b) the topology pivot per this ADR; (c) the new `Driver::Http2KeepAlive` driver + helper details; (d) the bilateral discriminating observable preserved under the new topology. The 13.2 Task 5 commit lands BOTH this ADR's referenced topology pivot AND the implementation.
  - **The 13.2 PLAN's "21 Docker-gated fixtures green simultaneously" Task 7 acceptance signal is PRESERVED** — fixture 0021 still lands, just with the reshaped topology.
  - **`DECISIONS.md` ledger head:** **ADR-0039** (this ADR; was the next-available number after ADR-0038 from the parent-13 state-2 split commit `1b1c8f5`). Next available ADR-0040.
  - **STATE.md advances at this commit** to record: Active phase `13.2` state-3-partial / state-3-resume-at-Task-5-next; HEAD `4ab2c61` (Tasks 1+2+3+4 + 2 fold-ins landed); the PLAN-time miss + topology pivot documented; the next session resumes Task 5 with the new driver shape.
  - **No PLAN.md edit at this commit** — the PLAN is an immutable historical artifact per the project's append-only doctrine. The deviation is documented in PROGRESS + this ADR; the next-session implementer reads the ADR + PROGRESS as the source of truth for the reshaped Task 5 scope.
  - **No re-attribution of 06.3 REVIEW I2 (b) closure** — Task 4's BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT row tightening (already landed at `4ab2c61`) stands. The closure attribution at Task 4 + Task 8 (parent-13 close) names the bilateral fixture as fixture 0021 (under the new reshaped topology when it lands).
- **Provenance:** discovered at the 13.2 state-3 Task 5 implementer's first dispatch (the implementer dispatched per `superpowers:subagent-driven-development` with the PLAN's prescribed shape; reported `NEEDS_CONTEXT` with the architectural blocker named). Verified by direct read of `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs:1997-2016` (the 06.3 D14.3 gate) + `crates/envoy-config/src/lib.rs:265-267` (the `ConfigError::Http2ClusterFromHttp1Listener` variant definition). The implementer reverted all Task 5 artifacts cleanly — no orphaned files. This ADR lands as a standalone docs-only commit to ratify the topology pivot decision; the next session's Task 5 dispatch carries it forward as the binding source of truth.

## ADR-0040: Split phase 14 into sub-phases 14.1 and 14.2

- **Date:** 2026-05-25.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Phase 14 (`14-outlier-detection`) — the third concrete Upstream-robustness-family phase (after parent-12 closed at `3ec7fb9` and parent-13 closed at `96630f9`) — was projected as a single phase by the parent-14 state-1 brainstorm SPEC (`docs/envoy-rust/phases/14-outlier-detection/SPEC.md`, committed at HEAD `542e8b5`, the immediate predecessor of this commit). Phase 14 lands passive health checking via outlier detection: an envoy-config `Cluster.outlier_detection` schema (D1) + validator (D2), a per-endpoint `EndpointEjection` state machine sibling to the 12.1 `EndpointHealth` (D3), response-receipt hooks at the H1+H2 router-proxy arms (D4 — the response-receipt site at `crates/envoy-http1/src/router.rs::write_proxied_response` + the H2 sibling at `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs`), a `Cluster::pick()` extension to AND-compose the new ejection-exclusion filter with the existing 12.1 unhealthy-exclusion filter (D5), outlier-detection stats wiring at 5 stat rows + the ratified `cluster.<name>.outlier_detection.*` namespace (D6), a per-cluster ejection sweeper as the **fourth periodic-background primitive** (D7 — mirroring 12.2 + 13.1 + 13.2 cancellation discipline; `outlier.rs` module inside `envoy-cluster` + `OutlierManager` external sibling registry to `ClusterManager`), differential fixture `0022-upstream-outlier-detection-consecutive-5xx` reusing the 13.x configurable-status synthetic backend + the 13.1 `Driver::Http1KeepAlive` driver verbatim (D8.1), a `parse_bootstrap` fuzz seed extending the corpus 21 → 22 (D8.2), and an in-process backstop exercising both ejection AND un-eject convergence directions (D8.3). The parent SPEC §6.1 projected **~1500-2100 LoC** (production ~700, tests ~880, fixture/backstop ~370 + docs) across **~12-16 tasks**. The state-2 PLAN-write evaluated the gate against the locked §6.2 findings (see ADR-0041) and confirmed the surface is **at or over the `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §6.1 ~1500-LoC split gate** (the task-count gate ~12-16 is under ~25; the LoC gate is the binding trigger). The parent SPEC §6.1 explicitly recommended the 14.1 / 14.2 split shape ratified here, and §7 reserved `ADR-0040` for it ("Conditional ADR-0040 (option A — phase split). LIKELY TO FIRE.").
- **Options considered:**
  - **(i) Single phase.** Rejected — the LoC gate fires at projection time (~1500-2100 vs ~1500 cap), with no margin for execution-time drift. The 14-deliverable surface (D1–D9) splits cleanly along a foundation-slice / observable-behavior seam without forcing artificial bundling.
  - **(ii) Three-way split** (14.α = envoy-config schema + validator + fuzz seed; 14.β = `EndpointEjection` + LB integration + stats; 14.γ = response-receipt hooks + sweeper + fixture + parent close). Rejected — the envoy-config schema slice (14.α) is too small (~200-300 LoC) to motivate a full state-machine cycle and is mechanically intertwined with the `EndpointEjection` consumer (the validator's `OutlierDetectionConfig` flows directly into the state machine), mirroring the rationale rejecting the (ii)-3-way option in ADR-0036 (phase-12) and ADR-0038 (phase-13). The same anti-pattern phase-07's ADR-0030 + phase-12's ADR-0036 + phase-13's ADR-0038 all rejected.
  - **(iii) Two-way split by protocol cohesion (H1-side ejection + fixture; H2-side ejection + parent close).** Rejected — outlier detection is **protocol-agnostic** at the state-machine layer per the §6.2 item-8 finding (the `EndpointEjection` + `Cluster::record_response` + the ejection sweeper are all protocol-independent; only the response-receipt hook surface differs between H1 and H2 router arms). Splitting by protocol creates two duplicated state-machine + sweeper slices and fragments the contract surface (the stat namespace is protocol-agnostic; tightening it once on H1 and again on H2 would be artificial).
  - **(iv) Two-way split by foundation-slice cohesion: schema + state machine + LB integration + stats foundation slice (14.1) → response-receipt hooks + sweeper + fixture + parent close (14.2).** Chosen. 14.1 lands the **inert-when-unconfigured foundation** — D1 (schema), D2 (validator), D3 (EndpointEjection state machine), D5 (pick() ejection-filter integration), D6 (stats wiring + BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT extension), AND optionally D8.2 (fuzz seed — recommended at 14.1 per the 14.1 SPEC since the seed exercises the new envoy-config surface 14.1 introduces). NO new fixture; regression-equivalence on the 21 existing fixtures is the acceptance signal (the 05.1 / 07.1 / 12.1 inert-foundation pattern — the `outlier_detection.is_none()` short-circuit at `pick()` keeps the existing 21 fixtures unchanged). ~700-900 LoC. 14.2 lands the **observable-behavior slice + parent close** — D4 (response-receipt hooks on H1+H2 router-proxy arms with synth-status-bypass nuance per ADR-0041 §6.2 item-9), D7 (ejection sweeper — the fourth periodic-background primitive), D8.1 (fixture 0022 reusing the 13.x configurable-status backend + 13.1 `Driver::Http1KeepAlive` verbatim), D8.3 (in-process backstop exercising both convergence directions), parent-14 ROADMAP close (the closing-sub-phase invariant — flips rows `14.2` AND parent `14` `in-progress → done` SIMULTANEOUSLY). ~900-1200 LoC.
  - **(v) Nested split of 14.1 or 14.2.** Rejected — three-level numbering is awkward (`14.1.1`); `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §6.1 flags nested splits of an already-split sub-phase as suspicious. Each sub-phase fits comfortably under the §6.1 gates per the LoC projections above.
- **Decision:** split phase 14 into two sub-phases by foundation-slice cohesion (schema + state machine + LB integration + stats foundation slice → response-receipt hooks + sweeper + fixture + parent close), mirroring phase-05's ADR-0022 (`05.1` foundation + `05.2` observable), phase-07's ADR-0030 (`07.1` foundation + `07.2` filter + fixture), phase-12's ADR-0036 (`12.1` foundation + `12.2` probe + fixture + parent close), AND parent-14's SPEC §6.1 recommendation. **The SEVENTH 2-way split in the project (after phases 02 / 03 / 05 / 07 / 08 / 12).** Phase-13's ADR-0038 picked protocol-cohesion (rejected here); phase-14's seam is foundation-cohesion because outlier-detection is protocol-agnostic at the state-machine layer (§6.2 item-8 finding).
  - **14.1 (`14.1-endpoint-ejection-and-lb-integration`)** — envoy-config OutlierDetection schema + validator + `EndpointEjection` state machine + `Cluster::pick()` AND-composition + outlier-detection stats foundation slice + optional fuzz seed. Deliverables D1 (`OutlierDetection` struct + `Cluster.outlier_detection: Option<OutlierDetection>` at `crates/envoy-config/src/bootstrap.rs`; `deny_unknown_fields` rejects the parent-§4 deferred set) + D2 (validator: ~3-4 `ConfigError` variants — `InvalidOutlierDetectionThreshold`, `InvalidOutlierDetectionTiming`, `InvalidMaxEjectionPercent`) + D3 (`EndpointEjection` state machine at `envoy-cluster` next to 12.1's `EndpointHealth`; declared mutation surface + classifier per ADR-0041 §6.2 item-9; tested via direct unit tests) + D5 (Cluster::pick() AND-compose with !is_ejected() filter; inert when unconfigured) + D6 (7-name minimum-viable subset wired against StatsRegistry per ADR-0041 §6.2 item-2 revision: `ejections_active`, `ejections_enforced_total`, `ejections_overflow`, `ejections_detected_consecutive_5xx`, `ejections_enforced_consecutive_5xx`, `ejections_detected_consecutive_gateway_failure`, `ejections_enforced_consecutive_gateway_failure`) + optional D8.2 (fuzz seed `cluster_outlier_detection.yaml`; corpus 21 → 22). ~700-900 LoC. Depends on `04 06 12`. No new fixture; acceptance = regression-equivalence on the 21 existing Docker-gated fixtures.
  - **14.2 (`14.2-response-receipt-hook-and-fixture`)** — response-receipt hooks (H1+H2 router-proxy arms) + ejection sweeper + fixture 0022 + in-process backstop + parent-14 close. Deliverables D4 (`Cluster::record_response` call sites at `crates/envoy-http1/src/router.rs::write_proxied_response` + `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs` post-dispatch; the connect-failure synth path also calls `record_response` per ADR-0041 §6.2 item-9; the no-healthy-upstream `pick() -> None` synth-503 path does NOT call `record_response`) + D7 (`OutlierEjectionSweeper` + `OutlierManager` external sibling registry — the FOURTH periodic-background primitive mirroring 12.2 + 13.1 + 13.2 cancellation discipline verbatim; `outlier.rs` module inside `envoy-cluster`) + D8.1 (fixture 0022-upstream-outlier-detection-consecutive-5xx reusing 13.x `health-aware-http1-backend` + 13.1 `Driver::Http1KeepAlive` verbatim; per-request status + body + counter bilateral assertions per ADR-0041 §6.2 item-6) + D8.2 (fuzz seed if deferred from 14.1) + D8.3 (in-process backstop exercising both ejection AND un-eject convergence directions per parent-14 SPEC §6.4) + parent-14 close (flips ROADMAP rows `14.2` AND parent `14` `in-progress → done` SIMULTANEOUSLY per the closing-sub-phase invariant). ~900-1200 LoC. Depends on `14.1`.
- **Rationale:** the 2-way foundation-cohesion split mirrors the 12.1 / 12.2 + 05.1 / 05.2 + 07.1 / 07.2 split shapes verbatim. The split boundary is by **deliverable cohesion via the inert-foundation axis** (schema + state machine + LB integration + stats are atomic and INERT when `outlier_detection` is unconfigured → 21 existing fixtures stay green), NOT by code area (a 14.α envoy-config-only slice was rejected as too thin) and NOT by protocol cohesion (the state machine + sweeper are protocol-agnostic per §6.2 item-8; per-protocol splitting fragments the contract surface and duplicates work). Failure attribution localizes per sub-phase: 14.1's REVIEW.md owns the schema + state machine + LB integration verdict; 14.2's REVIEW.md owns the response-receipt hook + sweeper + fixture verdict + the parent-14 close attribution. **Sub-phase ordering invariant: 14.1 → 14.2 cannot be parallelized** because (a) 14.2's D4 calls `Cluster::record_response` which 14.1 D3 declares; (b) 14.2's D7 sweeper iterates over the 14.1-landed `EndpointEjection` per-endpoint metadata; (c) 14.2's D8.1 fixture exercises the end-to-end seam (schema → validator → state machine → response-receipt → sweeper → un-eject); (d) 14.2 closes parent-14.
- **Consequences:** parent SPEC at `docs/envoy-rust/phases/14-outlier-detection/SPEC.md` was committed at parent-14 state-1 (`542e8b5`, the predecessor of this commit) projecting this split per parent-SPEC §6.1 + §7 + §8; sub-phase SPECs (`14.1-endpoint-ejection-and-lb-integration/SPEC.md`, `14.2-response-receipt-hook-and-fixture/SPEC.md`) land alongside this ADR in this same parent-14 state-2 split commit. ROADMAP gains 2 new rows (`14.1`, `14.2`) at this commit; row `14`'s `sub-phases` column updates from `—` to `14.1, 14.2`; row `14`'s `status` flips `planned` → `in-progress` at this commit. Sub-phases ship strictly in order (14.1 → 14.2) — they cannot be parallelized per the rationale above. Parent ROADMAP row `14` flips `done` at sub-phase 14.2's state-6 phase-done commit, mirroring the phase-02/03/05/07/08/12/13 closing-sub-phase precedents. STATE.md advances at this commit to point at `14.1` lifecycle **state 2** (next-skill `superpowers:writing-plans` scoped to the 14.1 SPEC) — the parent state-2 split commit lands each sub-phase's SPEC and advances STATE.md to the first sub-phase's state 2 directly (skipping 14.1 state 1 brainstorm) because the parent-14 SPEC already brainstormed the full surface and the sub-phase SPECs are surface-slice carve-outs derived from it (mirrors the parent-07 `6db5a01` + parent-08 `56dee82` + parent-12 `4f9ba04` + parent-13 split-commit precedents). **Phase 14's projected ADR ledger after this commit:** ADR-0040 (this ADR) + ADR-0041 (the §6.2 empirical-verification revision — landing inline at this same commit). Both ADRs land in this single parent-14 state-2 split commit per parent SPEC §7 ("Numbered `ADR-0041` if the split ADR took `ADR-0040`. **Recommended posture: verify all 9; land the revision ADR if any differ.**" — the §6.2 stat-namespace divergence + connect-failure classification finding both qualify as material differences).
- **Provenance:** projected as the next-sequential available ADR number in parent-14 SPEC §7 ("Conditional ADR-0040 (option A — phase split). LIKELY TO FIRE."). The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0039** (the phase-13.2 Task 5 fixture topology pivot, `e2b8d1b`); ADR-0040 lands at the next-sequential number with no renumbering (no inter-ADR landings between phase-13.2 close at `96630f9` and this commit — the phase-14 state-1 brainstorm `542e8b5` landed no ADR). Like phases 05 / 07 / 12, phase-14's projected split-ADR number matches the actual landed number.

## ADR-0041: Phase-14 §6.2 empirical-verification revision — outlier-detection stat-namespace expansion + connect-failure classification

- **Date:** 2026-05-25.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Phase 14 (`14-outlier-detection`)'s state-1 brainstorm SPEC §2.1 projected a **5-name minimum-viable subset** under `cluster.<name>.outlier_detection.*` (`ejections_consecutive_5xx`, `ejections_consecutive_gateway_failure`, `ejections_enforced_total`, `ejections_overflow`, `ejections_active`) and §5.7 projected a uniform **synth-status-bypass** discipline on the response-receipt hook (envoy-rust-side synth-502/503 paths do not call `Cluster::record_response`). The parent-14 SPEC §6.2 mandated empirical verification at state-2 PLAN-write time against `envoyproxy/envoy:v1.33.0` for 9 items. The verification (executed at THIS commit's prologue per the parent-14 state-2 PLAN-write workflow) surfaced **two material divergences** from the SPEC's projection: (a) Envoy emits **21 names** under the namespace (not 5), with per-detector-type counters split into `_detected_` AND `_enforced_` pairs (e.g. `ejections_detected_consecutive_5xx` + `ejections_enforced_consecutive_5xx`) and additional siblings for the deferred detectors + legacy aliases (`ejections_total`, `ejections_consecutive_5xx`, `ejections_success_rate`); and (b) connect-failure synth-status paths in Envoy are **classified as BOTH `consecutive_5xx` AND `consecutive_gateway_failure`** for the picked endpoint that failed — the synth-status bypass per parent §5.7 is NUANCED: it applies ONLY to the `pick() -> None` no-healthy-upstream synth-503 (no endpoint attribution), NOT to connect-failure synth paths (picked endpoint, classified-as-5xx-AND-gateway-failure). The remaining 7 items matched the SPEC's projection. Per parent SPEC §7 ("Conditional ADR-0041 (option B — §6.2 empirical-verification revision). PLAUSIBLE. ... Numbered `ADR-0041` if the split ADR took `ADR-0040`. **Recommended posture: verify all 9; land the revision ADR if any differ.**"), this ADR records the divergence + ratifies the revised projection. Mirrors ADR-0034 (phase-10 §6.2 revision per upstream Envoy empirical observation) + ADR-0037 (phase-12 §6.2 no-healthy-upstream-body reconciliation) precedents verbatim.
- **Options considered:**
  - **(a) Track Envoy's full 21-name emission set verbatim.** Rejected — most names (success_rate_*, failure_percentage_*, local_origin_*) correspond to detectors envoy-rust does NOT implement at phase-14 minimum-viable scope (out per parent §4: success-rate-based, failure-percentage-based, `consecutive_local_origin_failure`, `enforcing_*`). Emitting them at-zero would mislead operators into thinking the detectors run. The legacy aliases (`ejections_total`, `ejections_consecutive_5xx`, `ejections_success_rate`) are Envoy-specific compatibility shims with no analog in envoy-rust's clean-slate codebase.
  - **(b) Stick with the SPEC's 5-name projection and accept the fixture-0022 expectations.yaml `allowlist_envoy_only` covers the missing names.** Rejected — the SPEC's 5 names DO NOT MATCH Envoy's emission set (Envoy emits `ejections_detected_consecutive_5xx` + `ejections_enforced_consecutive_5xx`, NOT a single `ejections_consecutive_5xx`; the SPEC's projected name would be ambiguous — is it the detected or enforced variant?). The bilateral assertion would falsify silently (envoy-rust emits its made-up name; Envoy emits its actual names; the harness might not surface the mismatch if both sides allow-list each other's unique names blindly). The differential contract demands envoy-rust emit the same NAMES Envoy emits for the metrics envoy-rust DOES implement.
  - **(c) Emit a `7-name minimum-viable subset` matching Envoy's per-detector-type breakdown for the two supported detectors (consecutive_5xx + consecutive_gateway_failure).** Chosen. Set: `ejections_active` (gauge), `ejections_enforced_total`, `ejections_overflow`, `ejections_detected_consecutive_5xx`, `ejections_enforced_consecutive_5xx`, `ejections_detected_consecutive_gateway_failure`, `ejections_enforced_consecutive_gateway_failure`. The fixture-0022 expectations.yaml uses `allowlist_envoy_only` for the 14 Envoy-side names envoy-rust does NOT emit (the deferred-detector counters + legacy aliases).
  - **(d) Uniform synth-status bypass (parent §5.7 verbatim).** Rejected — the §6.2 item-9 finding showed Envoy classifies connect-failures as both 5xx AND gateway-failure for the picked endpoint. Following the SPEC's projection verbatim would make envoy-rust UNDER-COUNT ejections for clusters where connect-failures matter (e.g., flaky upstream networks). The fixture-0022 expectations.yaml would then assert envoy-rust's missing counter ticks, falsifying the bilateral signal.
  - **(e) Nuanced synth-status bypass — connect-failure synth paths DO call `record_response` (classified as 5xx + gateway-failure for the picked endpoint); the `pick() -> None` no-healthy-upstream synth-503 path does NOT call `record_response` (no endpoint to attribute).** Chosen.
- **Decision:** Adopt options (c) + (e). The parent-14 SPEC's §2.1 projected 5-name set is REVISED to the 7-name minimum-viable subset; the §5.7 synth-status bypass projection is REVISED to apply ONLY to `pick() -> None` synth-503 (not connect-failure synth paths). The revision is enacted in the two sub-phase SPECs landing at THIS same commit: 14.1 SPEC §2.1 + §6.2 lock-ins (the 7-name subset, the connect-failure classifier in `EndpointEjection::record_response`) + 14.2 SPEC §2.3 (the response-receipt hook nuance — connect-failure synth path calls `record_response`; no-healthy-upstream synth path does not).
- **Rationale:** option (c) preserves the differential contract's name-equivalence requirement (envoy-rust emits the same NAMES Envoy emits for metrics envoy-rust implements) while keeping the deferred-detector scope honest (envoy-rust does not emit metrics for unimplemented detectors). The per-detector-type breakdown matches Envoy's v3 proto-design (the `_detected_` vs `_enforced_` distinction is load-bearing for the `enforcing_*` knobs that defer per parent §4 — at minimum-viable scope `enforced == detected` modulo the max-ejection-percent overflow). Option (e) preserves the differential contract on ejection counter values (envoy-rust's response-receipt classifier matches Envoy's): connect-failure synth-status from a picked endpoint contributes to ejections (matches Envoy); `pick() -> None` synth-503 does not (no endpoint to attribute — the orthogonal property holding regardless of envoy-rust's design choice). The state-3 implementer (14.2 D4) explicitly wires the bypass at the router-arm dispatch site (the connect-failure path calls `record_response`; the no-healthy-upstream path does not). Mirrors the ADR-0037 reconciliation discipline (phase-12 §6.2 reconciled the SPEC's empty-body no-healthy-upstream synth-503 to Envoy's 19-byte `no healthy upstream` body — the same pattern of empirical-finding-driving-SPEC-revision-via-inline-ADR).
- **Consequences:**
  - **Phase-14 sub-phase SPECs are written to the revised projection.** 14.1 SPEC §2.1 emits the 7-name minimum-viable subset; 14.1 D6 (stats wiring) registers exactly these 7 names. 14.2 SPEC §2.3 documents the connect-failure classifier; 14.2 D4 (response-receipt hooks) wires the bypass nuance.
  - **Fixture 0022 expectations.yaml shape** (lands at 14.2 D8.1): bilateral assertions on the 7 envoy-rust-emitted names; `allowlist_envoy_only` for the 14 Envoy-only names (the deferred-detector counters + legacy aliases). The fixture's per-request status sequence + body bytes are captured per §6.2 item-6 (request 1-3: backend 500 + `server error\n` body + `x-envoy-upstream-service-time` PRESENT; request 4: synth-503 + `no healthy upstream` body + `x-envoy-upstream-service-time` ABSENT).
  - **The §6.2 9-item findings are NOT re-verified at the 14.1 or 14.2 state-2 PLAN-write commits** — they are locked here. The 14.1 + 14.2 PLAN-writers pull forward the relevant lock-ins per the SPEC §6.2 subsections.
  - **Other §6.2 findings ratified (no SPEC revision needed; the SPEC's projection matched empirically):** Item 1 (config defaults 5/5/10s/30s/10 confirmed); Item 3 (initial state: implicitly never-ejected, no warmup); Item 4 (max_ejection_percent: `floor(N*pct/100)` cap with per-detection-tick overflow re-fire); Item 5 (ejection-time: un-eject at next interval-tick after `now - eject_time >= base_ejection_time`; per-endpoint counters reset on un-eject AND on 2xx/3xx/4xx); Item 6 (fixture observable — already covered above); Item 7 (composition with active HC — AND-composed via independent health-flag bits at the `pick()` site); Item 8 (H1 vs H2 sibling parity — identical stat namespace + same ejection-on-5xx semantics; the H2 router-arm hook fires at the H2 HCM post-dispatch site).
  - **`* num_ejections` multiplier finding (§6.2 item-5 deeper dive):** Envoy's docs mention `base_ejection_time * num_ejections` for repeated ejections; the empirical capture showed the multiplier exists but the effective ejection-duration ≈ `base_ejection_time` at low repeat counts. Out per parent §4; envoy-rust does NOT implement the multiplier at phase-14 scope.
  - **`DECISIONS.md` ledger head:** **ADR-0041** (this ADR; was the next-available number after ADR-0040 from the SAME parent-14 state-2 split commit). Both ADRs land in the SAME commit per parent SPEC §7's explicit numbering guidance — the only project-wide instance of TWO ADRs in a single commit, justified by their tight architectural coupling (ADR-0040 ratifies the split shape; ADR-0041 ratifies the §6.2 findings that the sub-phase SPECs encode). Next available ADR-0042. Project-cumulative ADR count 42.
  - **STATE.md advances at this commit** to record: Active phase `14.1` state-2-next; HEAD = this parent-14 state-2 split commit; both ADRs documented in the new `### Phase-14 state-2 split decision` Notes subsection.
- **Provenance:** §6.2 9-item empirical verification dispatched as a single general-purpose subagent foreground task at the parent-14 state-2 PLAN-write workflow's prologue. Methodology: Docker bridge networking with `envoyproxy/envoy:v1.33.0` + the project's existing `tests/helpers/health-aware-http1-backend/` Rust helper (`--per-path /fail=500`) + curl/python data-plane drivers + admin `/stats?filter=outlier_detection` scrapes + `/stats/prometheus` for TYPE info + `/clusters` for health-flag visibility. The subagent verified all 9 items + returned a structured findings report; 2 of 9 items surfaced material divergences (Items 2 + 9). This ADR ratifies the revised projection landing in the two sub-phase SPECs at THIS same commit.

## ADR-0042: Phase-15 minimum-viable circuit-breaker scope — `max_connections` enforcement already landed at phase 13; phase 15 delivers OBSERVABILITY + overflow differential coverage + the `max_pending_requests: 0` no-queue carve-out

- **Date:** 2026-05-31.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Phase 15 (`15-circuit-breakers`) is the **fourth concrete Upstream-robustness-family phase** (after parent-12 active HTTP health checking closed at `3ec7fb9`, parent-13 connection pooling closed at `96630f9`, parent-14 outlier detection closed at `b575bdc35`). The `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §9 charter line ("circuit breakers") and the phase-14.2-close planning-boundary note (STATE.md at HEAD `b575bdc35`) framed circuit breakers as *"`max_connections` enforcement — currently parsed but NOT enforced"* and projected phase 15 as a likely-single enforcement phase. **The phase-15 state-1 brainstorm verified the code directly and found this framing FALSE at HEAD `b575bdc35`:** the `max_connections` cap is ALREADY ENFORCED, landed at phase 13. Specifically: (i) `H1Pool::acquire` (`crates/envoy-http1/src/pool.rs:200-211`) checks `if *n >= self.max_connections { return Err(PoolError::Overflow { cluster, max }) }` on the connect-on-miss branch (per-endpoint `established: HashMap<SocketAddr, u32>`), and `H2Pool::acquire` (`crates/envoy-http2/src/pool.rs:304-314`) mirrors it after stream-slot exhaustion; (ii) the cap is config-sourced — `H1PoolManager::for_bootstrap` (`pool.rs:358-363`) extracts it via `cfg.circuit_breakers.as_ref().and_then(|cb| cb.thresholds.first()).and_then(|t| t.max_connections).unwrap_or(DEFAULT_MAX_CONNECTIONS)` (`DEFAULT_MAX_CONNECTIONS = 1024`), and `H2PoolManager::for_bootstrap` (`pool.rs:507-512`) mirrors it; (iii) both router arms already reject overflow with a synth-503 — H1 at `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:542`, H2 at `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs:368-380`; (iv) the schema (`bootstrap.rs:1170-1196`) + validator (`validate_circuit_breakers` at `bootstrap.rs:2583-2613`, with `UnsupportedMultipleCircuitBreakerThresholds` / `UnsupportedCircuitBreakerPriority` / `InvalidMaxConnections`) all landed at 13.1. **What is MISSING:** (1) envoy-rust emits NONE of Envoy's circuit-breaker observability stat tree; (2) NO differential fixture trips the cap (fixture 0020 sets `max_connections: 4` but its sequential single-connection `Driver::Http1KeepAlive` workload never needs >1 concurrent upstream connection, so it never overflows); (3) Envoy queues on cx-overflow by default (`max_pending_requests` default 1024) whereas envoy-rust rejects immediately, so a naive overflow fixture would DIVERGE. This ADR settles the genuine scoping ambiguity the finding creates: given enforcement is done, WHAT does phase 15 deliver, and where is the minimum-viable scope boundary? Per `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §1 ("disk is authoritative if it disagrees" with the planning note) + D-3.4 (cold-readability — a future session must understand WHY phase 15 is observability-not-enforcement) + D-3.5 (decisions are written, not remembered), the finding + the scope boundary are recorded here rather than left implicit in the SPEC.
- **Options considered:**
  - **(a) Treat phase 15 as a no-op / skip it** (enforcement is done; nothing to do). Rejected — there IS a real, well-bounded deliverable surface (observability stats + differential coverage of the overflow path + the no-queue carve-out). Skipping would leave the `max_connections` circuit breaker UNTESTED against Envoy (the overflow→503 wire shape + the `upstream_cx_overflow` counter are differentially unverified) and UNOBSERVABLE (operators get no `upstream_cx_overflow` / `cx_open` signal), violating the mission's differential-equivalence charter for an implemented surface.
  - **(b) Re-implement `max_connections` enforcement "properly" (per-cluster instead of per-endpoint) + the full circuit-breaker threshold set (`max_pending_requests` queue, `max_requests`, `max_retries`).** Rejected — over-scoped. The per-endpoint cap is a working 13.x design choice that is bilaterally correct for single-endpoint clusters; reconciling it to per-cluster + adding the pending-request queue + `max_requests`/`max_retries` is a multi-phase surface (the queue alone needs `upstream_rq_pending_*` machinery + a queueing scheduler) that blows past the §6.1 split gate and bundles unrelated breakers. The established minimum-viable-scope cadence (ADR-0036/0037/0038/0039/0040/0041 for phases 12/13/14) carves the thinnest coherent slice.
  - **(c) Deliver observability + overflow differential coverage + the `max_pending_requests: 0` no-queue carve-out; defer the rest.** Chosen. Phase 15 adds (1) two minimum-viable circuit-breaker stats — `cluster.<name>.upstream_cx_overflow` (counter, at the pool cap-check site) + `cluster.<name>.circuit_breakers.default.cx_open` (gauge, edge-driven at the `established`-count cap edges) — the 14.1 "emit the names Envoy emits for the breaker you enforce; allow-list the rest" precedent; (2) the project's first CONCURRENT differential driver (`Driver::Http1Concurrent` + a `--hold-ms` backend knob) to force concurrent upstream-connection demand > cap, since every existing driver is sequential and cannot trip the cap; (3) a schema carve-out accepting `max_pending_requests: 0` ONLY (the no-queue config that makes Envoy reject overflow immediately, matching envoy-rust's existing behavior) while rejecting `max_pending_requests > 0` (the queue) — this is what makes the fixture bilaterally green WITHOUT implementing the deferred pending-request queue; (4) fixture `0023-upstream-circuit-breaker-max-connections` (K=2 concurrent / cap=1, single-endpoint cluster) proving the `{200, 503}` status multiset + `upstream_cx_overflow: 1` bilaterally. Projected single un-split phase (~950–1300 LoC).
- **Decision:** adopt option (c). Phase 15's minimum-viable scope boundary is: **deliver `upstream_cx_overflow` + `circuit_breakers.default.cx_open` observability + the `max_pending_requests: 0` no-queue carve-out + fixture 0023 (concurrent overflow) + the conditional overflow-503-body reconciliation.** DEFER: the `max_pending_requests > 0` pending-request queue (+ `rq_pending_open` / `upstream_rq_pending_*` stats); `max_requests` (+ `rq_open`); `max_retries` / `retry_budget` (+ `rq_retry_open`); `max_connection_pools` (+ `cx_pool_open`); `track_remaining: true` (+ the `remaining_*` gauges); non-DEFAULT `RoutingPriority` (the `circuit_breakers.high.*` namespace); and the per-endpoint-vs-per-cluster `cx_open`/`max_connections` reconciliation (sidestepped by fixture 0023's single-endpoint topology). Each deferred item is enumerated in the phase-15 SPEC §4.
- **Rationale:** option (c) preserves the mission's differential-equivalence charter (the implemented `max_connections` breaker becomes both observable AND differentially verified) while honoring the established minimum-viable-scope cadence (the thinnest coherent slice; ADR-0036→0041 precedent). The `max_pending_requests: 0` carve-out is the load-bearing equivalence enabler — it lets envoy-rust's existing immediate-503-on-overflow behavior match Envoy WITHOUT building the pending-request queue, exactly mirroring how prior phases carved minimum-viable boundaries (e.g. phase-12's HTTP-only health checks rejecting TCP/gRPC checkers; phase-14's consecutive-5xx/gateway-failure detectors rejecting success-rate/failure-percentage). Recording the enforcement-already-landed finding HERE (vs only in the SPEC) corrects the durable planning record: a future cold-start session reading the §9 charter ("circuit breakers") + the STATE history would otherwise reasonably assume phase 15 implements enforcement and be confused that the pool already enforces — D-3.4 cold-readability demands the correction be a written, ADR-numbered, append-only record. The brainstorm-time ADR is a cadence DEPARTURE from phases 12/13/14 (whose brainstorm SPECs landed no ADR — the split + §6.2 ADRs landed at PLAN-write); the departure is justified because the scope finding is non-obvious AND contradicts the charter framing, the exact situation D-3.5 ("decisions are written, not remembered") exists to capture.
- **Consequences:**
  - **Phase-15 SPEC is written to the option-(c) scope.** `docs/envoy-rust/phases/15-circuit-breakers/SPEC.md` lands alongside this ADR at the state-1 brainstorm commit, with §0 (the critical scoping finding), §2.1 (the 2-stat observability subset), §2.3 (the `max_pending_requests: 0` carve-out), §3 (D1–D9), and §4 (the deferral list). The ROADMAP gains row `15` (`status: planned`) beneath the "Upstream robustness family" heading at this commit.
  - **No code changes at this commit** — the brainstorm is docs-only (SPEC + ROADMAP row + this ADR + STATE advance). Implementation is the state-3 work of a LATER session (subagent-driven per `feedback_execution_style`).
  - **Conditional follow-on ADRs reserved** (phase-15 SPEC §7): **ADR-0043** (PLAUSIBLE — §6.2 empirical-verification revision, fires if Envoy's `max_pending_requests: 0` behavior or the overflow-503 body bytes diverge from the projection; mirrors ADR-0037/0034 body-reconciliations) lands at the state-2 PLAN-write if it fires; **ADR-0044** (UNLIKELY — phase split, fires only if the state-2 LoC estimate exceeds ~1500) reserved.
  - **`DECISIONS.md` ledger head:** **ADR-0042** (this ADR). Next available **ADR-0043**. Project-cumulative ADR count 43.
  - **STATE.md advances at this commit** to record: Active phase `15` state-1-complete / state-2-next (next-skill `superpowers:writing-plans` scoped to the phase-15 SPEC); the prior "awaiting next planning" status demoted to a `_Historical_` line; this ADR + the scope finding documented in the new `### Phase-15 state-1 brainstorm` Notes subsection.
- **Provenance:** the enforcement-already-landed finding was surfaced at the phase-15 state-1 brainstorm via a read-only `Explore` subagent survey of the circuit-breaker code foundation + direct controller verification of `crates/envoy-http1/src/pool.rs:177-398` (the `acquire` cap-check + `for_bootstrap` config-sourcing) and `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:525-559` (the overflow→503 router arm). The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0041** (the phase-14 §6.2 empirical-verification revision, `e2b8d1b`-lineage, landed at the parent-14 state-2 split commit); ADR-0042 lands at the next-sequential number with no renumbering (no inter-ADR landings between the phase-14.2 close at `b575bdc35` and this commit — the phase-14.2 close-out landed no ADR). Per D-3.5 this ADR lands inline at the state-1 brainstorm commit alongside the SPEC it scopes.

## ADR-0043: Phase-15 §6.2 empirical-verification revision — `max_pending_requests:0` rejects ALL requests (not just over-cap) via `upstream_rq_pending_overflow`; overflow-503 body is the 81-byte Envoy "reset reason: overflow" local reply; re-scope phase 15 around the pending-overflow reject path

- **Date:** 2026-05-31.
- **Status:** accepted. **Partially supersedes ADR-0042** — narrows the §2.3 `max_pending_requests:0` carve-out model and the §2.1 star-stat; the ADR-0042 observability charter (`upstream_cx_overflow` + `circuit_breakers.default.cx_open`) is RETAINED.
- **Context:** Phase 15 (`15-circuit-breakers`) is at lifecycle state 2 (SPEC.md exists; PLAN.md being written). Per the phase-10/11/12/13/14-ratified verify-at-PLAN-write process + the phase-15 SPEC §6.2 (which flagged the empirical verification as HEAVY for this phase and explicitly reserved ADR-0043 as PLAUSIBLE), a foreground general-purpose subagent ran `envoyproxy/envoy:v1.33.0` under Docker (sibling-container topology on a user-defined bridge network — `host.docker.internal` resolved to an unreachable IPv6 on the macOS Docker Desktop host, so a Python slow-backend container was used) against a STRICT_DNS single-endpoint cluster with `circuit_breakers.thresholds[0]: { max_connections: 1, max_pending_requests: 0 }` + a `%RESPONSE_FLAGS%` stdout access log, driving K=2 concurrent / K=1 single / sequential workloads + admin `/stats` scrapes. **The verification surfaced THREE material divergences from the SPEC §2.1/§2.2/§2.3 projection — the SPEC's central mental model is empirically FALSE:**
  - **(finding 1) `max_pending_requests:0` rejects EVERY request, not just the over-cap one.** A single (K=1) request 503s; K=2 → BOTH 503; sequential requests 503 indefinitely; the connection pool NEVER warms; the backend receives ZERO requests; `upstream_cx_total` stays `0`. Envoy treats establishing the upstream connection itself as a "pending" operation requiring a pending-queue slot — with `max_pending_requests:0` there is no slot, so the very first cold connect is rejected before any TCP connection is attempted. The SPEC §1/§2.3 projected "K=2 → one 200 (acquired the single connection) + one 503 (overflowed the cap)." **There is no served 200 — it is an effective total outage for an on-demand-connection cluster.**
  - **(finding 2) The live overflow counter is `cluster.<name>.upstream_rq_pending_overflow` (= number of rejected requests), NOT `cluster.<name>.upstream_cx_overflow`.** Under `max_pending_requests:0`, `upstream_cx_overflow` stays `0` (no connection demand reaches the cap — the pending-reject fires first) and `circuit_breakers.default.cx_open` stays `0` (the cluster is never AT the connection cap because it never opens a connection). The SPEC §2.1 made `upstream_cx_overflow:1` the discriminating observable; empirically it is `0`, and `upstream_rq_pending_overflow:N` is the live signal. (Control config — same cluster, `max_pending_requests` REMOVED → Envoy default 1024 — K=2: BOTH served 200 after the 2s hold, with `upstream_cx_overflow:1` + `upstream_rq_pending_overflow:0` + `upstream_cx_total:2`: the cap-overflow connection demand is COUNTED in `upstream_cx_overflow` but then QUEUED as pending and served. This confirms the two-counter split: `upstream_cx_overflow` = cap-hit count [request may still be served via the queue]; `upstream_rq_pending_overflow` = pending-queue-exhaustion reject count [request is 503'd].)
  - **(finding 3, resolving the SPEC §2.2 open question) The overflow-503 body is NON-EMPTY.** Exactly `upstream connect error or disconnect/reset before headers. reset reason: overflow` (**81 bytes**, no trailing newline; last byte `0x77` `w`), with response header `x-envoy-overloaded: true`. Full header set: `{x-envoy-overloaded: true, content-length: 81, content-type: text/plain, date: …, server: envoy}` — NO `connection` header, NO `x-envoy-upstream-service-time` (no upstream contacted). The `UO` (UpstreamOverflow) response flag is **access-log-only** (`flags=UO` in the `%RESPONSE_FLAGS%` log); on the wire it surfaces as the `x-envoy-overloaded: true` header. The SPEC §2.2 was unsure empty-vs-non-empty and reserved D5 conditional on the finding; it is non-empty → D5 FIRES.
  - **(findings 4–7, MATCHING the projection — no revision)** `cx_open` is an inclusive at-cap gauge (`cx_open=1` exactly when `upstream_cx_active == max_connections`, returns to `0` after drain — confirmed via the control config which actually opens a connection); `max_connections` is per-cluster/per-priority (the stat namespace `cluster.<name>.circuit_breakers.<priority>.*` carries no host component — the single-endpoint fixture makes per-cluster == per-endpoint, the bilateral-safe topology); the full always-emitted `circuit_breakers.*` gauge set is 10 names (`{default,high}.{cx_open, cx_pool_open, rq_open, rq_pending_open, rq_retry_open}` — both priorities emitted regardless of config); the overflow-rejected request does NOT increment `upstream_cx_total` (matches the existing `hcm.rs:542` comment). These land as plain PLAN lock-ins.
- **The genuine scoping problem the finding creates.** The SPEC's bilateral fixture (`{200, 503}` status multiset under `max_pending_requests:0`) is NOT achievable: under `max_pending_requests:0` Envoy serves `{503, 503}` while envoy-rust (which connects inline with no pending-queue concept) would serve `{200, 503}` for K=2 (the first request connects + serves; the second hits the cap + 503s). And under DEFAULT `max_pending_requests` Envoy serves `{200, 200}` (queue absorbs the overflow) while envoy-rust serves `{200, 503}`. **There is NO `max_connections` config under which the two proxies produce an identical response-status multiset on the overflow path** without envoy-rust implementing the pending-request QUEUE (the wait-until-a-connection-frees machinery the SPEC §4 + ADR-0042 explicitly defer). The differential mission (D-3.3) + the green-build doctrine (D-3.6) therefore forbid the SPEC's fixture as written.
- **Options considered:**
  - **(a) Ship the SPEC's fixture as written** (K=2 concurrent, `max_pending_requests:0`, assert `{200,503}` + `upstream_cx_overflow:1`). Rejected — empirically RED on both axes (Envoy serves `{503,503}` not `{200,503}`; `upstream_cx_overflow` is `0` not `1`). Violates D-3.6.
  - **(b) Drop the differential fixture entirely; ship phase 15 as an observability-foundation-only slice** (the 05.1/07.1/12.1/14.1 inert-foundation pattern): wire `upstream_cx_overflow` + `cx_open`, validate via in-process backstop + inert-0 assertions on existing fixtures, defer ALL bilateral overflow coverage to a future pending-queue phase. Rejected as the PRIMARY path (held as the fallback, see Consequences) — it abandons ADR-0042's "overflow differential coverage" charter when a robust bilateral fixture IS achievable (option c).
  - **(c) Implement `max_pending_requests:0` FAITHFULLY (reject-on-establish) and build the bilateral fixture around the pending-overflow reject path.** Chosen. envoy-rust's H1/H2 pool `acquire()` gains a `max_pending_requests` field (default 1024 = behave-as-today); on the connect-on-miss path, BEFORE the `max_connections` cap-check, if `max_pending_requests == 0` it increments `cluster.<name>.upstream_rq_pending_overflow` and returns a new `PoolError::PendingOverflow` → the router arm synthesizes the reconciled overflow-503. This exactly reproduces Envoy's `max_pending_requests:0` behavior (every cold/at-cap connect-on-miss rejected; pool never warms; `upstream_cx_total:0`; `upstream_cx_overflow:0`). Fixture `0023` then drives a SINGLE GET against a `max_pending_requests:0` cluster → BOTH proxies 503 with the byte-exact 81-byte overflow body + `x-envoy-overloaded: true` + `upstream_rq_pending_overflow:1` — **timing-robust (no concurrency, no slow backend, zero timing sensitivity)**, strictly more robust than the SPEC's concurrent design. The `upstream_cx_overflow` counter + `cx_open` gauge (ADR-0042's named observability) are RETAINED and validated (inert-0 bilaterally on fixtures 0020 + 0023; non-zero in the in-process backstop's cap=1/K=2-concurrent/default-pending path, which is envoy-rust-internal because Envoy serves `{200,200}` there — that path stays differentially deferred with the queue).
  - **(d) Implement the full pending-request QUEUE** so envoy-rust matches Envoy's default-pending queue-and-serve behavior, enabling a `{200,200}` bilateral fixture. Rejected — over-scoped (the queue + `upstream_rq_pending_active`/`upstream_rq_pending_total`/`rq_pending_open` machinery + a queueing scheduler), explicitly deferred by ADR-0042 §4; blows the §6.1 split gate.
- **Decision:** adopt option (c). Phase 15's re-scoped deliverable surface: **(1)** `envoy-config` `Thresholds.max_pending_requests: Option<u32>` schema + a validator that accepts `0`/absent and rejects `> 0` (`ConfigError::UnsupportedNonZeroMaxPendingRequests` — the `> 0` queue defers); **(2)** a faithful `max_pending_requests:0` reject-on-establish gate in the H1 + H2 pools + the `cluster.<name>.upstream_rq_pending_overflow` counter; **(3)** the ADR-0042 `cluster.<name>.upstream_cx_overflow` counter + `cluster.<name>.circuit_breakers.default.cx_open` gauge (RETAINED — at-cap inclusive gauge per finding 4; edge-driven, inert-when-unconfigured); **(4)** the overflow-503 wire reconciliation — a `synth_overflow()` helper emitting the byte-exact 81-byte `upstream connect error or disconnect/reset before headers. reset reason: overflow` body + `x-envoy-overloaded: true` header, wired into BOTH the pending-overflow reject arm AND the existing cap-overflow arm, on H1 AND H2 (the H2 arm currently mis-synthesizes a **502** via `synth_h2_502` — a PLAN-time-discovered pre-existing divergence contradicting ADR-0042 §0's "both router arms already reject overflow with a synth-503"; corrected to 503 here); **(5)** bilateral fixture `0023-upstream-circuit-breaker-max-pending-requests` (single GET, `max_pending_requests:0`); **(6)** fixture-0020 inert-0 assertions on the two new gauges/counters; **(7)** an in-process backstop validating both the pending-overflow path (single request → 503 + 81-byte body + `rq_pending_overflow:1`) AND the cx-overflow path (cap=1/K=2 concurrent → `{200,503}` + `upstream_cx_overflow:1` + `cx_open` both edges). DEFER (unchanged from ADR-0042 §4): the `max_pending_requests > 0` pending-request QUEUE (+ `rq_pending_open`/`upstream_rq_pending_active`/`upstream_rq_pending_total`); `max_requests`/`max_retries`/`track_remaining`/multi-priority; and the multi-endpoint per-cluster `cx_open` reconciliation. **The bilateral `{200,200}` queue-and-serve overflow fixture defers to the future pending-queue phase** (named owner = the deferred `max_pending_requests>0` work).
- **Rationale:** option (c) preserves the differential-equivalence charter (a GREEN bilateral fixture proves the overflow-reject wire shape + the pending-overflow counter byte-for-byte) AND keeps ADR-0042's observability deliverable, at modest added cost (the reject-on-establish gate + one counter, ~80 LoC over the foundation-only fallback). It is FAITHFUL to Envoy — `max_pending_requests:0` genuinely is a reject-all-establish config in upstream Envoy; envoy-rust now reproduces it exactly rather than papering over the divergence. The timing-robustness (single GET, no concurrency, no slow backend) is a strict improvement over the SPEC's concurrency-tuned design, eliminating the §5.5 flakiness vector the SPEC itself flagged. Recording the finding + re-scope HERE (vs silently mutating the ratified SPEC) honors D-3.4 (a future session must understand WHY the fixture tests the pending-overflow reject and not the cx-overflow `{200,503}` multiset) + D-3.5 (decisions are written). This mirrors ADR-0037 (phase-12 §6.2 no-healthy-upstream-body reconciliation) + ADR-0034 (phase-10 RBAC-body) + ADR-0041 (phase-14 §6.2 stat-namespace revision) — the established empirical-finding-drives-SPEC-revision-via-inline-ADR cadence.
- **Consequences:**
  - **Phase-15 PLAN is written to the option-(c) scope.** `docs/envoy-rust/phases/15-circuit-breakers/PLAN.md` + `PROGRESS.md` skeleton + Task 1 preamble land alongside this ADR at the state-2 PLAN-write commit. The SPEC is NOT edited (ratified; the corrections live in this ADR + the PROGRESS Task 1 preamble per the 06.2→14.2 cadence).
  - **Fixture renamed.** The SPEC's `0023-upstream-circuit-breaker-max-connections` becomes **`0023-upstream-circuit-breaker-max-pending-requests`** (the subject is the `max_pending_requests:0` reject path). ROADMAP row `15` summary updates accordingly at this commit.
  - **`upstream_cx_overflow` + `cx_open` are differentially asserted ONLY at inert-0** (fixtures 0020 + 0023 — both never open a connection or never reach the cap bilaterally). Their non-zero behavior is validated in-process only (the backstop's cap=1/K=2/default-pending path, where Envoy would serve `{200,200}` — bilaterally deferred). The BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT rows record this: name-required + value-exact-at-0 bilaterally; the non-zero cross-proxy value DIVERGES on the overflow path (Envoy queues, envoy-rust 503s) until the pending-queue phase lands — recorded as a known divergence.
  - **No phase split.** The re-scoped surface is ~12 tasks / ~1100–1250 LoC (production ~430, tests/backstop ~520, fixture/docs ~300) — UNDER the `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §6.1 ~1500-LoC / ~25-task gate. **ADR-0044 (the reserved split ADR) does NOT fire.**
  - **`DECISIONS.md` ledger head:** **ADR-0043** (this ADR). Next available **ADR-0044** (still reserved for a split that is now projected NOT to occur). Project-cumulative ADR count 44. The state-2 PLAN-write commit title carries `[ADR-0043]`.
  - **STATE.md advances at this commit** to: Active phase `15` state-2-complete / state-3-next (next-skill `superpowers:subagent-driven-development` scoped to the phase-15 PLAN); prior state-2-next pointer demoted to `_Historical_`; this ADR + the §6.2 findings documented in the new `### Phase-15 state-2 PLAN-write` Notes subsection.
- **Provenance:** §6.2 7-item empirical verification dispatched as a single foreground general-purpose subagent at the phase-15 state-2 PLAN-write prologue (mirrors the ADR-0041 provenance methodology). Methodology: Docker bridge networking with `envoyproxy/envoy:v1.33.0` + a Python slow-backend container + curl/python concurrent + sequential drivers + admin `/stats?filter={circuit_breakers,cx_overflow,upstream_cx,upstream_rq_pending}` scrapes + a `%RESPONSE_FLAGS%` stdout access log + `curl -i` / `wc -c` / `hexdump` body capture + a control run with `max_pending_requests` removed. The subagent verified all 7 items + returned a structured findings report; findings 1/2/3 surfaced material divergences (the SPEC's mental model), findings 4–7 matched. This ADR ratifies the revised scope the PLAN encodes. DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0042** (the phase-15 state-1 scoping ADR, this same predecessor commit `35236eec3`); ADR-0043 lands at the next-sequential number with no renumbering.
