## ADR-0044: Phase-16 minimum-viable HTTP-retry scope + the body-replay feasibility finding — retries are body-replay-safe with no new buffering machinery (H1 empty-body / H2 buffered-clone); deliver retry-on-status/connect-failure/reset + `num_retries` + 3-stat observability + `x-envoy-attempt-count`; defer per-try-timeout, custom back-off, retry budgets, host-predicates, header overrides, vhost-level policy, gRPC retry, and hedging

- **Date:** 2026-05-31.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Phase 16 (`16-http-retries`) is the **fifth concrete Upstream-robustness-family phase** (after parent-12 active HTTP health checking closed at `3ec7fb9`, parent-13 connection pooling closed at `96630f9`, parent-14 outlier detection closed at `b575bdc35`, and phase-15 circuit-breaker observability closed at `4dad7c4ae`). The `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §9 charter line pairs *"retries + hedging"*; the phase-15-close planning-boundary note (STATE.md at HEAD `4dad7c4ae`) listed "retries + hedging" among the standing Upstream-robustness posture options for the next family pick. The phase-16 state-1 brainstorm picked **retries** as the next phase: it is the natural family capstone (lowest architectural risk — a retry loop wraps the EXISTING H1/H2 dispatch seams with no new crate/transport/periodic-background primitive; highest foundation reuse — the 12.x health + 14.x ejection `pick_endpoint()` seam, the 13.x pools, the 14.2 `record_response` hook, the 04.2 config-schema patterns, the configurable-status synthetic backend), it has an excellent timing-robust differential (a single GET against a 503→200 stateful backend), and it UNBLOCKS the deferred `max_retries` circuit-breaker budget (a phase-15 deferral that depends on retries existing). A retry feature is only feasible, however, if a failed request can be **re-dispatched** — which requires the request (headers + body) to be replayable; in a streaming proxy this is the load-bearing constraint (Envoy buffers the request body up to a limit and refuses to retry beyond it). **The state-1 brainstorm verified envoy-rust's request-body handling directly via a read-only `Explore` subagent survey + controller confirmation and found retries are ALREADY body-replay-safe:** (i) H1 DRAINS the downstream request body into `/dev/null` (`crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:401-420`, 4 KiB at a time; chunked bodies rejected with a synth-501 at `:375-381`) and dispatches `body: Some(Bytes::new())` (`:488`) — a deliberate phase-04.3 empty-body scope choice, so every H1 attempt trivially re-sends the same headers + empty body; (ii) H2 BUFFERS the body into an in-memory `body_bytes` (`crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs:153-162`) and dispatches `body: envoy_req.body.clone()` (`:290`) — so every H2 attempt re-clones the already-buffered `Bytes`. **Consequence: phase 16 needs NO new request-body buffering machinery** — the existing H1 (empty-body) and H2 (buffered-clone) postures already make every retriable request replayable. This finding (analogous to phase-15's non-obvious "enforcement-already-landed-at-13" finding) is what makes a minimum-viable retry phase tractable as a single un-split (or thin-2-way-split) phase rather than a multi-phase body-buffering sub-project. This ADR settles the genuine scoping decision the finding creates: given replay is free, WHAT does phase 16 deliver, and where is the minimum-viable scope boundary?
- **Options considered:**
  - **(a) Open a NEW feature family instead of continuing Upstream-robustness** (HTTP/3+QUIC, gRPC, xDS, Observability, Runtime+hot-restart, WASM-host). Rejected as the next pick — those are large foundation lifts (new transport/codec/state-machine sub-projects, high architectural risk, large first-phase scope) better taken deliberately AFTER the in-flight Upstream-robustness family is wrapped. Retries is the family's natural next member with the lowest risk + highest reuse + an excellent timing-robust differential. (The new families remain seeded §9 headings for a later deliberate pick.)
  - **(b) Continue Upstream-robustness with circuit-breaker-budget expansion (`max_requests`/`max_retries`/`track_remaining`/multi-priority + the pending-request queue) or TCP-proxy connection pooling.** Rejected as the next pick — `max_retries` (the retry circuit breaker) DEPENDS on retries existing, so retries must come first; the pending-request queue is the more complex deferred phase-15 surface; TCP-proxy pooling (the `upstream_cx_total` carve-out) is a narrower opportunistic close better folded into whichever phase touches the TCP-proxy surface. Retries unblocks (b)'s `max_retries` and is the correct ordering.
  - **(c) Implement the FULL Envoy retry surface** (`per_try_timeout` + custom `retry_back_off` intervals + retry budgets + `retry_priority`/`retry_host_predicate` + `x-envoy-retry-on`/`x-envoy-max-retries` header overrides + vhost-level policy + `retriable_headers` + gRPC retry + hedging). Rejected — over-scoped; bundles a half-dozen independently-deferrable sub-features (the timeout machinery, the budget circuit breaker, the host-selection predicates, the header-override path, hedging's concurrent-attempt racing) that blow past the §6.1 split gate. The established minimum-viable-scope cadence (ADR-0036/0037/0038/0039/0040/0041/0042/0043 for phases 12/13/14/15) carves the thinnest coherent slice.
  - **(d) Deliver a minimum-viable retry policy: retry-on-status (`5xx`/`gateway-error`/`retriable-status-codes`) + connect-failure/reset + `num_retries` + the 3-stat observability subset + `x-envoy-attempt-count` + a default back-off + fixture 0024 (success + limit-exceeded); defer the rest.** Chosen. Phase 16 adds (1) the `RouteAction_Route.retry_policy: Option<RetryPolicy>` schema (`retry_on` comma-token list + `num_retries` default 1 + `retriable_status_codes`) + validator; (2) a retry loop wrapping the EXISTING H1 (`hcm.rs:465-706`) + H2 (`hcm.rs:238-481`) dispatch seams, re-picking a 12.x-health/14.x-ejection-aware endpoint each attempt and replaying the (free, per the §0 finding) request; (3) the 3-name minimum-viable stat subset `cluster.<name>.upstream_rq_retry{,_success,_limit_exceeded}` (the 14.1/15 "emit the names Envoy emits for the behavior you implement; allow-list the rest" precedent) PLUS the per-attempt `upstream_rq_total`/`upstream_rq_5xx` counting reconciliation (the §6.2-CRITICAL item); (4) the `x-envoy-attempt-count` downstream-response header; (5) a NEW STATEFUL fail-then-succeed synthetic-backend harness primitive (503 on attempt 1, 200 on attempt 2 — the discriminating observable); (6) fixture `0024-upstream-retry-on-5xx` (single timing-robust sequential driver covering BOTH the retried-to-200 success path AND the retry-limit-exceeded path). Projected single un-split phase (~1300–1600 LoC), with a thin H1-then-H2 split (16.1/16.2) held in reserve.
- **Decision:** adopt option (d), continuing the Upstream-robustness family with **HTTP retries**. Phase 16's minimum-viable scope boundary is: **deliver retry-on-(`5xx`/`gateway-error`/`connect-failure`/`reset`/`retriable-status-codes`) + `num_retries` + `retriable_status_codes` + the 3-stat observability subset + `x-envoy-attempt-count` + a default back-off + fixture 0024 (success + limit-exceeded).** DEFER: `per_try_timeout` (per-attempt timeout — the prime follow-up); custom `retry_back_off`/`rate_limited_retry_back_off` intervals; retry budgets (`retry_budget` + `upstream_rq_retry_overflow`); `retry_priority`/`retry_host_predicate`/`host_selection_retry_max_attempts` (retry-time host selection); `x-envoy-retry-on`/`x-envoy-max-retries`/`x-envoy-retry-grpc-on` request-header overrides; vhost-level `retry_policy`; `retriable_headers`/`retriable_request_headers` (header-match conditions); gRPC retry; and hedging (`hedge_policy`). Each deferred item is enumerated in the phase-16 SPEC §4.
- **Rationale:** option (d) continues the mission's differential-equivalence charter on the family it has been building for four phases (health-checks → pooling → outlier-detection → circuit-breakers → retries), at the lowest architectural risk (no new crate/transport/periodic-primitive — a loop around an existing seam) and the highest foundation reuse, with an excellent timing-robust differential (a single GET; the retry is internal — no concurrency, the §15 concurrent-driver flakiness vector is avoided entirely). Recording the body-replay feasibility finding HERE (vs only in the SPEC) honors D-3.4 (a future cold-start session must understand WHY phase 16 needs no body-buffering machinery — the empty-body H1 + buffered-clone H2 postures are non-obvious and load-bearing) + D-3.5 (decisions are written, not remembered). The minimum-viable boundary mirrors how prior phases carved their slices (phase-12's HTTP-only health checks; phase-14's consecutive-5xx/gateway-failure-only detectors; phase-15's `max_pending_requests:0`-only carve-out). The brainstorm-time ADR is consistent with phase-15's ADR-0042 cadence (an ADR at brainstorm when the scope rests on a non-obvious code finding); phases 12/13/14 brainstorms landed no ADR because their scope was charter-obvious — phase 16's tractability rests on the non-obvious replay finding, so it warrants the written record.
- **Consequences:**
  - **Phase-16 SPEC is written to the option-(d) scope.** `docs/envoy-rust/phases/16-http-retries/SPEC.md` lands alongside this ADR at the state-1 brainstorm commit, with §0 (the body-replay feasibility finding), §1 (goal + fixture 0024), §2.1 (the 3-stat observability subset + the per-attempt-counting reconciliation), §2.2 (`x-envoy-attempt-count`), §3 (D1–D9), and §4 (the deferral list). The ROADMAP gains row `16` (`status: planned`) beneath the "Upstream robustness family" heading at this commit (the FIFTH concrete row there).
  - **No code changes at this commit** — the brainstorm is docs-only (SPEC + ROADMAP row + this ADR + STATE advance). Implementation is the state-3 work of a LATER session (subagent-driven per `feedback_execution_style`).
  - **Conditional follow-on ADRs reserved** (phase-16 SPEC §7): **ADR-0045** (PLAUSIBLE — §6.2 empirical-verification revision, fires if the `retry_on` token semantics, the per-attempt vs per-request `upstream_rq_total`/`5xx` counting [the most likely trigger], or the limit-exceeded wire shape diverges from the projection; mirrors ADR-0037/0041/0043) lands at the state-2 PLAN-write if it fires; **ADR-0046** (POSSIBLE — phase split, fires if the state-2 LoC estimate exceeds ~1500 — genuinely close; seam `16.1` config+H1 / `16.2` H2+limit-exceeded+close) reserved.
  - **`DECISIONS.md` ledger head:** **ADR-0044** (this ADR). Next available **ADR-0045**. Project-cumulative ADR count 45. The state-1 brainstorm commit title carries `[ADR-0044]`.
  - **STATE.md advances at this commit** to: Active phase `16` state-1-complete / state-2-next (next-skill `superpowers:writing-plans` scoped to the phase-16 SPEC); the prior "awaiting next planning" status demoted to a `_Historical_` line; this ADR + the body-replay finding documented in the new `### Phase-16 state-1 brainstorm` Notes subsection.
  - **ADR-0028** (H1-listener × H2-cluster dispatch deferral) REMAINS OPEN — phase 16 does not engage it.
- **Provenance:** the body-replay feasibility finding was surfaced at the phase-16 state-1 brainstorm via a read-only `Explore` subagent survey of the retry-relevant code surface (the H1/H2 dispatch seams, the request-body handling, the `RouteAction` schema, the `upstream_rq_*` stat sites, the `record_response` hook, the differential `Driver` variants + configurable-status backend, and the `x-envoy-*` header machinery) + direct controller cross-check of the reported `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs` empty-body dispatch (`:488`) and `crates/envoy-http2/src/hcm.rs` buffered-clone dispatch (`:290`). The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0043** (the phase-15 §6.2 empirical-verification revision, landed at the phase-15 state-2 PLAN-write `c699ccfae`); ADR-0044 lands at the next-sequential number with no renumbering (the phase-15 state-4/5/6 commits landed no ADR — ADR-0044 was reserved for a phase-15 split that did not fire, and is now consumed by this phase-16 scoping ADR). Per D-3.5 this ADR lands inline at the state-1 brainstorm commit alongside the SPEC it scopes.

## ADR-0045: Phase-16 §6.2 empirical-verification reconciliation — unknown `retry_on` tokens are accept-and-ignore; `upstream_rq_total` counts per-ATTEMPT (retried-away codes live in the Envoy-only `retry.*` sub-scope); `x-envoy-attempt-count` is gated on `include_attempt_count_in_response` (not automatic)

- **Date:** 2026-05-31.
- **Status:** accepted. **Refines (does not supersede) ADR-0044** — ADR-0044's minimum-viable retry scope stands; this ADR reconciles three §2.x SPEC projections against empirical Envoy v1.33.0 behavior.
- **Context:** Phase 16 (`16-http-retries`) is at lifecycle state 2 (SPEC.md exists; PLAN.md being written). Per the phase-10/11/12/13/14/15-ratified verify-at-PLAN-write process + the phase-16 SPEC §6.2 (which flagged the empirical verification as HEAVY and reserved ADR-0045 as PLAUSIBLE, with finding 4 — per-attempt counting — called the most likely trigger), a foreground general-purpose subagent ran `envoyproxy/envoy:v1.33.0` under Docker against a route configuring `retry_policy: { retry_on: "5xx", num_retries: 1 }` + a stateful fail-once-then-succeed backend + an always-503 backend + a separate NO-retry control cluster + a `%RESPONSE_FLAGS%` stdout access log + admin `/stats`, driving single GETs and cross-checking every claim against the real backend's request count. The 9-item §6.2 checklist resolved as follows — **items 2/4(values)/6/7/8/9 MATCHED the SPEC projection; three items DIVERGED materially and are reconciled here:**
  - **(finding 1 ✦ — DIVERGES; resolves the SPEC §3 D2 open question) Unknown `retry_on` tokens are ACCEPT-AND-IGNORE, not rejected.** Empirically `retry_on: "5xx,bogus-token-xyz"` booted `state: LIVE` with no error/invalid/warning log line, and the valid sibling token (`5xx`) still drove retries. The SPEC left the unknown-token posture open (accept-ignore OR reject-with-ConfigError). **Envoy-faithful resolution: accept-and-ignore.** No `ConfigError` variant for unknown tokens.
  - **(finding 4 ✦ — DIVERGES; the §6.2-CRITICAL item) `upstream_rq_total` counts per upstream ATTEMPT; the retried-away attempt's response code lives in a separate `cluster.<name>.retry.*` sub-scope, NOT the main `upstream_rq_<class>`.** Empirically, one downstream request that made 2 attempts (503→200) → `cluster.<name>.upstream_rq_total: 2`; the NO-retry control cluster → `upstream_rq_total: 1`. The intermediate retried-away 503 surfaced under `cluster.<name>.retry.upstream_rq_503` / `retry.upstream_rq_5xx` (an Envoy-only sub-scope that appears only when a retry fires), while the MAIN `upstream_rq_5xx`/`upstream_rq_<code>` reflected the COMPLETING (downstream-returned) attempt's class only (limit-exceeded 503→503 → main `upstream_rq_5xx: 1` [the completing 503] + `retry.upstream_rq_5xx: 1` [the retried-away 503]; success 503→200 → main `upstream_rq_200: 1`, main `upstream_rq_5xx: 0`). The SPEC §2.1 projected that the MAIN `upstream_rq_5xx` ticks for the retried-away 503 "as well as any final 5xx" — empirically the retried-away code goes to the `retry.*` sub-scope, not the main counter. The HCM downstream per-class counters (`http.<prefix>.downstream_rq_{2,5}xx`) are strictly per-downstream-request (already correct in envoy-rust).
  - **(finding 5 ✦ — DIVERGES) `x-envoy-attempt-count` is NOT emitted merely because a `retry_policy` is configured — it is gated on the VirtualHost flag `include_attempt_count_in_response: true`.** Empirically the header was ABSENT on every response (success, limit-exceeded, no-retry) and was not injected request-side, with a standard `retry_policy` and no `include_attempt_count_in_response`. The SPEC §2.2 projected "emit on the downstream response when a `retry_policy` is configured" — empirically false.
  - **(findings 1-set / 2 / 3 / 6 / 7 / 8 / 9 — MATCHED)** `5xx` = 500-599, `gateway-error` = 502/503/504 only (a 500 did NOT retry under `gateway-error`); default `num_retries` = 1 (`google.protobuf.UInt32Value` wrapper, omitted ⇒ router default 1); retry stat VALUES success 1/1/0 + limit-exceeded 1/0/1 exactly as projected; default back-off exponential base 25 ms / max 250 ms (not differentially asserted); each attempt recorded against the host (`/clusters`: `rq_total::2`, `rq_error::2`) — per-attempt `record_response` confirmed; limit-exceeded final response = the last upstream 503 verbatim (status + body, NOT a synth), `%RESPONSE_FLAGS%` = `URX` (access-log-only, NOT a response header); fail-once-then-succeed backend saw exactly 2 attempts under `num_retries: 1`.
- **Options considered:**
  - **(finding-1 posture) accept-and-ignore vs reject-with-ConfigError.** Chose accept-and-ignore — it is what Envoy does (differential equivalence, D-3.3), and it is simpler (no new error variant; deny_unknown_fields still rejects the deferred STRUCT fields like `per_try_timeout`).
  - **(finding-4 reconciliation) (a) replicate Envoy's `retry.*` sub-scope faithfully; (b) move `upstream_rq_total` to per-attempt + keep `upstream_rq_5xx` on the completing response + allow-list the `retry.*` sub-scope; (c) leave envoy-rust per-request and allow-list the divergence.** Chose **(b)**. (a) over-scopes (the `retry.*` sub-scope is observability beyond the minimum-viable subset). (c) would make `upstream_rq_total` differ from Envoy on every retried request (Envoy 2, envoy-rust 1) — a real differential miss. (b) is the thinnest change that makes the asserted names (`upstream_rq_total`, `upstream_rq_5xx`) bilaterally exact while keeping the 23 existing fixtures byte-identical (a 1-attempt request counts identically under per-attempt and per-request). The Envoy-only `retry.*` + `upstream_rq_retry_overflow`/`_backoff_*`/`retry_or_shadow_abandoned`/`circuit_breakers.*.rq_retry_open` names go on fixture 0024's `allowlist_envoy_only`.
  - **(finding-5 reconciliation) (i) add `include_attempt_count_in_response` to the VirtualHost schema, emit `x-envoy-attempt-count` gated on it, set it true in fixture 0024; (ii) defer `x-envoy-attempt-count` entirely.** Chose **(i)**. It keeps `x-envoy-attempt-count` as the SPEC-scoped deliverable, is Envoy-faithful (the paired fixture configs use the real `include_attempt_count_in_response` field), and is small (one `bool` VirtualHost field + a gated header push reusing the `x-envoy-upstream-service-time` injection machinery). Request-side `x-envoy-attempt-count` (`include_request_attempt_count`) is DEFERRED (not needed for the fixture).
- **Decision:** reconcile envoy-rust to the empirical behavior: (1) `validate_retry_policy` / `RetryConfig::from` parse the `retry_on` comma list and silently drop unrecognized tokens (no ConfigError); (2) the H1 + H2 retry loops increment `cluster.<name>.upstream_rq_total` **per upstream attempt**, and `cluster.<name>.upstream_rq_5xx` **once on the completing (downstream-returned) response only** — both inert (== today) for 1-attempt requests; envoy-rust does NOT emit the Envoy-only `cluster.<name>.retry.*` sub-scope (allow-listed); (3) add `VirtualHost.include_attempt_count_in_response: bool` (default false) and emit `x-envoy-attempt-count: <total attempts>` on the downstream response only when it is true. Fixture 0024 sets `include_attempt_count_in_response: true` + `dns_lookup_family: V4_ONLY` (the macOS-Docker IPv4 carry-forward, finding/L11). All other §6.2 findings matched and need no change.
- **Rationale:** D-3.3 — differential correctness beats internal fidelity: the asserted stat names + the response header must match real Envoy on identical config/inputs. Option (b) for the counting model and option (i) for the header are the thinnest reconciliations that achieve bilateral equivalence on the names envoy-rust emits while preserving the 23-fixture regression baseline (the per-attempt change is provably inert for the no-retry path). Accept-and-ignore for unknown tokens mirrors Envoy and avoids a spurious config-rejection divergence. Recording these here (vs only in the PLAN) honors D-3.4/D-3.5 — a future session must understand WHY `upstream_rq_total` is per-attempt, WHY the retried-away 5xx is allow-listed rather than counted in the main scope, and WHY `x-envoy-attempt-count` needs the VirtualHost flag.
- **Consequences:**
  - **PLAN.md is written to this reconciliation.** `docs/envoy-rust/phases/16-http-retries/PLAN.md` lands alongside this ADR at the state-2 PLAN-write commit, with the §6.2 lock-ins L1–L11, the per-attempt-counting tasks (Tasks 3/4/5), the `include_attempt_count_in_response` schema + gated emit (Tasks 1/4/5), and fixture 0024's `allowlist_envoy_only` (the L10 Envoy-only names).
  - **Schema surface grows by one VirtualHost field** (`include_attempt_count_in_response: bool`, default false) beyond the SPEC §3 D1 projection. The deep-clone sites `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs` `clone_route_action` (`:240`) + `clone_route_config` (`:220`) must clone the new `retry_policy` + `include_attempt_count_in_response` fields (else they are silently dropped on the `Arc<RouteConfiguration>` clone).
  - **No phase split.** The §6.2-refined surface is ~1450–1650 LoC / ~13 implementation tasks — at but not over the `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §6.1 ~1500-LoC / ~25-task gate; the work is tightly coupled (the per-attempt reconciliation spans both protocols + one fixture + a shared `RetryConfig`). **ADR-0046 (the reserved split ADR) does NOT fire.**
  - **`DECISIONS.md` ledger head:** **ADR-0045** (this ADR). Next available **ADR-0046**. Project-cumulative ADR count 46. The state-2 PLAN-write commit title carries `[ADR-0045]`.
  - **STATE.md advances at this commit** to: Active phase `16` state-2-complete / state-3-next (Next expected skill → `superpowers:subagent-driven-development` per `feedback_execution_style`, dispatching the PLAN tasks SERIALLY per `feedback_serial_subagent_dispatch`, running `cargo clippy` PER TASK per `project_state3_arc_skips_clippy`).
  - **ADR-0028** (H1-listener × H2-cluster dispatch deferral) REMAINS OPEN — phase 16 does not engage it.
- **Provenance:** the §6.2 findings were produced by a foreground general-purpose subagent (the ADR-0037/0041/0043 methodology) running `envoyproxy/envoy:v1.33.0` (digest `sha256:56da5afd…`) under Docker Desktop, with every stat/header/flag claim cross-checked against the real backend's per-attempt request count. A methodology note worth carrying forward (L11): on macOS Docker, a `host.docker.internal` STRICT_DNS endpoint resolves to an unreachable IPv6 unless `dns_lookup_family: V4_ONLY` is set — otherwise the upstream is silently unreachable (`URX,UF`); all envoy-rust upstream fixtures already force IPv4 (phase-05.4 ADR-0024). The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0044** (the phase-16 state-1 scoping ADR, landed at the state-1 brainstorm `0fa80aba9`); ADR-0045 lands at the next-sequential number with no renumbering. Per D-3.5 this ADR lands inline at the state-2 PLAN-write commit alongside the PLAN it scopes.

## ADR-0046: Phase-17 minimum-viable circuit-breaker-budget scope + the zero-cap-always-trips deterministic-fixture finding + the cluster-scoped budget-ownership boundary — deliver `max_retries` + `max_requests` + `track_remaining` + `upstream_rq_retry_overflow` + the breaker-state/remaining gauges + fixture 0025; defer the pending queue, `retry_budget`, `max_connection_pools`, multi-priority, and concurrency-regime differential fixtures

- **Date:** 2026-06-01.
- **Status:** accepted.
- **Context:** Phase 17 (`17-circuit-breaker-budgets`) is the **sixth concrete Upstream-robustness-family phase** (after parent-12 active HTTP health checking closed at `3ec7fb9`, parent-13 connection pooling closed at `96630f9`, parent-14 outlier detection closed at `b575bdc35`, phase-15 circuit-breaker observability closed at `4dad7c4ae`, and phase-16 HTTP retries closed at `8dd1aa7f6`). It is the **promised owner phase** of deferred surface enumerated in BOTH prior phases' rollovers: the phase-15 rollovers deferred *"`max_requests`/`max_retries`/`track_remaining`/multi-priority"* to "the same budget phase", and the phase-16 rollovers deferred *"the `max_retries` circuit breaker (`circuit_breakers.thresholds[].max_retries` + `track_remaining`) — the circuit-breaker-budget expansion phase, which phase 16's retries now UNBLOCK"*. With phase 16 closed, the dependency that blocked the budget phase (retries must exist before a retry breaker can gate them) is discharged. A circuit-breaker *budget* is, however, inherently a **concurrency** feature (`max_retries`/`max_requests` cap retries/requests in flight at the same instant), and the phase-15 precedent (its SPEC's `Driver::Http1Concurrent`/`--hold-ms` concurrency machinery was DROPPED at the ADR-0043 re-scope as a timing-fragility vector) says concurrency-driven differential fixtures are not acceptable. The phase-17 state-1 brainstorm identified the deterministic alternative: **phase-15's §6.2 Docker verification (ADR-0043) empirically established that Envoy treats an explicitly-configured `0` threshold as an always-open breaker** (`max_pending_requests: 0` rejects the very FIRST request — fixture 0023 asserts this bilaterally today with one sequential GET); by the same resource-manager semantic, `max_retries: 0` and `max_requests: 0` are projected to be always-open breakers, so **a single sequential GET deterministically trips each budget breaker with zero concurrency** (verified empirically at the state-2 PLAN-write, §6.2 items 1/2). Two further seam findings make the phase surgical: the enforcement points already exist as single-site seams (the phase-16 retry-decision points H1 `crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:776-792` / H2 mirror; the dispatch-arm entries + the phase-15 `synth_overflow` 503 helpers), and real Envoy v1.33 ALREADY emits the full `circuit_breakers.{default,high}.{cx_open,cx_pool_open,rq_open,rq_pending_open,rq_retry_open}` gauge family + `upstream_rq_retry_overflow` regardless of config (recorded in fixture 0023/0024 expectation notes as Envoy-only unasserted names) — phase 17 moves exactly those names from "Envoy-only, unasserted" to value-exact bilateral assertions.
- **Options considered:**
  - **(a) Open a NEW feature family instead of continuing Upstream-robustness** (HTTP/3+QUIC, gRPC, xDS / dynamic config, Observability, Load balancing, Network filters, more HTTP filters, Runtime+hot-restart, WASM-host). Now legitimate (phase 16 was the family's capstone — every §9 Upstream-robustness charter member has a landed minimum-viable phase EXCEPT the budget surface), but rejected as the next pick: the budget surface is PROMISED deferred work named by two prior phases' rollovers as "the budget phase"; closing it first finishes the family cleanly at the lowest risk, after which a new family can be opened deliberately with a clean deferred-surface ledger. (The new families remain seeded §9 headings for the phase-18 pick.)
  - **(b) Continue Upstream-robustness with `per_try_timeout`** (the phase-16 "prime follow-up" deferral). Rejected — too thin for a standalone phase (~400–600 LoC: a per-attempt deadline + a timeout-classified retry condition), and its observable is inherently TIMING (a slow-backend-triggered timeout), conflicting with the BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT "Timing tolerances: not compared by default" posture — a per-try-timeout differential fixture would be exactly the timing-fragile shape the project avoids. Better folded into a future retry-hardening phase that has a non-timing primary observable.
  - **(c) Continue Upstream-robustness with pool hardening** (the phase-16 carryforward family: the H1-pool `Connection: close` re-pooling gap + the H2-pool send-failure no-invalidate asymmetry + a stateful H2 test backend). Rejected as the next pick — it is a correctness/hardening close with NO new Envoy feature surface (the §9 charter is feature-driven), and its differential observable requires a `Connection: close`-serving backend primitive that does not yet exist. It remains the named owner of the pool-liveness carryforwards (a future pool-hardening + pending-queue phase — see the deferral list below).
  - **(d) Deliver the FULL circuit-breaker surface** (budgets + the `max_pending_requests > 0` pending QUEUE + `retry_budget` percent-based budgets + `max_connection_pools` + multi-priority HIGH thresholds + concurrency-regime differential fixtures). Rejected — over-scoped: the pending queue alone is an async wait-queue inside the pools (FIFO fairness + timeout interaction — a fundamentally different architectural lift), and concurrency-regime differential fixtures re-introduce the timing-fragility vector phase 15 dropped. Blows the §6.1 gate.
  - **(e) Deliver a minimum-viable budget phase: `max_retries` + `max_requests` + `track_remaining` schema; cluster-scoped budget primitives (RAII acquire/release); the retry-budget gate at the phase-16 retry-decision seams; the request-budget gate at the dispatch entries (reusing the phase-15 overflow synth-503); `upstream_rq_retry_overflow` + `circuit_breakers.default.{rq_retry_open,rq_open}` + `remaining_{retries,rq}` observability; fixture 0025 anchored on the deterministic zero-cap regime; the >0-cap concurrency regime covered in-process only.** Chosen.
- **Decision:** adopt option (e), continuing the Upstream-robustness family with **circuit-breaker budgets**. Phase 17's minimum-viable scope boundary is: **deliver `max_retries` (the retry breaker gating the phase-16 retry loop; overflow → `upstream_rq_retry_overflow` ticks and the would-be-retried response surfaces downstream verbatim) + `max_requests` (the request breaker gating dispatch entry; overflow → the phase-15 81-byte 503 overflow local reply) + `track_remaining` (→ `remaining_retries`/`remaining_rq` gauges) + the `circuit_breakers.default.{rq_retry_open,rq_open}` breaker-state gauges + fixture `0025-upstream-circuit-breaker-retry-budget` (three sequential probes: budget-blocked retry / budget-allowed control / request-breaker overflow).** The budget state + new stat handles live in **`envoy-cluster::Cluster`** (NOT the per-protocol pools): `max_retries`/`max_requests` are cluster-wide concepts spanning both protocol pools, the H1/H2 gate sites already hold a `&ClusterHandle`, and the phase-15 pool-owned stats (`cx_*`, pending) are connection-lifecycle concepts that genuinely belong to pools — the resulting two-site registration seam for the `circuit_breakers.default.*` namespace is documented in BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT. DEFER: the `max_pending_requests > 0` pending QUEUE (owner: a future pool-hardening + pending-queue phase, which also owns the phase-16 pool-liveness carryforwards); `retry_budget` (`budget_percent` + `min_retry_concurrency`); `max_connection_pools` + `cx_pool_open` + `remaining_cx_pools`; multi-priority (HIGH) thresholds + `circuit_breakers.high.*`; concurrency-regime DIFFERENTIAL fixtures (>0 caps tripped by concurrent load — in-process backstop only); `per_try_timeout` (still deferred from phase 16). Each deferred item is enumerated in the phase-17 SPEC §4.
- **Rationale:** option (e) closes the family's promised deferred surface at the lowest architectural risk (no new crate/transport/periodic primitive — two RAII atomics in `envoy-cluster` + two ~10-line gate insertions at existing seams) and the highest foundation reuse (the phase-16 retry loop + stateful backend + `Driver::Http1KeepAlive`; the phase-15 conditional-stat-registration discipline + overflow synth-503 + fixture-0023 template), with a deterministic timing-robust differential made possible by the zero-cap finding. Recording the zero-cap-always-trips finding + the cluster-scoped ownership boundary HERE (vs only in the SPEC) honors D-3.4 (a future cold-start session must understand WHY no concurrency harness is needed and WHY the budget stats live in the cluster rather than the pools — both non-obvious and load-bearing) + D-3.5 (decisions are written, not remembered). The minimum-viable boundary mirrors how prior phases carved their slices (phase-15's `max_pending_requests:0`-only carve-out; phase-16's 3-stat retry subset). The brainstorm-time ADR is consistent with the ADR-0042/0044 cadence (an ADR at brainstorm when the scope rests on a non-obvious finding); the zero-cap projection + the ownership boundary warrant the written record.
- **Consequences:**
  - **Phase-17 SPEC is written to the option-(e) scope.** `docs/envoy-rust/phases/17-circuit-breaker-budgets/SPEC.md` lands alongside this ADR at the state-1 brainstorm commit, with §0 (the zero-cap-always-trips finding + the seam findings), §1 (goal + fixture 0025 three-probe topology), §2.1 (the stat subset), §2.2 (the two wire shapes), §3 (D1–D8), §4 (the deferral list), §5.4 (the cluster-scoped ownership boundary), and §6.2 (the 10-item empirical-verification checklist). The ROADMAP gains row `17` (`status: planned`) beneath the "Upstream robustness family" heading at this commit (the SIXTH concrete row there).
  - **No code changes at this commit** — the brainstorm is docs-only (SPEC + ROADMAP row + this ADR + STATE advance). Implementation is the state-3 work of a LATER session (subagent-driven per `feedback_execution_style`).
  - **After phase 17 lands, the Upstream-robustness family is complete in minimum-viable form** — every `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md` §9 charter member (active health checks; outlier detection; circuit breakers; retries; per-protocol connection pooling) has a landed minimum-viable phase. The phase-18 brainstorm can deliberately open a new §9 family (the family's REMAINING deferred surface — the pending queue, pool hardening, hedging, per_try_timeout, TCP/gRPC health checks — stays on the deferred-surface ledger for opportunistic or dedicated future phases).
  - **Conditional follow-on ADRs reserved** (phase-17 SPEC §7): **ADR-0047** (PLAUSIBLE — §6.2 empirical-verification revision; fires if the zero-cap semantics [the most consequential], the `max_requests: 0` wire shape, or the max_requests-overflow counter identity [the most likely trigger] diverges from the projection; mirrors ADR-0037/0041/0043/0045) lands at the state-2 PLAN-write if it fires; **ADR-0048** (POSSIBLE — phase split; fires if the state-2 LoC estimate exceeds ~1500; seam `17.1` schema+cluster-primitives+retry-breaker / `17.2` request-breaker+gauges+close) reserved.
  - **`DECISIONS.md` ledger head:** **ADR-0046** (this ADR). Next available **ADR-0047**. Project-cumulative ADR count 47. The state-1 brainstorm commit title carries `[ADR-0046]`.
  - **STATE.md advances at this commit** to: Active phase `17` state-1-complete / state-2-next (next-skill `superpowers:writing-plans` scoped to the phase-17 SPEC); the prior "awaiting next planning" status demoted to a `_Historical_` line; this ADR + the zero-cap finding documented in the new `### Phase-17 state-1 brainstorm` Notes subsection.
  - **ADR-0028** (H1-listener × H2-cluster dispatch deferral) REMAINS OPEN — phase 17 does not engage it.
- **Provenance:** the zero-cap-always-trips projection rests on the phase-15 §6.2 empirical finding ratified in ADR-0043 (`max_pending_requests: 0` rejects ALL requests — verified against `envoyproxy/envoy:v1.33.0` under Docker at the phase-15 PLAN-write) and on Envoy's documented circuit-breaker semantics; it is itself re-verified empirically for `max_retries`/`max_requests` at the phase-17 state-2 PLAN-write (§6.2 items 1/2) BEFORE the PLAN locks it. The seam findings (the retry-decision points, the dispatch entries, the Envoy-side gauge family already present in fixture 0023/0024 notes) were surfaced at the phase-17 state-1 brainstorm via a read-only `Explore` subagent survey of the budget-relevant code surface (the `Thresholds` schema + `validate_circuit_breakers`; the H1/H2 retry loops + `AttemptResult`/`run_attempt`; the pools' acquire/overflow paths + conditional stat registration; the `Cluster` stat fields + `from_bootstrap` registration; the gauge primitives; the fixture 0023/0024 expectation shapes) + direct controller cross-check of the fixture expectation notes and the fuzz-corpus seed count (28). The DECISIONS.md ledger head before this commit is **ADR-0045** (the phase-16 §6.2 reconciliation, landed at the phase-16 state-2 PLAN-write); ADR-0046 lands at the next-sequential number with no renumbering (ADR-0046 was reserved for a phase-16 split that did not fire, and is now consumed by this phase-17 scoping ADR). Per D-3.5 this ADR lands inline at the state-1 brainstorm commit alongside the SPEC it scopes.
